Axiom Constellation

Chapter 1 - The Arrival

SumireV was running a test on her latest invention, the Vulcan-E2. For months, it had kept failing to reach 100% potential and remained capped at 65%. The memory of her near-death experience during her encounter with a Celestial still haunted her. She was afraid of dying and knew that she was not immortal, yet she had a bold idea to end the threat. She looked back at her Core of Absolute, which was still recharging after prolonged use; the indicator showed it was only at 44%.

Suddenly, her mentor, Dr. Arc, appeared behind her. He was a hologram, having died a long time ago and managed to preserve his consciousness in the Archived Hall. Dr. Arc told SumireV that a war was raging near a nearby star.

SumireV sighed. "We aren't in good shape for another battle. Without my Core, we can't do much except rely on our technology."

Dr. Arc replied, "SumireV, it doesn't mean we have to fight them. Maybe we could just teleport our stronghold to a safer solar system."

SumireV perked up. "Good idea. Tell all our people to prepare for Lightspeed Travel in three days."

That night, SumireV couldn't fall asleep because the distant war could still be heard. She knew something needed to be done—she could feel that something was about to happen. She got up and notified the entire crew to stay on high alert. SumireV was always prepared, as she loved having safety nets and backup plans. She stepped into her armor and grabbed a few pieces of gear.

In the morning, alarms went off as multiple unknown objects penetrated the perimeter. SumireV's defense missiles activated, destroying the objects—which turned out to be fragments of a destroyed planet. SumireV ran to her main lab to check on her Core of Absolute. It was almost at 100%, and she had to pause work on her Vulcan-E2, leaving it stored in the PROTOTYPE Lab. She knew a massive war was underway, and it turned out a faction called the "Bureau Coalition" was at war with galactic invaders known as The Hunt from Galaxy-4 (Sector-B12 in SumireV's mapping). SumireV's hands trembled; she knew this wasn't her war, yet the rate at which the invaders kept expanding was alarming.

SumireV readied her entire armada to rescue any survivors on the affected planets while the Stronghold completed its final steps before the next day's teleport. SumireV brought her forces not to confront the enemy, but to rescue people—though she and a few other specialists would hold the frontline in case they engaged hostiles.

Chapter 2 - Indomitable Resistance

SumireV saw several destroyed planets, all strangely silent—no distress calls, no signs of battle. Suddenly, out of nowhere, SumireV's fleet was pinned down by multiple lasers and rockets. SumireV had to use her Core of Absolute to create a temporary energy barrier. When the enemy paused to reload, SumireV counterattacked using blinding light from her support ships, followed by a plasma-laser barrage and several space fighters.

The fight grew intense. SumireV had to descend to a planet, where she met several Bureau Coalition forces. Captain Hein asked SumireV to identify herself.

SumireV shouted, "SumireV from Interstellar Chronicle! We're here to assist you!"

They couldn't talk for long due to the sheer amount of firepower coming from the enemy. SumireV used her Vulcan-E1 tech to create a massive AoE of burning-hot smoke to blind the enemy. She then used her Core to manifest artillery and fired into the enemy line, following up with airstrikes. This bought them temporary cover to retreat and evacuate everyone while SumireV held the line.

The enemy warlord, Pegasus, arrived and provoked her. "Oh, what do we have here? A so-called legend? A genius? What's wrong, SumireV? Don't you want to use that little black hole of yours?"

SumireV gritted her teeth. She knew it was a trap to goad her into doing something reckless that would kill innocent people. Gripping her hammer, she shot back, "You want a black hole? Let me show you!"

SumireV charged with Phaser 1 activated alongside her space fighters, which kept providing covering fire. Pegasus used his Storm ability to conjure a thunderstorm over SumireV, but she evaded it using her suit's supersonic speed. SumireV ascended into the sky and created a fleet of warships—consisting of battleships—that rained a barrage of shells down on Pegasus and his army.

Pegasus continued mocking her. "GAH! Is that all you've got? Borrowing power that doesn't belong to you—pathetic!"

SumireV smirked. "Does it look like that's going to stop me from killing you? No, right? Time to end this for good."

SumireV kept overwhelming Pegasus with a barrage of rockets and shells while Pegasus steadily ran low on energy and stamina. SumireV readied herself to activate her Primordial Black Hole with Prometheus Modulation to contain its effect locally. Suddenly, Pegasus was teleported away by his supervisor, and the entire enemy force teleported out, retreating and pulling all their troops from Bureau Coalition territory.

With a serious expression, SumireV ordered her flagship, "Launch the Divine Bullet." The rocket traveled at hypersonic speed and reached The Hunt fleet in just four hours.

SumireV and several Bureau Coalition survivors returned to her temporary mothership for medical treatment. However, some of them disliked SumireV because of her past sins. A little boy shouted at her, "WE KNOW WHAT YOU DID! REMEMBER PLANET VL-5?! YOU DESTROYED IT DESPITE KNOWING THERE WERE 5 TRILLION PEOPLE STILL NOT EVACUATED!"

SumireV looked away, feeling guilty, though deep down she knew she had had no choice. As SumireV tended to the injured using her Core of Absolute, a soldier asked her, "SumireV, are you on the good side or the bad side?"

SumireV replied, "I suppose somewhere in between, though I lean toward the good side."

The soldier asked, "Why do you always destroy planets even when you know there are still people on them?"

SumireV's face fell slightly. "Well, sometimes you don't have any other choice. You have to make sacrifices for the greater good—the death of a few so others can live. Still, I wish I didn't have to. I want to save everyone."

Just then, SumireV received a report from the control room: "The Divine Bullet hit. Confirmed target down."

SumireV went to the control room to check for updates. "Status of our Stronghold? Status of casualties on our side and the Bureau Coalition's? Status of damages?"

Operator 1 reported, "Our Stronghold has moved to the Pantera Region and is safe."

Operator 2 added, "Casualties in Interstellar Chronicle: 5,091. Bureau Coalition: 44,000,000,000,000, due to the planets destroyed by The Hunt."

Operator 3 finished, "Total damage exceeds two suns combined and would normally take 100 million years to repair. However, with our tech and your power, it will be shortened to five decades."

"Alright," SumireV said. "It's time for the recovery process. I will meet with the Bureau Coalition leader to discuss terms."

The Bureau Coalition, on the other hand, knew SumireV had helped them in exchange for something that would benefit her faction, and they hoped to take advantage of her technology. A few hours later, SumireV arrived at the council with a stoic, unreadable expression.

"I'll get straight to the point. Interstellar Chronicle helped the Bureau Coalition by repelling The Hunt's forces, and we just assisted you with the recovery process. I have two options for you all. One: once all of your people have recovered, I ask for 5 MT of Compound Meteorite and your finest herbs, and we will leave. Two: Interstellar Chronicle will give the Bureau Coalition protection against any threats for at least 250 years, and we will boost your post-war recovery as fast as possible using our technology. I'm giving you all 30 minutes."

One of the council members protested, "But that is unfair—we lost several planets!"

SumireV replied, "Do I have to repeat myself? If I were you, I'd choose the second option to future-proof yourselves. Besides, we aren't dictators; as the leader of Interstellar Chronicle, I am clearly showing you my full trust."

After 30 minutes, the Bureau Coalition agreed to the second option. SumireV smirked in approval. "That's more like it. Wise choice," she said before leaving the room.

Chapter 3 - Under Watch

Somewhere inside the headquarters of the Government Authority of the Universe, a petty officer reported, "Sir, we've detected an abnormality in Galaxy-6. Our intel says The Hunt has become an invasive species there, but the energy spike isn't on the same scale as normal tech—it's almost as strong as an Archon."

Admiral Blackbear's face turned grim. Clenching his fist tightly, he said, "SumireV... It's her. A force to be reckoned with. She's the one who wiped out six of our divisions sent to raid her stronghold seven months ago. Deploy the First Battle Unit to steal SumireV's tech. We cannot fight her head-on—send two Archons as well."

SumireV returned to her floating fortress, a temporary but well-equipped space station armed with heavy weaponry. She resumed her research on black holes, trying to figure out how to cast one at maximum damage with the lowest possible backlash. Knowing the recent battle had exposed her location to rival forces, SumireV rubbed her forehead and sighed.

"Son of a... ugh. I guess I have to prepare myself for an expected encounter. I could run, but I care about my people—they don't have powers like I do."

As SumireV held her Core of Absolute, the space station was destroyed within the blink of an eye. SumireV quickly activated her battle suit. "Gaahhh!! What the hell?!"

She spotted a Herald with the power of Sound.

"I detected a weird frequency," the Herald said. "I thought I had run into something special, but it turns out it's just you. A legend."

"You want me to kill you just like I killed the other Herald?" SumireV shot back.

The Herald immediately unleashed a Sonic Boom to rupture SumireV's organs, but she evaded it at hypersonic speed.

"This is why I hate smart people," the Herald muttered.

SumireV used her Core to conjure an armada out of thin air and fired a barrage of hundreds of missiles and rockets.

"Tsk! Mere human toys won't do any DAMAGE to ME!" The Herald retaliated with sound waves, destroying several of SumireV's ships.

SumireV smirked. "It hurts, doesn't it? I can clearly see the scratch on your arm. How does it feel to be a Herald and still get hurt by human tech from a 1945 World War II battleship?"

SumireV blinded the Herald with smoke from the explosions, then suddenly called out, "Prometheus Modulation, Grade 1!" She cast a black hole large enough to swallow the Herald.

The Herald managed to resist it with a sound barrier. "You think I don't know your cheap tricks, SUMIREV? I know better!"

"We'll see... Surprise!" SumireV created a tank with amplified gravity, sending it ramming into the Herald at 50,000 km/h straight into the black hole, killing the Herald instantly.

SumireV floated in space, letting her body relax—she hadn't rested properly since the war in the Bureau Coalition. Using her Core's dimension ability, she rested inside for a bit before teleporting to the Pantera Region, returning to her quarters to sleep for days.

Meanwhile, in Sector-14, SumireV's friend MiRi—an engineer of Interstellar Chronicle—discovered a strange liquid orb that had fallen from another universe. MiRi and her troops seized the area before any other major faction could arrive, though they quickly came under attack from The Empire along their perimeter.

Chapter 4 - The Expected

MiRi began extracting the Orb as quickly as possible while Nagasawa and Ampasy held back the enemy advance. Ampasy fought using his Strength Percentile combination of Phaser VII Tech and Serum-ESD, tanking The Empire's fleet while Nagasawa guided their own fleet into a defensive formation.

"What the fu*k is this?! The Empire is sending their strongest unit!" Ampasy yelled, his body shaking with exhaustion.

MiRi contacted them over the comm line in a rushed tone. "The extraction is almost complete—ten more minutes before I activate Lightspeed Travel!"

With a trembling hand, Nagasawa activated the fleet's last energy core to fire a laser barrage at the enemy. "This is the last of our energy! After this, we won't have any heavy firepower left to hold them! Repeat, this is the last energy left!"

The fleet fired the laser, but The Empire deployed energy shields that reflected it back, wiping out 70% of Nagasawa's ships. Enraged, Ampasy activated his Phaser-5. "They shall face doom!" Screaming, he charged and broke through every layer of enemy defense, his body pushed into overdrive.

MiRi contacted them again. "Extraction done! Let's evacuate now—we can't stay any longer because The Empire is sending reinforcements, and an Orion fleet is closing in from the west. Five minutes!"

Nagasawa and Ampasy boarded the warship and engaged Lightspeed Travel straight into the portal MiRi had opened. The Empire reached the planet only to find nothing—and soon after, they were ambushed by the Orion fleet.

MiRi, Nagasawa, and Ampasy returned to Planet Pantera-55 in the Pantera Region, where they reported their findings to SumireV. Sitting in a lobby chair with her legs crossed, SumireV asked, "How did it go? I heard about what you found. Now I need to know about The Empire—what did they send at you?"

Ampasy sat across from her confidently. "They sent basic forces first as a trick before pouring their strongest units on us. We didn't spot Brian, though—maybe he wasn't part of that assault."

Nagasawa added, "From what I saw, they only sent Grade-S forces and tried to overwhelm us with numbers."

SumireV's expression stayed unreadable and silent before she said, "Go on."

Nagasawa swallowed nervously. "We... we lost 70% of our fleet. Twenty percent was completely destroyed, 10% heavily damaged, 15% took minor damage, and 25% suffered unrepairable damage."

"Hmm... that's okay," SumireV said. "At least we got this alien artifact. All of you may go get some rest now."

Everyone left the room while SumireV went to inspect the Alien Orb.

Chapter 5 - Silent Stars

SumireV walked through the space station orbiting directly above Pantera-55. She silently calculated every possibility following the recent battles; for now, her priority was rebuilding morale and recovering before focusing on technological upgrades.

SumireV visited Divine Bullet Lab-1 (DBL-1) to inspect the GS-1 Rocket alongside the head researcher of the Divine Bullet Project, Doctor Zack. In a slightly tired tone, she asked, "How's the project coming along? We need to bring our rocket capacity up to 5/5."

Zack answered with a stoic expression, "Professor, we're doing our best. In just 12 hours, we've already completed the rocket body. As for the warhead, it's still charging energy from a nearby sun, though we already installed a backup warhead using two thermonuclear reactors rigged with an overload system once target-locked."

SumireV smiled and patted the doctor's shoulder. "Did I tell you to put thermonuclear reactors in there?"

Zack began sweating nervously, knowing his odds of surviving her wrath felt 50/50. "Um... we are sorry, SumireV. I truly am sorry." He trembled slightly.

SumireV smirked. "Relax, I'm not going to kill you. Actually, I like it when you make backup plans—and using thermonuclear tech is smart, way better than our old blueprint with atomic bombs. Keep up the good work." She opened a doorway-sized teleport gate so the researchers could go take a break. "Don't forget to relax, fellas." SumireV walked down the hallway, swaying her hips and waving goodbye.

She headed down to Pantera-55 to check on her friends and found Ampasy and Nagasawa eating in the lounge. Crossing her arms, SumireV asked, "How are you guys holding up?"

Nagasawa stood up to shake her hand, and Ampasy followed suit. Nagasawa smiled. "Morning, Sumi! We're doing fine, though I keep wondering when our Mecha Battle Suits will be ready. It'll feel like becoming a Transformer!"

SumireV smirked reassuringly. "Relax, sis. It's still a PROTOTYPE. Once it's finished, we'll all get to use it."

Nagasawa nodded in acknowledgment, and SumireV turned to Ampasy. "What about you? What do you think of your Phaser 5 now that I upgraded your cyber system?"

"I love it—way better than Phaser 4," Ampasy said boldly and excitedly. "At least I'm not straining myself in Phaser 5, unlike you."

SumireV crossed her arms. "Wow, bold words, but alright—I'll let it slide this time."

They quickly sat down and played a few rounds of poker. A few hours later, SumireV visited the Vulcan-E2 Lab and told the researchers to resume the work postponed during the battle against The Hunt. "I'll leave the research in your hands," she said stoically. "Maybe I'll help out when I have some free time." Checking her stats and seeing everything in the green, she decided to head to the training grounds to practice her Vulcan-E1 elemental combinations.

SumireV entered the simulator room, unlocking full access to all of her abilities. Five target dummies matched to the skill level of Government Authority of the Universe professional units approached her. SumireV released water to levitate them, then channeled high-voltage lightning through the water, electrocuting all five targets. Next, 20 enemy convoys rolled in. SumireV fused earth and fire to create a flood of magma, enhancing it with the Core of Absolute to unleash a magma tsunami.

Switching the simulation to a speed run, she activated Phaser 4 and focused Vulcan-E1 Lightning into her feet—pushing past lightning speed (>330,000 km/h). Because she was capped at 65%, her peak straight-line speed reached roughly 214,000 km/h, with average bursts around 145,000 km/h: fast enough for mid-tier enemies, though not enough for an Archon. Finally, she used her Core of Absolute to conjure a Global Abomination—a pre-Type-I Human Civilization satellite from 2050 carrying 12 tungsten-alloy rods. With no explosives, it relied purely on kinetic energy and gravity.

"How exciting... I really like this tech," SumireV murmured. "I should study it more and make some improvements—maybe add an unstable nuclear core or overloaded energy warhead, heh." She continued her simulator training.

A few days later, a handful of Government fleets approached, only to be wiped out by SumireV's defenses. Sitting in the Control Room, she observed all enemy movements crossing her Interstellar Radar; the moment any ship entered the defensive perimeter, it was obliterated. She turned to watch Pantera-55 below, where drones were repairing several damaged ships, the room humming with machinery and rapid typing.

SumireV spoke to Dr. Arcturus's hologram. "How is Project Vulcan-E2 progressing?"

Dr. Arc replied, "The project isn't complete yet; however, we've already found a way to calibrate it so you can combine more than four elements instead of just one. That's solid progress, though effectiveness and damage output are still capped at 65%."

SumireV nodded in approval. "I suppose I can rest for an entire week then. Hostile factions keep attacking us, but don't worry—our defense AI is strong enough to hold them off. Still, it'll take about two weeks before their stronger units arrive, meaning I'll have to deploy our forces eventually." She paused for a moment before adding, "Have some intermediate scientists report to me. I want us to modernize and modify this Global Abomination technology. Don't worry about materials—we have plenty coming in from the Bureau Coalition."

Chapter 6 - Wound of the Past

SumireV tossed in her sleep, drenched in sweat, reliving a nightmare of the first invasion by otherworldly invaders.

In the dream, SumireV was working in her lab when the world suddenly went dead silent. Emergency warnings flooded every screen: the planet was being invaded. SumireV ran to her battle suit and looked up to see an interstellar war raging in the sky. Dr. Arc mobilized their units to teleport to a safer sector of space. Below, the aliens disintegrated anyone they touched. SumireV cut them down using her World Breaker Sword—a broadsword forged with an obsidian edge.

"If they even graze you, your body disintegrates within minutes!" SumireV shouted as she went to check on her colleagues—only to find they had all been slaughtered. In the sky, meteorites rained down from neighboring destroyed planets. She watched stars shatter inside a massive black hole, feeling as though she could hear light itself screaming in agony. Streaks of radiation tore across the heavens like northern lights from a distant nebula explosion.

SumireV tried to save everyone she saw. Less than an hour later, she witnessed two titanic beings clashing before crashing down to the earth: a Herald and a Liminal.

"I shall not let you destroy humanity! We will fight to the end without kneeling!" the Herald roared, using a temporal black hole to absorb the Liminal's gamma attack. SumireV saw the Herald was bleeding badly, his intestines spilling out—he had clearly been fighting for hours. SumireV fired her laser destroyer to help.

"What are you doing?! Get out of here! He isn't your fight!" the Herald yelled.

The Liminal conjured a radiation trident and thrust it straight through the Herald's shield, impaling his chest. Weakened, the Herald collapsed to the ground. As the Liminal moved in to finish him off, SumireV rammed a tank directly into the Liminal. Her battle suit was severely damaged by radiation.

"I won't let you kill him! He's our hero!" she cried.

The Liminal casually fired a burst of radiation light, gravely injuring SumireV. Before the Liminal could finish her, Dr. Arc arrived to draw its attention.

The Liminal stood tall and sneered. "Universe-26, are you all really naive enough to think you can stop our conquest? Never. We know you halted the previous war, but never again. This is your demise, Universe-26!"

The Liminal rushed Dr. Arc in hand-to-hand combat, manifesting radiation weapons out of thin air while Dr. Arc spammed missiles and machine-gun fire, blocking strikes with his energy shield.

SumireV crawled over to the dying Herald. "I'm sorry I couldn't save humanity... I always hated Heralds, but I was wrong—not all Heralds are evil. Forgive me."

The Herald only had a few minutes left. "I understand... I used to be human, just like you. I wanted to save humanity, and I became a Herald through corruption and emotional instability. I'm dying—I can't be saved."

SumireV could hear distant cries for help—people burning, mutilated, suffering—forced to watch it all raw and helpless. "If only I were strong enough to save all of you," she whispered to herself. "If only I had a chance... I wanted to be the Protector, and I failed."

Beside them, Dr. Arc was losing; his mecha had lost an arm to the Liminal's Tetra Blast.

The Herald realized his core hadn't been destroyed—merely scratched by the Liminal's trident. Looking at SumireV, he managed a faint smile. "I've been watching you ever since we met last month. I grew fond of you... your determination and eagerness to improve remind me of my youth. My IQ wasn't high enough to invent newer technology, but you're different, SumireV. You're something else—something the Liminal don't have, something everyone fears... even me. You have immense potential."

The Herald plunged his own hand into his chest and pulled out the Core of Absolute. SumireV's eyes widened as she realized what he was about to do. "What are you doing?"

The Herald cupped her face. "I only have a few seconds left. Tell me—are you willing to fight for humanity at any cost? Are you willing to be the Protector? Wait... I already see the answer in your eyes. Perhaps... you truly are the Ingenuity of Humanity."

The Herald thrust the Core directly into SumireV's chest. "Stop this invasion for good. Humanity shall not go extinct. I'll always be right in here. You... are... the... Chosen... One..." With his final words, the Herald dissolved into dust.

SumireV screamed in agony as the Core embedded itself inside her. Memories of hundreds of previous wielders from different eras flooded her mind and soul—some corrupted, some noble—all at once. Surrounding energy surged violently into her body, disorienting her. Inside her lab, the energy calculator exploded from the sudden spike as the Core forcefully and unstably bound itself to its new host.

Corruption overtook her in an instant. SumireV shot forward at hypersonic speed, driving the trident into the Liminal's head and sending it crashing into a nearby mountain. She pressed the assault relentlessly, even conjuring nuclear bombs out of thin air alongside thousands of laser cannons firing down at once.

The Liminal reeled back. "What the fu*k is this power?!" Attempting to calculate her energy output, its sensors returned only Error. SumireV spammed Primordial Black Holes, tore open the fabric of reality, and shredded the entire Liminal armada in seconds at the speed of light. She tried crushing the Liminal under the combined gravity of 50,000 planets.

Enraged, her eyes glowing amber, she spoke: "Have you ever seen stars shattered?"

Right there above the city, she summoned an interstellar black hole—TON 618—which began expanding uncontrollably. Watching from afar, Dr. Arc frantically transferred his consciousness into a distant space station just out of reach of her attack. SumireV obliterated Earth-A0D4 along with the entire galactic sector and the Liminal forces. Instability across Universe-26 spiked as thousands of stars collapsed. SumireV slowly blacked out, watching light itself get devoured as the event horizon burned bright orange. Everyone she loved—and Dr. Arc—died alongside 5 septillion lives across that galaxy. She had unleashed TON 618 that day.

SumireV jolted awake in her bed, realizing it had just been another nightmare from her past. She ran straight to the control room and asked Dr. Arc if anything had happened while she slept.

"Hmm, nothing abnormal occurred," Dr. Arc replied. "Neither our radar nor our detectors show any anomalies."

SumireV sank onto a couch and rubbed her forehead. "Must be fragments of my past... I'm sorry, everyone." Guilt washed over her again at the memory of wiping out all living things in that galaxy forever. Quietly tearing up, she retreated to her comfort zone: her luxury bathroom.

Dr. Arc sighed and looked away. "There's something still unfinished in that galaxy... something you haven't settled yet. Maybe returning there will solve it." Looking down at his holographic hands, he added quietly, "I was killed by your black hole, SumireV. Good thing I managed to inject a few stabilizing doses into you before the end—otherwise you'd be wandering space as a mindless Herald right now. Still, I'm pretty sharp—installing my consciousness into that space station worked, and once you stabilized, you built my holograms in bases across the universe. Humans shouldn't wield cosmic powers, yet I suppose you really are the Chosen One."

Unbeknownst to either of them, Nagasawa had been standing outside, listening to their entire conversation.

Chapter 7 - Emerging Concern

Going through her daily routine, SumireV noticed her Core of Absolute's energy output was lower than usual—something that had started right after the Orb was brought aboard. Visiting the experiment site, she met with MiRi and Dr. Edward.

With a calculating expression, MiRi explained, "I placed anti-nullification fields around the Orb because it weakens all energy nearby. Touching it directly isn't recommended—it feels like being stung by millions of needles."

SumireV nodded in approval and turned to Dr. Edward.

"From what I've researched so far," Dr. Edward reported concisely, "this Orb came from another universe. Its matter doesn't match anything in Universe-26. I extracted a few samples and discovered several traits: it's in liquid form, it feeds on energy, and it emits strange transmissions. I believe this thing is a tracker from another universe. I'm trying to pinpoint which universe it originated from and will update you once I know."

SumireV crossed her arms. "I see. You've all done good work, but stay on high alert—we don't know what kind of disaster this Orb might bring down on us. Let me try something."

Raising her hand, SumireV used her Core of Absolute to construct an Infinity Barrier around the Orb, yet the sphere still drained energy and refused to be fully contained. SumireV could sense it transmitting a signal outward to another universe. Understanding the urgency, Dr. Edward immediately got to work trying to sever the transmission.

SumireV returned to the control room to check on the Divine Bullet's impact. She hailed Saifuru, a Scout Unit A officer of Interstellar Chronicle. "Saifuru, do you have a status update on The Hunt armada? We launched the Divine Bullet at their solar system days ago, targeting their command center."

Saifuru replied, "Target hit, though it didn't wipe out their entire civilization. However, because of the massive energy signature from the blast, a Government Authority fleet is now heading toward Pantera. I can see their Main Fleet—it consists of Galactic-Class spaceships escorted by two Archons. They've been raiding planets along the way for resources. I'm sure their smaller vanguard was wiped out by our defenses, but... the main fleet is coming."

SumireV placed the entire Pantera Sector on war footing and unlocked all planetary arsenals. "PROTOCOL-G INITIATED!" she announced.

Saifuru, Ampasy, and Nagasawa took position along the perimeter, with SumireV standing directly ahead of them. In the distance, starlight began to bend—the Government Armada was approaching at lightspeed.

SumireV already had a trap laid out: draw them inside the perimeter using a pocket gravity pull, then hit them with a surprise strike from Saifuru's Voltrauma Electric—which burned hotter than the sun and carried EMF properties strong enough to disrupt enemy ships—before raining down everything they had. Nagasawa would target enemy logistics, Ampasy would serve as a frontline distraction, and SumireV would go head-to-head with the enemy's elite units.

Suddenly—BOOM—thousands of spaceships dropped out of lightspeed alongside more than four motherships.

Chapter 8 - Incineration of the Soul

SumireV immediately triggered her gravity pull, yanking half the incoming fleet deep into the perimeter. Saifuru unleashed Chain Lightning, turning a 250 km radius into an electrocuted ground zero. His lightning was so intense it tore heavily shielded warships clean in half. Instantly, the entire perimeter lit up in a blinding barrage of lasers—nothing was visible except overlapping beams of fire. One-third of the enemy fleet was destroyed within moments.

From the very back of the fleet, Admiral Blackbear watched calmly. "Yeah, keep burning through your traps. We'll exhaust your manpower soon enough."

Nagasawa and Ampasy launched a counteroffensive from the east, ramming enemy vessels at 300,000 km/h with multi-layered hulls built to absorb impacts like sponges, targeting Space Fighter Carriers and Support Vessels. SumireV used her Creation power to forge an armada out of thin air, fueled by the ambient energy from explosions and enemy warships. Fire was exchanged across the void, though SumireV's suppressive barrage overwhelmed them.

"ACTIVATE YOUR EMP AGAIN! NOW!" SumireV shouted to Saifuru.

"Roger that—activating right now!" Saifuru shocked the enemy's second fleet, though strangely, it wasn't nearly as effective as the first strike.

SumireV realized immediately that the enemy had deployed energy nullifiers. She hailed Nagasawa: "Nagasawa, we're dealing with energy nullifiers inside the enemy armada. I need you and Ampasy to locate and destroy them ASAP! We're outnumbered!"

The battle raged fiercely, tearing through defenses as casualties spiked. SumireV began charging a Radical Collapse with her Core to wipe out the enemy motherships—only for a fierce spear to strike and shatter the orb prematurely. SumireV looked up and spotted an Archon.

"Oh no," SumireV murmured.

The Archon laughed. "Hahaha! Got you now! We're pushing forward—I want to see what you're really made of!"

SumireV smirked. "Looks like you're the ones who fell into my trap."

SumireV cast a Quasi Black Hole, which the Archon resisted. "We already know your moves, Sumi!"

SumireV simply stepped aside—revealing the real fully charged Radical Collapse behind her. The one she had created earlier was only a decoy.

"Ah... I guess this is it," the Archon breathed right before—KABOOM!

Admiral Blackbear watched his jaw drop as nearly his entire vanguard got wiped out. He immediately ordered the Second Armada forward with another 25,000 spaceships and two massive motherships.

Heavily wounded by the Radical Collapse, the Archon attempted to retreat, only for Saifuru to ambush her and drag her down onto a nearby planet.

"I'll never forget how you destroyed my planet!" Saifuru snarled. "My family died there—all because your ego got bruised!"

The Archon unleashed her Phoenix power, an eternal flame of Judgment. Saifuru activated his Voltrauma Electric and overclocked his body with nuclear reactor fission for extra energy output. Clearly faster than the Archon, Saifuru struck hard enough to crumble mountains, moving so swiftly that chunks of torn earth flew everywhere.

The Archon sneered. "Look at you, trying to get validation from a Cosmic being like me? In your sweetest dreams!"

"Quit talking and fight me!" Saifuru slammed a lightning spear into her chest, triggering a massive electric shockwave. Unfazed, the Archon blasted Saifuru back with a thermobaric wave, only for Saifuru to surge right back and unleash a Voltarum Shockwave. Both pushed into Stage 3 output, clashing with blinding auras—Unending Flame against Voltarum Storm. Saifuru summoned his World Breaker Sword while the Archon conjured her Spear of Judgment.

They clashed relentlessly, every blow sending shockwaves across the surface, counter after counter. Whenever the Archon tried charging her planet-destroyer attack, Saifuru disrupted her recovery. Falling for a feint, the Archon dodged the wrong way—allowing Saifuru to slash her skin. At first she thought she was fine, until her skin began decaying and hardening.

Fear crept into the Archon's voice. "How did you hurt a Cosmic being, you moronic human?!"

Saifuru smiled coldly. He had fused his Frost ability with Voltrauma, creating Voltra-Frost. Every movement poisoned the air with freezing static, triggering coagulation necrosis inside the lungs. Trying desperately to escape, the Archon was pushed down again as Saifuru turned the landscape into a blasted wasteland.

"ARCHON! YOU WILL NEVER ESCAPE ME!" Saifuru roared. "Look me in the eyes. Nothing is fine. The price of your greed is your life and soul. Beg me for mercy. Now I am the violence—I AM THE SICKNESS! I won't accept your silence. You will beg for forgiveness. When the last tree has fallen, there will be nowhere left to hide. I will be your nightmare!"

Pushing Phaser 5 to the absolute limit, Saifuru's eyes glowed purple-red as he overcharged his power and tore through everything in his path. Left speechless, the Archon lost both arms and legs in seconds before Saifuru impaled her into the ground with the World Breaker Sword and Voltra-Frost.

"This is for everything you took from me," Saifuru said coldly and precisely. "For your greed—feel disintegration."

The final strike cracked the planet's crust, shattering the world into drifting fragments along with the Archon.

Watching from afar, SumireV whispered, "Your potential has been unlocked. You deserve it."

On his bridge, Admiral Blackbear shot up from his chair, ripping off his glasses in disbelief. "I can't believe my eyes! That was just one of SumireV's soldiers?! ONE of them is that STRONG?! Release our Herald and our last Archon RIGHT FU*KING NOW!" He slammed his fist onto the control console. "Fire our Fission Anti-Matter Bombs! That'll buy them time while we pull back—the Orion fleet will reinforce us for a secondary assault on SumireV's stronghold!"

Chapter 9 - Almighty Foe

Seeing the incoming barrage of Anti-Matter Bombs, SumireV activated her Zeroth-Level Quasi Black Hole (with Prometheus Modulation) and successfully swallowed every single warhead. Just as she thought the crisis was handled, a cosmic ray struck her directly, sending her plummeting onto a planet below. Looking up, she found thousands of Government Authority weapons firing lasers and rockets at her.

SumireV deployed her Infinity Barrier to block the storm of fire, spotting a dozen enemy elites alongside an Archon wielding the Necrosis ability. Analyzing them instantly, she realized the elites' powers were specifically chosen to counter her.

She quickly hailed Communication Control. "This is SumireV speaking. I will engage the enemy's main elite force while the frontline divisions continue pressing their fleet. Rear command, charge the Radical Collapse—Orion is about to join the fight against us. Hold the line. I'm with you all; we can win this battle just like in the past—"

Enemy jammers cut her transmission mid-sentence.

SumireV levitated into the air. "Why even try fighting me? This won't be my demise—it'll be my warm-up."

As enemy forces braced for impact, SumireV's eyes glowed orange and she activated her Core. "Let's see what I can do with my new skill—Cognitive Mindstorm!"

Instantly, the entire battlefield filled with manifested artillery, armored vehicles, battleships, HIMARS launchers, and every war machine from land to space. Opening fire on the enemy line, she vaporized the first wave in seconds. When the enemy began charging a massive railgun, SumireV's Global Abomination (GAV-B/P) dropped cluster bombs that disabled the gun and killed the repair crews around it. Believing they had destroyed SumireV's vanguard vehicles, the enemy pressed forward—only to realize thousands of heavy artillery batteries and battleships stood waiting behind the hills. Hundreds of thousands of shells screamed through the sky, followed by thousands of bombers dropping incendiary munitions. Panic tore through the enemy ranks as casualties skyrocketed in minutes.

Suddenly, four elite troops rushed SumireV directly: an Energy Nullifier, a Shockwave user, a Malware user, and an Absorber. Using Vulcan-E1, SumireV flooded the area with water and sent high-voltage lightning surging through it, forcing the elites to take to the air with rocket packs. Activating Phaser IV to endure the fight, she felt her energy draining rapidly while Malware weakened her tech's damage output. When she tried Flame and Windstorm, Shockwave canceled it out; when she used a lightning blitz, Absorber tanked and redirected it. Moving at sub-sonic speed, SumireV mixed Sand and Wind to kick up a blinding sandstorm. Because Vulcan-E1 could only combine two elements at once, she used her Core to conjure a second combination—Blizzard and Wind—turning the entire battlefield into a choking blizzard-sandstorm.

Taking advantage of the blind chaos, she picked off the elites one by one. She targeted the Energy Nullifier first: well protected against energy attacks, he wasn't prepared for a physical explosive trident shield-buster, which punched straight through his barrier and detonated beside him. Next, she turned on Malware, who was hiding behind an artificial infinity barrier; SumireV grew crystal spikes beneath them and crushed them between two massive crystal walls inside their own shield.

Searching for Absorber and Shockwave, she felt their signatures already fleeing—just as two immense energy signatures closed in at supersonic speed. Raising her Infinity Barrier just in time, SumireV watched two Heralds—a Death user and a Telekinesis user—blow the storm apart.

"Well, hello there, false Herald," Death sneered. "We brought you a gift."

Moments later, another terrifying opponent crashed onto the planet in a massive explosion: an Archon Necromancer.

Unfazed, SumireV crossed her arms. "So this is the best the Government can offer? Battle suit—activate."

She charged forward while her war machines laid down heavy covering fire as a distraction. Telekinesis deflected the projectiles to shield Death, who engaged SumireV head-on while the Necromancer raised ancient soldiers wielding elemental powers. Death flung Chains of Darkness to bind SumireV; she fired shells at the chains, only for them to melt on contact with the grasping hands at the tips. Evading at supersonic speed, SumireV quickly spotted the flaw and shot rockets at the chain links themselves rather than the tips, snapping the Chains of Darkness. Next, she used GAV-P/B to drop five tungsten rods on Death, though Telekinesis deflected them all.

SumireV frowned. "You know what? You all suck. Just die already."

She triggered Clock Lock, freezing both Heralds in place, then immediately dropped a Quasi Black Hole (with Prometheus Modulation), wiping them both out.

Still pestered by the Necromancer's ancient elemental army—and annoyed by stray lightning bolts—SumireV muttered, "If he keeps summoning these things, he's still nearby, just invisible and masking his energy sense... I have an idea."

Deploying Energy Beacons around the battlefield to draw power, she used her Core to rain thousands of artillery shells across the entire landscape. Spotting a subtle spatial distortion, she fired a laser cannon at it, though it kept shifting positions. Charging in with her hammer, she tried pulling the Necromancer and his cloak of invisibility into a black hole, nearly succeeding before the Necromancer manipulated space itself to resist.

"Damn it, I forgot you're an Archon," SumireV growled. Instantly, she dragged the Necromancer into her Dimension. Inside, reality warped and shattered; standing at a distance, SumireV snapped the Necromancer's legs into atoms with intense gravity. "Witness your own demise. First Level: Neutronium Stars!"

The compressed stellar mass disintegrated his body. Leaving her Dimension, SumireV checked her Core strain—already at 80%—and activated her Emergency Buffer to bring it back down to 60%. Attempting to reach Nagasawa, she learned the Orion Fleet had arrived and was tearing into Nagasawa and Ampasy.

Arriving in open space, SumireV found 90% of Nagasawa's fleet destroyed and Ampasy badly battered by six Orion Heralds—four of whom Ampasy had already killed. Knowing they had run out of nanobots for repairs, SumireV used her Core to manifest a dozen support ships loaded with nanobots to stabilize Nagasawa's remaining vessels.

"Nagasawa, use the pieces to open a portal!" SumireV ordered. "We'll loose that Space Kraken to delay Orion's assault while we fall back. I need to return to base to restore my Core."

Nagasawa nodded and opened the portal, unleashing the Space Kraken straight into the Orion Fleet and forcing their remaining two Heralds onto the defensive. SumireV and Nagasawa's fleet escaped via Lightspeed Travel back to Planet Pantera-44, their Restore and Repair Center.

Seeing the base damaged and dead Government infiltrators littering the perimeter, SumireV heard MiRi's voice over comms: "Hey, this is MiRi. We were attacked a few hours ago—they tried stealing our tech, but we held them off with minor casualties. I deployed two Mechatronic divisions under my command, and Edward sent his Chemical Troopers to guard the Stronghold Vault."

SumireV sighed in relief. "Thanks, MiRi—I appreciate all of you." Clapping her hands for attention, she addressed her troops. "Listen up! We've already used our first frontliners; now we move to the second wave. Nagasawa, Ampasy, and Saifuru—stand down, recover, and help repair our solar system. I'm taking Astra, Mao, and Iflaren as our second frontline assault. You all have five hours to prepare."

Chapter 10 - Counter-Offensive

SumireV returned to the battlefield with an entirely new fleet—this time specifically designed to counter enemy armadas, outfitted with heatwave cannons for area denial capable of neutralizing Orion's Lightspeed Tech. Deploying a massive circular energy barrier, SumireV trapped a third of the Orion armada inside, then fired heatwaves into the dome to fry the Orion crews' brains while leaving their technology minimally damaged.

Astra leaped into action using her Strings of Domination, each strand burning hot enough to slice straight through energy shields, while Mao channeled ambient energy to empower Astra and help clear out Orion cluster formations. Iflaren led the main charge in a V-formation to punch right through the enemy armada. With her Core still recovering from strain, SumireV shifted into a support role—managing technology, repairs, teleport mobility, and heavy artillery and orbital strikes.

The Orion Armada joined forces with the remnants of the Government Authority fleet, though most of Blackbear's ships had already fled. SumireV launched thousands of missiles and rockets to punch through their shields while Astra and Mao hunted down enemy support vessels. Keeping larger ships behind medium and light cruisers for rapid bombardment, Iflaren hailed every unit: "I'll ram them the moment they're caught off guard. Overwhelm them—don't give them room to breathe. Astra, let me know the second their shield reactors drop."

"Acknowledged," SumireV replied. "We'll lay down suppressive fire."

SumireV's second wave of artillery shook the Orion fleet, leaving them dazed and reeling. Realizing something catastrophic was coming, the Orion General ordered an immediate emergency retreat.

Astra checked in: "They're pulling back—what are our orders? Spare them or finish them?"

SumireV cut in sharply, "End them. Do not let them escape—kill as many as possible!"

Every vessel in the Interstellar Chronicle Battle Fleet engaged Robust Mode, overclocking energy capacity to 300% and bypassing weapon reload limits altogether.

"They'll learn what it means to face the best of the best," SumireV declared. "Witness the almighty power of Interstellar Chronicle! FIRE!"

The entire fleet unloaded without pause. Iflaren activated his Stage 3 power, The Vile Venom, scattering hundreds of tiny venom droplets that multiplied fifteenfold per drop upon impact, corroding and weakening enemy hulls. Astra unleashed her Whip of Reality, slashing open a massive spatial fracture that swallowed dozens of Orion warships into an unknown void. Mao's eyes glowed yellow as he drained the energy from the Orion ships and funneled it directly to SumireV, who routed it into every IC vessel while Iflaren coated the bows of their ships with pointed Vile Venom shields built to pierce anything.

"DO IT NOW!" SumireV commanded.

Without hesitation, Iflaren drove the entire fleet forward at lightspeed, ramming straight through the remaining Orion forces. Only the Orion Command Ship managed to slip away, half-destroyed, through an emergency Lightspeed Travel portal while its panicked general screamed for the gateway to open.

When the final assault ended, SumireV surveyed hundreds of thousands of wrecked Orion and Government Authority ships. Checking the isolated one-third of the Orion fleet she had trapped earlier, she saw it remained largely intact—with every enemy troop inside dead.

"Astra, haul those ships in with your strings," SumireV instructed. "DO NOT damage them—we need to study their tech. I'll prepare storage bays for them. Mao and Iflaren, assist with casualties and repairs. We have a lot of work ahead of us."

Hours later, they returned to Pantera-55, where Zack greeted them in astonishment. "Whoa, how did you finish so fast?!"

Iflaren grinned. "You know us—we didn't leave them any other option, hahaha."

Using her technology, SumireV constructed temporary storage facilities for the captured Orion hardware on Planet Pantera-23, while thousands of overworked battleships lined up for recharging and repairs.

SumireV whispered to herself, "It's time to recover—and we have to move fast, because we never know what might hit us next."

Chapter 11 - Integration

SumireV stood aboard the Control Space Station watching over all the Pantera Sectors. Checking the statistics, she saw that repairs across the entire Pantera Solar System were roughly 60% complete. She glanced at her tablet to verify that the Core of Absolute was usable—integrity sat at 98%.

Dr. Arc materialized behind her. "SumireV, we've picked up an unknown frequency and movement on Earth-A0D4."

Her Core suddenly felt heavy inside her chest. SumireV gasped as old memories surged back, though she quickly forced them down. "Show me. Now."

Dr. Arc projected a hologram. "This is everything we've gathered so far. Perhaps you understand it better than I do."

SumireV studied the readings, sensing her Core trying to speak to her in an inaudible hum.

Dr. Arc began explaining. "Sumi, I know I'm telling you this late, but something remains unfinished on Earth-A0D4, and you have to seek it out. You're a hero—humanity's finest regard. Ever since you received the Core of Absolute, from what I can tell, you haven't been using it at 100% potential. According to the Ancient Record Journal, previous users of the Absolute could bend reality effortlessly, whereas you keep straining yourself."

SumireV took a deep breath. "I understand, Arc. I forgive you. As for my Core, I think you're right—it keeps pulling me back toward Earth-A0D4, almost as if I missed a step when receiving it. I want to go back there, but we're still mentally unprepared and in the middle of rebuilding. Maybe soon, we'll return to Earth-A0D4."

Dr. Arc nodded in acknowledgment before adding, "We also received news that Orion-TW3 is now at war with the Government Authority of the Universe because they feel betrayed. The bigger problem is that The Empire recently developed a technology called M-GenMu—'Manual-Generative Mutation'—which alters biological cells to resist virtually any threat."

SumireV shot Dr. Arc a knowing look, silently signaling that Interstellar Chronicle needed to steal that tech.

Down on Planet Pantera-02, Saifuru was being treated by MiRi and Dr. Edward. Saifuru had suffered severe damage; his organs were failing and required cybernetic modification to keep him alive, though signs already showed he was recovering. Ampasy and Nagasawa arrived after finishing repairs on neighboring planets, reuniting with everyone.

"How are you guys holding up?" Nagasawa asked.

"Everything seems to be going according to plan," Dr. Edward replied. "The Second Main Assault Unit should be arriving any moment now."

A few minutes later, Astra, Mao, and Iflaren walked in.

"We f*cked Orion up so badly they probably won't be coming back," Iflaren grinned.

Everyone shook hands and traded greetings. "I just hope we don't get dragged into any more wars against cosmic beings," Astra sighed. "I'm way too tired." They settled into chatting, swapping stories about the battle and the powers they had unleashed.

Meanwhile, SumireV visited the lab located in the deep-space center of the Pantera Solar System to inspect Vulcan-E2, which was finally complete and ready for field testing. Taking the device outside, she fired a fused blast of four elements—lightning, fire, wind, and ice—achieving results far superior to Vulcan-E1.

The next day, with all repairs completed, SumireV announced a faction-wide vacation. Anyone was free to travel anywhere, though she advised staying within the Pantera Solar System for safety while automated AI handled remaining tasks. Checking on the captured Orion tech, she found very little intact—almost all systems had self-destructed except for a single Blackbox containing a voicemail. She instructed a team of scientists to extract and decrypt its contents before leaving the lab.

Returning to her normal routine, SumireV spent the day acting as a professor, teaching a lecture on black holes.

"A black hole is one of the most dangerous phenomena in the universe," she explained enthusiastically in the classroom. "Its gravitational pull is so immense that no amount of force can pull you back out once you cross the event horizon—which means you're dead. Depending on its size, a small black hole kills quickly and almost instantaneously. With ultra-massive black holes like TON 618 or Phoenix A, however, it takes longer to be swallowed while crossing the horizon. Massive black holes disrupt everything nearby, and more importantly, you actually feel time slow down. Every black hole possesses a Singularity of infinite density, crushing you down to atoms through a process called spaghettification."

Once class dismissed, SumireV headed down to Pantera-02, where most of her elite units were relaxing. She spotted Iflaren practicing his Acid and Venom abilities while Nagasawa and MiRi lounged in a swimming pool. Waving to let them carry on, she stopped by Ampasy's room to check on him.

"Ampy, how are you feeling? Do you need any upgrades on your Phaser—or maybe your muscle fibers?"

Ampasy shook his head. "No need, Prof. You've already done more than enough for me. I appreciate the offer, truly, but you're the one who needs rest."

SumireV nodded and left the room.

Later, while sitting in the lounge, all of the Assault Units gathered around her. Nagasawa spoke up first. "SumireV... tell me about your past."

SumireV raised an eyebrow. "Are you sure you want to hear this?"

"Yes," Nagasawa replied. "I overheard what you did in the A0D4 Solar System."

SumireV fell quiet for a moment before nodding. "Gather around, all of you. I'll tell you what happened there."

She began her story. "Earth-A0D4 is where I first became... strong. To be precise, the Protector of Humanity. Our world was already locked in war against other humans when all of Universe-26 was suddenly invaded by the Liminal. Humanity was losing, and the universe united to stand against the Liminal forces. A lone Herald fought the Liminal by himself—and lost, even though he was the last cosmic defender left at that moment. I was dying nearby, bleeding out from a direct hit, when that dying Herald gave me his Core of Absolute. It corrupted me almost instantly. Dr. Arc tried to assist, but when I lost control, I unleashed a TON 618 black hole. It killed everything—Dr. Arc, the Liminal forces, and innocent civilians alike. Septillions died, mostly innocents who couldn't evacuate in time. TON 618 killed everyone instantly—children, teenagers, adults, the elderly, the sick, the disabled. They all died. I killed them, and I know I did. That's when I started asking myself whether I'm good or evil—and I know I sit somewhere in between."

Silence hung heavily over the room as everyone processed her words. Finally, Ampasy asked, "Why did you still kill people back then when you always tell us to avoid collateral damage?"

"I know," SumireV answered softly. "But things don't always go according to plan. Personally, I wish I never had to take a single innocent life. Yet in desperate, chaotic situations where everyone faces extinction, what choice do you have? Eliminate the threat completely at a terrible cost, or leave the threat alive to later claim even more lives than necessary? We minimize the risk as close to zero as possible, though it's never zero. At the very least, we show up to aid people, don't we? Any other questions?"

Just then, Saifuru—recovering from his coma—spoke up. "What happened to Intellectual Chronicle? Tell us the full story."

SumireV smiled faintly and nodded. "Intellectual Chronicle wasn't just a faction—it was life and peace. I saw everyone there as family. Most of our members were people I had rescued and sheltered myself. I founded Intellectual Chronicle primarily to shield people from threats and grief. Everything went well until the height of our war against the Government Authority, back when they were at their absolute peak. It turned out... most of my own people turned against me. Some out of jealousy, some out of fear, and some simply wanting an easy peace—even if it meant surrendering to the Government Authority.

"They tricked me and got me sealed inside a dimension by their Sentinel. Those who stayed loyal to me were slaughtered. Eighty-five percent of my own people betrayed me because they wanted to watch me fall—because I had never fallen before. The remaining fifteen percent fought to their very last breath without kneeling, and they died for it. When I finally broke free from the dimensional seal, all I saw was treachery... and the best friend I loved dearly dying right before my eyes. His eyes begged me to save him, and he tried to tell me he loved me—right before an enemy Tanker Soldier crushed his head like a watermelon under a sledgehammer.

"Looking into the eyes of everyone who betrayed me, I shut off all mercy. Even my own soul asked what the point of protecting people was if the very ones you swore to guard turned knives on you. I decided to end everything. I had finally had enough—and that was when I understood why my gut had felt uneasy all along. Right then and there, I summoned a massive Sagittarius A* black hole, killing everyone and erasing every hope, dream, and ambition in that system. After that day, I wandered alone for centuries with no one except Dr. Arc and a few AIs. Eventually I met Ampasy... and then I met all of you. That's when I founded Interstellar Chronicle—with strict standards. Everyone in Interstellar Chronicle must have an IQ above 115 and proven general skills. Every single member has passed a simulation clearing an entire enemy camp using only a suppressed handgun. I want everyone combat-ready in case the worst happens again. And here we are—fully prepared behind a thousand layers of defense."

Nobody in the lounge spoke. Some wiped away tears; others shook their heads in disbelief. Gently, MiRi placed a hand on SumireV's shoulder. "We're always here for you, Sumi. We love you, and we take care of each other."

SumireV smiled, clasping MiRi's hand, before everyone stepped in together to wrap SumireV in a hug.

Meanwhile, just outside the Pantera Solar System, an enemy spy attempted to infiltrate Interstellar Chronicle to sabotage SumireV's plans and steal intelligence. The instant the spy crossed the border, their ship exploded in a burst of fire—taken out by the Anti-Spy Squad under explosives specialist Ashton.

"Report," Ashton spoke calmly over comms. "This is Ashton. Just eliminated an enemy stealth ship in Sector 4."

Chapter 12 - Great Loss

Somewhere in a galaxy deep within Government Authority of the Universe territory, a battered armada limped back from its battle against Interstellar Chronicle. One warship popped open its hull, and blood spilled onto the deck; each vessel carried 200,000 soldiers packed inside amid horrific wounds and corpses. Medical teams rushed forward to treat survivors missing limbs, chunks of flesh, eyes, and even heads.

"They're monsters! Ferocious monsters!" a soldier screamed wildly. "Just ONE fu*king IC soldier tore my entire unit apart—ripping limbs off like paper!"

"There were twelve of us siblings—all my brothers were killed!" another sobbed. "Their corrosion melted their brains like butter! I can't—I CAN'T TAKE THIS ANYMORE!"

Others suffered even graver wounds, clutching their own spilled intestines while relying entirely on pain suppressants. Roughly 100 temporary field hospitals were erected near the drop point to handle an estimated 400 million casualties. Because the faction poured nearly its entire economy into military hardware rather than medicine or education, its low medical technology couldn't save them quickly, and many died of shock.

Inside one of the military camps, undeployed recruits crowded around a handful of scarred survivors.

"So... what's it actually like?" one recruit asked.

A survivor stared blankly ahead. "It's like walking straight into a meat grinder. You don't even get to set foot on their soil. Why? Because they bury you under everything they've got. The suppressive barrage is so overwhelming you can't even breathe—their rockets pack some oxygen-depleting agent that fu*ks up your lungs. And after that, they come to you. Our own warships turn into the battlefield. They punch straight through ship hulls barehanded or carve inside with blades. You shoot, you throw bombs—everything ricochets. They're terrifyingly smart, wearing top-grade armor and deflecting attacks by dodging or angling impacts away. Some of our super-ability users tried burning them, only for IC's energy shields to block it and counter right back. I watched them clear hallway by hallway, room by room, faster than we could react—unless one of our Heavy-Class fighters stepped in, and even that wasn't guaranteed to stop them."

Another survivor chimed in grimly. "I've been through plenty of wars, but fighting Interstellar Chronicle? IC is dangerous. Nah, screw that—I'd rather get shipped off to fight the Pivot Federation or The Empire than IC. Those maniacs are bloodthirsty and on hyper-alert 24/7. Fighting them just for fun? Just to throw our lives away? Fu*k the Ministry, man. We've fought multiple factions and we've never won a major battle against IC—only tiny skirmishes they take back anyway. It's pointless."

"Is it true IC's leader—the Professor, SumireV—actually controls black holes and gravity?" a private asked quietly.

"Yes," the survivor confirmed. "The second regiment paired with Orion saw it firsthand. It inflicted brutal losses on Orion too—we couldn't even recover their dead because their bodies were atomized inside the singularity."

"So with IC," another soldier muttered, "you plan on invading their planet, and instead they invade your warship?"

"Exactly right. They close in fast right after crippling your ship's outer turrets, forcing you into close-quarters combat inside your own corridors."

"Did we really betray Orion?" a soldier asked. "When they showed up, we got ordered to pull out of the combat zone. I heard Orion took IC's direct counteroffensive head-on and lost 90% of their fleet, while the Government only lost 60%."

"Pretty sure Orion is going to open another warfront against the Government Authority," a veteran muttered darkly. "Sh*t... why did I have to be born in this era?"

Back at Government Authority Headquarters, Admiral Blackbear faced sharp questioning over the debacle, presenting video logs of his shattered armada.

"Bear," Admiral Adam said sternly, "I told you not to get overconfident picking a fight with IC. I knew it was a bad call. We need to spend our resources wisely."

"We need their tech to stay superior!" Blackbear snapped.

"It's too difficult—we can't beat them directly," one commander argued. "Besides, we're already at war with The Hunt; our transport ships keep getting hijacked and our tech stolen. On top of that, the Pivot Federation is at war with us, and intelligence reports they're holding secret diplomatic talks with Orion—the very allies we betrayed!"

Admiral Blackbear fell silent for a beat before saying, "Let's finish this cleanly. We need stronger allies. Reach out to Parliament Abstract for their Cryo Tech, and The Commonwealth for their natural resources and heavy war machines."

Chapter 13 - Unfinished Past

Back in Interstellar Chronicle, SumireV knew it was time to return to Earth-A0D4. It wouldn't be easy—the region lay far beyond IC territory, in a sector she had previously ravaged with TON 618 during the Liminal War. Now that the black hole had finally dissipated, intelligence confirmed the Government Authority had moved in and heavily fortified the sector.

Calling her senior officers and friends into the meeting room, SumireV announced her departure. "I called you all here because I have a mission to carry out, and it's dangerous. I'm not taking any of you with me—I'm going alone. Work together and defend the Pantera Solar System while I'm gone. If you need to relocate it, move it. Minimize risks and casualties while I'm away."

Ampasy slammed his hand on the table. "Sumi! Why go alone?! Why not take us along? We can back you up!"

"Yeah!" MiRi agreed anxiously. "We can't afford to lose you! You're the leader of Interstellar Chronicle—everyone depends on you!"

"I understand," SumireV said firmly. "But this mission is too dangerous—and none of you possess cosmic resistance at that scale. If I'm forced to unleash my full power, I risk killing you all by accident. Do not follow me. That's an order."

Everyone fell silent and nodded in reluctant obedience. As she turned to leave, SumireV added, "Good luck. Stay safe, stay alive. You're all the finest humanity has to offer."

Visiting the control room, SumireV met Dr. Arc and downloaded every piece of data on the A0D4 Solar System directly into her battle suit. After sending contingency instructions to all senior IC leaders, she boarded a space fighter and initiated Lightspeed Travel toward A0D4.

Along the way, she reviewed the tactical data: now that TON 618 had faded, an enormous Government Authority armada—roughly 600 billion warships—guarded the A0D4 Solar System behind thick fortifications, planetary ring cannons, energy shields, and an estimated 90 trillion defenders. Her heart pounded as her anger began to stir. Memories of past betrayals, the fall of Intellectual Chronicle, foolish leaders making reckless choices, arrogance, and helplessness fueled her rage. Accelerating corruption shifted her eyes from glowing orange toward blood red.

"No one will save any of you," she whispered.

The instant she dropped into the A0D4 Solar System, every planetary defense battery and frontline ship unleashed everything they had—enough firepower to annihilate a hundred suns. When the weapons finally paused to vent overheating barrels, they stared in horror: SumireV hovered calmly inside her Infinity Barrier without a single scratch.

"This is your end," SumireV spoke, her voice echoing coldly. "There will be no hope, no dreams, no second chances. I will slaughter every single one of you in a way not even a Celestial could explain. This is what you earn for attacking Interstellar Chronicle! I will be your demise, your doomsday, the nightmare you never wake up from! None of you even realize what you've provoked. I am the one who slayed the Liminal! I AM the one who stopped The One from conquering this universe! I AM THE ONE WHO DESTROYED UNIVERSE-95 ALONGSIDE THE CELESTIALS! There will be no mercy, no smile—witness reality collapse as the very stars give up on you."

Raising a hand, SumireV used her Core of Absolute to manifest billions of warships, flooding deep space with blinding light, cutting-edge technology, and the worst of all: Anti-War Machine spacecraft.

"I didn't come here to play games—I came to end all of your lives. I am the Chosen One. Now witness Total Annihilation Collapse!"

Activating her cosmic power, she pushed her output into critical overload. Nearby stars began plummeting directly into the Government Authority defenses as surrounding space turned crimson. Behind SumireV, the ultra-massive black hole TON 618 tore open—surrounded by exploding star clusters while the galaxy shrieked as light itself vanished down the throat of the singularity.

Defenders watched in helpless terror, begging for mercy. Even commanders and marshals broke down as communications went dead, leaving them unable to say goodbye to their loved ones. Falling stars and SumireV's Anti-War Machine craft melted warships and bastions effortlessly. Soldiers snapped mentally—shooting themselves in the head, tearing at their own flesh in panic, bashing their skulls against bulkheads as SumireV stripped away all oxygen while light itself screamed across space.

"GOD, PLEASE KILL ME! I DON'T WANT TO SEE THIS ANYMORE—KILL ME, KILL ME!" one soldier shrieked, gouging out his own eyes. Others wept like children, calling out for their mothers to save them. It was unspeakably gruesome—bodies imploding, boiled blood, skin, muscle, and bone whipping into massive red ribbons orbiting the system before disappearing into the black hole, screams suffocated in a vacuum devoid of air. Humans, Heralds, Archons, and Sentinels alike vanished without distinction once their end was decreed.

SumireV swept the entire sector clean in less than an hour—leaving only a single survivor alive to carry the tale back to Government Authority space. Just like that, the Government Authority lost its second-largest armada port.

Once everything had been consumed, SumireV swiftly dissipated TON 618, snapped back to her senses, and injected herself with anti-corruption serum.

"Oh, wow—that actually works," she breathed. "Well, it went according to plan. Letting corruption take over briefly let me clear their defenses without holding back—and they were enemies anyway. No need to feel guilty, SumireV. Remember: sacrifice for the greater good. Besides, building a fortress here was suspicious to begin with."

Descending toward Earth-A0D4, she used her Core Sense to trace the faint negative energy pulling at her. Spotting a tiny pinpoint of light shining from the core of Earth-A0D4, SumireV invoked her Core of Absolute—Dimension of Logic—and stepped inside.

Chapter 14 - Mind Storm of Predecessors

SumireV emerged into a dark, freezing, suffocating realm carpeted in burned ash. Uncountable echoes of every human era crashed against her ears—screams, weeping, agony, and the collapse of dying universes ringing simultaneously both far away and right beside her. Conjuring a bright light failed to illuminate the path ahead, yet as she walked, she spotted the faint white glow of a Core of Absolute fragment.

The moment she picked it up, the shard tore straight into her chest, ripping open flesh. Grunting in agony, she desperately tried activating her battle suit, only to find nothing responded except her manual Serum Phaser VII syringe. Still, the darkness receded slightly, and she followed the echoing pull of the remaining shards.

Voices began rising out of the gloom. "Lucky you—you don't have to hunt down 300 fragments, only four more." Another murmured, "Is this our new successor? A woman—and still human?"

As the chorus intensified, pain drove SumireV to her knees, crawling toward the fourth fragment—close enough to see, yet feeling miles away. Then she watched the shard float closer in the hands of a blurry, half-visible figure.

"Hi there, SumireV," he said gently, handing it to her. "I'm Alan, the 251st user. My ability doesn't matter right now—yours does."

Helping her up as the fourth fragment burrowed painfully into her chest, Alan instructed, "Use your Core to heal yourself. Inside the Realm of Absolute, the Core is the only power you can rely on."

Summoning the Core's power, she slowly stemmed the bleeding and rose to her feet. "Why is this place like this? Is all this pain really necessary?"

"It's normal for new users like you," Alan explained as they walked together. "I went through it myself. Focus on gathering all the fragments—after that, you'll be fine. I'll guide you. This is part of the process where the Core synchronizes and resonates with your body. Strictly speaking, you weren't even meant to wield this Core the way you got it, since Ronan passed it to you out of order."

SumireV glanced at his face. "Ronan... where is he?"

"You'll meet him as soon as we collect the rest."

Reaching the third fragment, SumireV absorbed it with far less agony as her body gradually adapted. Soon, Alan led her up a towering hill piled high with thousands of Celestial bones and ancient artifacts. SumireV's eyes widened in awe. "Did you kill all of these Celestials?"

"Not all of them—just some," Alan smiled. "These bones belong to every Celestial slain by Core of Absolute users. And if I'm not mistaken... three or five of them belong to you."

SumireV nodded quietly. "Yeah. I killed a few, though they nearly killed me."

Reaching the summit, SumireV claimed the second fragment. Before she could turn away, Alan stopped her. "Wait—I have a surprise for you, girl." Holding out his palm, he revealed the very first fragment.

As SumireV took it, all five pieces became whole. Reality swirled violently around them before the darkness shattered into blinding light. Shielding her eyes until they adjusted, SumireV gasped—standing all around the vast Realm were all 300 previous Core users.

"Welcome to the Realm of Absolute," Alan announced, "where the consciousness of every previous user resides, complete with their history and abilities at their peak."

SumireV greeted everyone—only for one voice to boom out immediately: "HEY THERE, GIRL! I'M BRIAN, THE 170TH USER!"

"Knock it off, Brian—it's not like girls are into you anyway!" someone shouted back, sparking laughter across the hillside.

"Can anyone tell me where Ronan is?" SumireV asked. Everyone pointed toward the far side of the hill.

Heading over, she found Ronan practicing with his Primordial Black Hole. Spotting her, he jumped in surprise. "Sumi?! How are you here? Did you get killed?!"

"No, I came because I felt the Core calling me back," SumireV explained. "Turns out I missed a step when receiving the Core from you."

"Ah... yeah, I handed it over in an emergency, so the transfer wasn't complete," Ronan admitted. "How have you been? Mind showing me what you can do?"

SumireV effortlessly manifested a TON 618 black hole and dissipated it just as fast.

Ronan stared in envy. "I only held this power for a year and a half—even at my peak, my abilities capped at Zeroth Level. What level are you at?"

"Currently at the Third Level," SumireV replied.

Ronan's jaw dropped. "Then I really can't teach you much! Since I'm capped at Zeroth, you should seek out the others. Some capped lower than me, some far higher—it all depends on energy control and understanding. Go on, SumireV—don't waste time on me!"

Wandering among the predecessors, SumireV marveled at their exotic skills—turning anything into offensive weapons, warping reality, reversing time, and peak Creation. Stopping before a user named Valket, the 96th user, she asked, "Sir, could you teach me your ability?"

Valket shook his head. "Are you sure your body could survive the energy? Watch this."

Activating his Elysian of Decimation, Valket drained an entire universe's energy and unleashed a concentrated beam so deafening that the shockwave ruptured SumireV's eardrums—even with Core durability. Across the void, the beam sliced two entire universes clean in half, instantly annihilating simulated invaders.

"Hmm, looks like you aren't at my tier yet," Valket noted. "I'm capped at the 9th Level. I recommend visiting Julian—she has skills that will suit you nicely."

SumireV nodded with a smile and headed to the estate of Julian, the 140th user, capped at the 6th Level.

Floating lazily, Julian greeted her. "Oh... a new successor? Welcome!"

"What can you teach me, Juli?" SumireV asked.

"Well, from what I can tell, you lack energy control. Let me give you a quick, easy lesson so you can move on—I know you're here because you're dealing with trouble back in your universe."

Stepping into an open field, Julian told SumireV to demonstrate everything she knew. SumireV ran through her repertoire from basic gravity pulls all the way up to 3rd-Level Singularity Rebuild—leaving herself panting with Core strain.

Julian diagnosed the issue immediately. "Aha! You have control over what you cast, not how you conserve it. Honestly, your baseline is impressive and your potential is extraordinary, so let me tell you why your Core keeps straining. You have massive capacity from your Core Reserve, your Battlesuit, and your six Mini Nuclear Reactors. The problem is, whenever you cast an ability, the impact releases huge amounts of energy—and you aren't reabsorbing any of that wasted energy! So you end up burning purely on your suit and nuclear reactors. Impressive, sure, but you won't last against a Celestial like that. Watch this, cutie."

Julian cast 3rd-Level Singularity Rebuild—then immediately cast it again, and again.

"See the difference? You're doing 100% damage for 100% energy cost, leaving yourself at 0% reserve and relying on batteries. I spend 100% energy for 100% damage, reabsorb the fallout to cast 70% damage for 60% energy, then 40% damage for 25% energy. The recycled energy isn't as pure as the first strike, but it's reliable and never wasted!"

"Why do your eyes turn red when you cast the 3rd Level?" SumireV asked.

"Because I'm focusing purely on Gravity," Julian explained. "Oh, I almost forgot about that! Right now, you can cast Dimensional, Creational, and Gravity-based powers. Normally when you attack, your energy leaks across all three—looking at your flow, you're putting 60% into Gravity, 20% into Dimensional, and 20% into Creation, which is why your eyes stayed normal. Watch mine: my eyes glow Blue when focusing solely on Dimension, White for Creation, and Red for Gravity. Since Gravity is your main affinity, give it a try!"

Focusing intently, SumireV whispered, "Gravity, gravity, gravity..." Her eyes blazed bright red. Sweeping a hand down, she shouted, "GRAVITY BARRAGE!" Huge craters tore into the earth, atomized by crushing force. Exploring further, she unlocked Gravity Disarray—turning an entire invisible zone into a lethal minefield of shifting gravitational vectors strong enough to crush anything entering it.

Julian walked over. "Dimensional powers probably feel weak to you right now because enemies have to step inside your Dimension first, right? What if I told you that you can trigger it outside your Dimension?"

"W-what? How?!"

Activating her Dimensional power, Julian simply pointed at a training dummy—and instantly, the dummy vanished, De-Constructed atom by sub-atom from physical existence.

"You can pull it off once you truly grasp the Law of Atoms," Julian smiled. "Pretty handy against enemies resistant to normal damage—you just deconstruct them outright."

Testing her Dimensional power immediately, SumireV managed to deconstruct half of a full spaceship before total exhaustion hit her.

"Oops—forgot how brutal your journey was just getting here," Julian laughed softly. "Go get some rest at my Estate first. You can train anytime—just ask whichever predecessor you want to learn from."

SumireV collapsed asleep right in the field, and Julian carried her inside.

Chapter 15 - Fate Rewind

SumireV woke up two days later inside Julian's Estate. "Ughh, my head hurts..." She jumped slightly noticing two women sitting beside her bed. "Who the heck are you two?"

"I'm Victoria, the 288th user, 2nd Level," the younger woman said warmly. "And this is my great-grandmother, Ava—the 287th user, 5th Level. We've been taking care of you. Your insides felt like an active oven, though you're safe now."

"I'm still too tired to move much," SumireV admitted. "Mind telling me what happened to you both—your history?"

"It happened when our home, Universe-NB00, was invaded by attackers from another universe called the Central Constellation Ace," Victoria began. "They built an imperial empire from the center of the multiverse, conquering one universe after another until they stumbled onto ours. Great-Grandma Ava fought them for 500 years until the Core began rejecting her and she passed away from old age. The Core chose me next, and I held it for nearly 250 years—until the CCA acquired Higher Authority technology. Neighboring universes collapsed rapidly before they came straight for mine. Giving up everything, I unleashed a massive ability called Big Bang—either 7th or 8th Level.
