The corruption tore my organs apart and killed me, yet it wiped out huge swaths of the CCA's army and tech, setting them back thousands of years and stopping them from stealing ours. Quite a mark to leave for humanity."

SumireV nodded sympathetically. "Lucky me—I don't have a massive multiverse empire invading my universe... well, except twice, with the Liminal and The One. Barely survived those by putting my life on the line. By the way, what are your Base Abilities? Mine is Gravity."

"Glad you made it through those hellish invasions alive," Victoria smiled. "For me, I never mastered Gravity or Dimension—they drained me too much—so I leaned mostly into Creation."

"I specialized in Energy," Ava added. "I could cast Singularity Rebuild too, though my 5th Level created Quasar Sovereignty, capable of vaporizing entire Filaments. As for you, SumireV... you probably have the potential to reach 9th Level and cast Truth Black Hole—like what saved you when you were dying, though not quite yet."

"Whoa, awesome—especially since we're all still human-based!" Sitting up for a sip of water, SumireV looked up as a man strolled into the room: Ethan, the 262nd user, 9th Level.

"Hey there, SumireV. Know who I am?"

"Ethan... the 262nd user," she read off the mark on his arm.

"Feel anything familiar about me?"

"Sort of..." SumireV tilted her head. "I can't quite explain why your aura feels so close."

"Hahaha, that's because I'm from your Universe-26—just from an era millions of years before yours!" Ethan grinned. "Want to know where Prometheus Modulation came from? It was built by my civilization! I sealed it inside a planetary core bunker tough enough to survive universe-ending attacks, turning myself into a black hole with my dying breath just so someone someday would uncover Prometheus Modulation—the same tech that let me cast 10th Level Truth Black Hole. Not to brag, I'm the strongest pure human Core of Absolute user; anyone stronger became a Herald or Celestial."

SumireV shot to her feet. "OH! You're the genius behind Prometheus Modulation?! No wonder the PM history logs listed a 10th-Level Gravity tier! Wait—so were you the one who cast Truth Black Hole when I was dying?"

Ethan smirked. "Actually, I appreciate you putting my tech to use after I died, though that cast wasn't me. Must've been Zimmerman, the 41st user—he lived long enough to evolve step-by-step from Human into a Sentinel."

"Well, looks like I found my senior boss," SumireV smiled. "Right now though, I need to test out De-Construct."

Stepping outside the Estate while Ronan watched, SumireV practiced locking onto targets. She managed to deconstruct a single dummy in three seconds, though the target had to remain still for five seconds for the lock to take hold. Trying multiple targets simultaneously, she only wiped out five out of twenty completely and partially damaged a few more.

"Hmm... if I only deconstruct their heads, they still die instantly, right?" Focusing her Dimensional power until her eyes blazed Blue, she aimed strictly at their heads—instantly killing ten out of twenty and partially deconstructing three more.

"The deeper your understanding, the deadlier the strike!" Ronan called out approvingly.

SumireV expanded her practice to houses, vehicles, fortified bunkers, and distant targets. Just then, Brian materialized out of nowhere and draped an arm over her shoulder.

"Brian, fu*k off—you sussy creep, let me—" SumireV instantly conjured a rocket, shoved Brian straight inside, and blasted him clear across the Realm.

Walking over to join Ronan, they sat together for a quiet moment. "Ronan, can I ask what happened during the Liminal Invasion?"

Ronan's face darkened with sorrow. "It happened far too fast. Our planet had barely reached Type-1 Civilization—and an unstable one at that, locked in a 50-year Solar System war. Then the Liminal arrived from another universe. I was the first human to engage him; others joined me, only to die in the blink of an eye under his radiation attacks. Back then, I knew nothing about the Core of Absolute since I was just a Herald relying on Water. I only unlocked Zeroth Level when I was already fatally wounded—too late to matter. If only I had understood the Core sooner, I might have beaten him and lived. Time wasn't kind to me. Still, you're my successor now, and I know you'll surpass me, SumireV. Back then we didn't even know factions like the Government Authority, Pivot Federation, or Orion existed—I only learned about all of them watching you fight through the Core."

SumireV moved closer and clasped his hand gently. "I understand. There's no victory without sacrifice. You entrusted the Core to me, and I will carry on as your successor. I promise you, Ronan."

Ronan squeezed her hand tightly. "We'll all be praying for you, Chosen One."

Having spent five days inside the Core, it was finally time to return to Interstellar Chronicle—she had learned enough to handle her current enemies. Before she departed, Julian stopped her with a parting tip: "SumireV, you can come back here anytime to train. Better yet, you never need to fly back to A0D4 again—you've unlocked the Core's true link, meaning you can enter here straight from your Dimension whenever you want. Also, remember we can see your activity whenever you use Core abilities, and if you're ever close to dying, we can speak to you directly from inside the Core to guide you! Take care, sister—we're always with you!"

Waving goodbye to everyone, SumireV returned to Universe-26 in the middle of the A0D4 Solar System, conjured a spacecraft with her Creation power, and initiated Lightspeed Travel back to Interstellar Chronicle.

Chapter 16 - Truth Behind SumireV

Before leaving the A0D4 system completely, SumireV circled back to trace the lingering energy left by her assault. Knowing something suspicious had been happening before she attacked, she muttered, "The Government Authority must have known about my origins. I should investigate."

Searching the ruined fortresses one by one toward the system's center for their headquarters, she suddenly sensed a presence behind her. Spinning around, she came face-to-face with a Celestial.

"So you're the one who obliterated this sector? Fascinating," the Celestial remarked.

"What are you doing here? If you're here to kill me, I'll fight you with everything I have."

"I'm not here to kill you, but—" Without warning, the Celestial unleashed an immense blast of energy intended as an instant kill. "Dead already, SumireV?"

When the glare faded, SumireV hovered untouched, her eyes blazing red and white. She immediately retaliated with a Prometheus Modulation Black Hole and a Radical Collapse alongside anti-Celestial missiles, forcing the shocked Celestial to flee through an emergency portal.

"I'll kill him sooner or later," she muttered. "First, let's see what the Government was really digging up here."

Sweeping the debris field above the destroyed headquarters, she finally spotted a floating Blackbox.

Opening the Blackbox and listening to the final audio logs before her attack, SumireV wondered, "Hmm... maybe I can reconstruct the original chip to see what truly happened during A0D4's first destruction." Using her Creation ability to build a supercomputer and restore the damaged chip—"Core of Absolute, Creation ability: Full-Construct!"—she slotted the chip in and pulled up the archived footage from the day she first absorbed the Core of Absolute.

She watched in mounting horror.

In the recording, the Liminal had actually been trying to withdraw their forces, while a completely corrupted SumireV relentlessly hunted down everyone—friend and foe alike—without showing any strain. She floated above solar systems, dropping Quasi Black Holes on planet after planet, even attacking neighboring factions approaching to observe. The footage showed her killing Dr. Arc herself by crushing his lungs with gravity. She entered civilian safe bunkers only to manifest nuclear bombs and detonate them inside, blasted cities with colossal flamethrowers that burned everyone alive, and even wiped out everyone inside a daycare. Finally, the last survivors fleeing the 320 solar systems of the A0D4 Sector died instantly as she summoned TON 618 and proclaimed, "I am not like the rest of you. I am neither evil nor good—I am the Apocalypse designated to erase." On that single day, she had claimed 5 septillion lives while several Celestials, Government Authority scouts, The Empire, and Sovereign of Eclipse watched in silence from afar.

SumireV shut off the footage. "It was part of the process... to achieve something greater, sacrifices happened—all for the greater good. Forgive me, God. Forgive me, everyone. May you all rest in peace."

Tears welled in her eyes at the realization that she had killed the people she loved most, though she quickly brushed them away. "Nothing lasts forever anyway."

Destroying the Blackbox completely, she set course for Radiant Guard territory—a faction extraordinarily wealthy thanks to its diversity—heading straight to Solar System RG31, one of Interstellar Chronicle's hidden outposts. IC maintained at least one covert planet inside every known faction's territory, sheltering over 500,000 undercover residents mostly operating underground or hollowed inside massive asteroids.

Approaching the hidden bunker, SumireV heard a guard call out, "Open the gate—it's the boss!" Inside, she met directly with Sky, the outpost's commander.

"I'm here to check in and rest a bit before heading back to our stronghold in Pantera," SumireV said.

"Yes, ma'am. Why the sudden visit?"

"Long story. I went back to A0D4 to confirm what I lost, that's all. Now tell me how things are progressing here."

"So far," Sky reported, "we've confirmed that Radiant Guard is officially at war with both The Empire and the Pivot Federation. The Empire wanted Radiant Guard's wealth and offered a lopsided alliance; when Radiant Guard refused, The Empire declared war. As for the Pivot Federation, they joined in out of loyalty to their new Empire alliance—and lingering vengeance from losing both the First and Second Radiant-Pivot Wars. Worse, Radiant Guard discovered our presence here and raided us at least three times over the past five months, costing us a few outposts. Radiant Guard is famous not only for its wealth, but for the Wall of Alcatraz—supposedly unbreakable by any faction, even Celestials."

"Hmm, fair enough," SumireV replied. "Relocate your forces for now—don't waste our manpower."

She remained at the outpost for three quiet days.

Chapter 17 - The Necessary Sacrifice

Sitting out on a veranda overlooking the city skyline of the Pantera Constellation, Nagasawa, MiRi, Iflaren, and Astra talked quietly about the Velocirax-Nyxvirus, since Iflaren had personally been on the ground during its deployment.

"Iflaren," Nagasawa and MiRi asked, "would you mind telling us what you actually witnessed during the outbreak on Planet Umbara in The Commonwealth?"

Iflaren shook his head grimly. "It was bad. I really don't like talking about it. Velocirax-Nyxvirus makes the Pivot Federation's bioweapons look half-baked."

"Wait—wasn't it banned by the Government Authority?" Astra asked. "Anyone caught using bioweapons faces an intergalactic blockade. Why did SumireV order its deployment?"

"I understand your concern, though we hold the cure," Iflaren answered. "As for why SumireV authorized it, let me be blunt: back then, The Commonwealth was a superpower while Interstellar Chronicle was barely top four. For 13 years of brutal warfare, The Commonwealth marched against us alongside its new allies—Sovereign of Eclipse, Cluster Creed, and a minority faction of Orion—throwing elite troops just to crack IC's defenses outside the city of Obscure. IC was bleeding soldiers rapidly, and SumireV lay in a coma following her battle against The Big Seven Sentinels backed by dozens of Archons and Heralds. Even though SumireV won, it put her into a coma. IC had to pull back from Commonwealth space—and unbeknownst to me at the time, Dr. Edward and Dr. Pedro held a standing order from SumireV: 'If I fall into a coma and we start losing to the Commonscum, deploy our greatest nano-virus.'

"So a Global Abomination detached from the mothership, entered orbit, and fired Velocirax-Nyxvirus warheads straight into all five cities on that planet—including Obscure, where I was fighting. It was the first time this universe ever witnessed cosmic biochemical warfare. Look... it was horrific. Victims turned into rabid monsters slaughtering each other. I was the very last soldier evacuated. I still have the bodycam footage if you really want to see it."

"Damn it, just play it on the hologram," Astra said.

Pulling up the holo-projector, Iflaren plugged in his bodycam and skipped to the outbreak. Everyone watched as the Global Abomination released five rods armed with Velocirax-Nyxvirus warheads. Each impact detonated with 20 megatons of TNT, billowing out crimson-red ash. Everyone caught inside began turning—and those few who didn't were immediately torn apart by the infected. Contaminated debris rained onto nearby military outposts, spreading the plague instantly. Infected victims saw the world tinted purple as their fingernails and teeth turned chalky and rotted away on contact; some transformed in seconds, others years later, either becoming rabid biters or sprinting straight into crowds to detonate themselves and spread more aerosolized spores. In the end, The Commonwealth had to turn an Orbit Anti-Planet Laser on its own world to glass the ground zero—losing four billion citizens on that planet alone and fracturing their coalition, leaving the war a bitter, immoral IC stalemate. Without that order, Iflaren admitted, he would have been slain by Zelaya, a super-cyborg built by The Commonwealth specifically to hunt ability users like him.

The footage was so sickening that Nagasawa bolted straight to the restroom to vomit.

"Yikes... close call for you, Iflaren," MiRi shivered. "I admire SumireV, truly, but she's lowkey terrifying. That's why I make sure never to get on her bad side."

"To be fair, the virus wasn't even designed by SumireV—it was a joint operation between my boss, Dr. Edward, and Saifuru's mentor, Dr. Pedro," Iflaren noted. "Good thing IC survived thanks to SumireV's layered contingency plans."

"Speaking of our boss, SumireV's been gone over a week now," Astra pointed out.

"She'll be back anytime soon," MiRi smiled. "We just have to wait."

Meanwhile, inside the Pantera training grounds, Ampasy and Saifuru were sparring against SumireV's combat simulation.

"She's insanely tough!" Ampasy groaned. "No wonder Heralds get squashed by her—I barely spawned in and she killed me in three seconds!"

"Look at me—I lasted over a minute thanks to my 500,000 km/h speed and Voltrauma!" Saifuru grinned. "Thankfully it's an inherited power embedded in my bloodline for generations."

"Yeah, you got yours naturally while I had to turn myself into a cyborg just to handle Phaser 5 without strain—and undergo brutal experiments fusing Serum Phaser VII straight into my bloodstream!"

Both pushed harder to beat the hologram, only to end up with a score of 0 to SumireV's 52. Suddenly, Mao popped his head in. "Hey, both of you, get out of there! The simulator engine's overheating—AND go eat lunch!" Groaning in frustration, they headed out.

Meeting Nagasawa and the others in the cafeteria, Mao, Saifuru, and Ampasy immediately noticed how pale they looked. When Saifuru asked why, Nagasawa whispered, "Interstellar Chronicle has tons of dark secrets we didn't even know about."

Saifuru smiled casually. "Oh, you mean the Velocirax-Nyxvirus? Honestly, I thought your supervisors already briefed you on that!"

Nagasawa gasped. "Wait—so SumireV wasn't even hiding it, you just forgot to tell us?!" Laughing through her relief, she started pinching Saifuru while Ampasy and Mao competed over who could eat the most spicy fried chicken wings.

"How lovely life is inside a balanced faction," Iflaren mused happily, tossing a glowing ball of Vile Venom from palm to palm. "Luxury, friends, freedom... way better than back when I served The Empire."

Not long after, SumireV arrived back at Pantera—only to fly straight through the surface of the first planet she approached. "Oh, for God's sake, I forgot there are two Panteras—one real and one decoy! Fifty-fifty odds!" Traveling on to the real Pantera, sirens and announcements warmly welcomed back the Leader of Interstellar Chronicle. Exhausted from her trials, SumireV headed straight to her quarters to sleep before attempting any more De-Construct training.

Elsewhere across deep space, trouble was quietly spreading.

In Pivot Federation space, Captain Raul's elite garrison defending the Fort of Wing fought desperately against a lone entity.

"Who sent you, bi*ch?!" Captain Raul yelled, unleashing freezing powers—only for the invader to dissolve effortlessly into Dark Smoke.

"Universe-23 has already fallen," the entity replied. "Next will be Universe-24... and eventually, Universe-26."

Inside Government Authority Headquarters, Knight Cassandra stood bleeding heavily as the same entity crushed a core of Anti-Matter Technology inside its grip. "Nice tech. Ours is stronger," it murmured before vanishing.

Orion-TW3's Fourth Largest Armada was wiped out, losing both its Lightspeed Travel Tech and its latest invention—the Weather Amplifier—as Commander Hasen choked out a final dying distress call.

At XERT-07L, Interstellar Chronicle's second headquarters, General Rahim frantically rallied his troops under siege. "This is General Rahim! Our base is under attack by an unknown enemy wielding Dark Smoke! They teleport constantly, shrug off cosmic attacks, and are nearly immune to conventional weapons! All available units to defensive positions NOW!" Before comms died, he sent an urgent ping to Pantera: "Send relief aid! They mentioned destroying Universe-23—tell SumireV it's an entity using Darkness!"

Even inside The Empire's heavily militarized sector of Poxionde-A2uC, artillery and orbital lasers fired into swirling Dark Matter Smoke—only for the smoke to reflect every blast straight back twice as strong. "Contact Pivot Federation!" General Hayato barked—only to learn Pivot Federation had been struck too and couldn't send help.

Waking the following morning, SumireV walked into the control room to find an avalanche of alerts: the Bureau Coalition, Tectonic Pact, Pivot Federation, Orion, Order of North, Cluster Creed, Radiant Guard, Imperial Phalanx, Sovereign of Eclipse—even the Government Authority—had all been attacked.

Dr. Arc stepped up behind her. "Urgent message from XERT-07L, sent by General Rahim. He lost 80% of his men and infrastructure to an entity wielding abyssal darkness."

Just then, an unknown transmission cut straight onto SumireV's private terminal:

"WHERE IS THE ORB?! RETURN IT TO US, AND WE WON'T KILL YOUR PEOPLE!"

SumireV's protective instincts flared immediately. "If our universe weakens itself fighting them," she whispered, "other multiversal invaders won't hesitate to pile on. Better to negotiate and minimize casualties." Gathering several of her strongest fighters, she prepared to confront the entity head-on.

Chapter 18 - Duality Action

SumireV mobilized a Relief Aid Fleet straight to XERT-07L while opening a direct channel to the mysterious entity, offering to return the Orb. Across the Axiom Constellation, every major faction panicked—boosting military budgets and increasing troop recruitment fivefold.

Accompanied by Saifuru and Mao, SumireV traveled at lightspeed to the besieged Empire sector to meet the entity face-to-face. Arriving amidst burning ruins—fortunately sparing The Empire's capital city—SumireV dispatched her aid crew to rescue trapped and wounded Empire soldiers while she stepped forward to confront the swirling cloud of Dark Smoke.

"Hey! I have your Orb!" SumireV called out. "Take it and leave this universe!"

From the darkness emerged thousands of warships led by a sharp-eyed commander. "Well, hello there, SumireV. I am Xaria, Admiral of the First Fleet. We hail from Universe-17—our faction, Colossal Verdict, specializes in overwhelming conventional forces."

"I am SumireV, Leader of Interstellar Chronicle in Universe-26. We don't want trouble. Here—take your Orb." She tossed it forward.

Xaria caught it smoothly. "This Orb is called Ars—a beacon tracker we deploy before invading new universes. Still, you seem unusually strong. Tell me—what are you?"

"The Protector of Humanity," SumireV replied evenly. "If you want to cause trouble here, I'll show you the full might of a Protector—though it's better for everyone if we don't."

Xaria smiled wickedly. "Oh~ I wonder what that looks like..." Instantly, countless more warships and legions materialized behind her out of the dark void.

SumireV's eyes blazed bright white. "Well, if you insist." With a single gesture, she manifested an entire armada matching Xaria's numbers ship for ship, flanked by massive Energy Beacons ready to draw natural energy.

Xaria's eyes widened in sudden terror as ancient nightmares flashed through her mind. She remembered Lorenzo Winchester—a Core of Absolute wielder from an old era who had obliterated multiple universes ruled by Colossal Verdict in a single week, forcing their empire to hide in terror for 30,000 years. And now, the instant they stepped out again, they had walked straight into another one.

"ALL UNITS FALL BACK!" Xaria shrieked, panic shattering her composure. "WE ARE NOT FIGHTING THIS MULTIVERSAL THREAT!"

Retreating frantically back to her headquarters, Xaria burst before her Supreme Leader: "AVOID UNIVERSE-26! A Core of Absolute user is there! Those maniacs are multiversal weapons of mass destruction—DO NOT ENGAGE!" Without hesitation, the Supreme Leader wiped Universe-26 off their conquest charts and blacklisted it permanently.

Left hovering in empty space, SumireV tilted her head. "Hmm... strange. Better report this back to Command." Watching every trace of Dark Smoke vanish across Universe-26, she immediately turned back to help Saifuru and Mao pull wounded Empire soldiers out of the wreckage. "Move as fast as we can! We only have five minutes before The Empire's main armada arrives—we need to stay off their radar."

After rescuing at least 15,000 surviving Empire soldiers, SumireV and her fleet jumped straight to XERT-07L to inspect the damage. Repairs were already nearly done, and General Rahim rushed out to greet her.

"SumireV! Welcome back to XERT. Thankfully, the entity only struck the outer fringes of the solar system, though losses were still steep—45,517 killed and 117,000 wounded out of our 690,000-strong garrison here."

"Don't worry—I dealt with them," SumireV assured him. "She was Xaria of Colossal Verdict from Universe-17. We nearly fought, then she suddenly pulled a full retreat. Turns out that Orb was just an invasion tracker—no wonder it matched their dark matter color. We also rescued some Empire soldiers caught in her attack."

Rahim blinked. "Wait—why help The Empire?! We're at war with them! They attacked MiRi during the Orb extraction, and just days ago they ambushed one of our supply ships—killing 60 of my soldiers using that new M-GenMu tech!"

"I know," SumireV said calmly. "But they were unarmed and injured. Remember our creed—we don't kill unarmed soldiers showing no hostility. We're Protectors of Humanity, aren't we?"

"Right—nearly forgot that rule," Rahim nodded before pulling up a fresh tactical file. "Anyway, I've got crucial intel for you. Look at this new Pivot Federation weapon called Silent Penetration. When fired, the projectile vanishes mid-trajectory as if slipping into another dimension—then reappears point-blank moving fast enough to punch straight through the hardest alloys in their sector. They tested it in a skirmish on one of our frontier planets weeks ago, and it tore right through our nanosuits."

SumireV studied the schematics and nodded thoughtfully. "Got it. Looks like I need to plant spies inside the Pivot Federation. Need anything else before I head back?"

"No, ma'am—your aid already saved us."

Meanwhile, far off in Order of North territory, an Interstellar Chronicle defense garrison was being overrun by the Northern Cavalry Battalion.

"Fix those comms and hail the nearest IC outpost for backup!" Commander Dakari shouted over incoming fire. "Comms are dead and the evacuation port's been captured—we're completely outnumbered!"

Artillery shells slammed down mercilessly from an Order of North mothership looming overhead. Major General Nikolai's voice boomed from its loudspeakers: "You cannot win this war! We sent our finest army and have you surrounded! Evacuation is impossible, your communications are severed—it's time to end this and get off our LAND!"

"Send out a manual distress beacon!" Dakari ordered.

"Sir! That'll attract unknown hostiles!" the radioman protested.

"That's an ORDER, son!"

The distress signal shot out into deep space, picked up almost immediately by a passing fleet. Surrounded on all sides as their battle suits ran out of power under relentless shelling, Dakari's troops braced for the end—until thunderous artillery suddenly tore straight into the Order of North's flank. Looking up, IC troops saw ships belonging to the Tectonic Pact.

"Get aboard my ship—we're allies!" Captain Russell hailed them. "I already contacted a nearby IC outpost, and they're about to drop a Bio-Bomb right here, so move fast!"

Clutching his bleeding thigh as Tectonic medics rushed out to stabilize his wounded men, Dakari ordered everyone aboard. As they lifted off, Order of North anti-ship lasers raked across their hulls—only to bounce off harmlessly, since Tectonic ships weren't built of steel, but of Vicious Earth, a rare mineral found solely in Tectonic space capable of shrugging off lasers (though still vulnerable to explosives).

Down below, a red missile slammed into the battlefield and burst into thick crimson mist.

"Oh... they f*cked up," Dakari muttered. "That's a bioweapon."

"Wait—IC actually builds those despite the Government Authority ban?" Russell asked in surprise.

"Banned, sure, though we don't answer to them—and Pivot Federation has them too, probably Imperial Phalanx as well."

Across the valley, Order of North soldiers screamed in panic: "BIO ATTACK! BIO ATTACK! Those fukers didn't hesitate!"* As monstrous failed experiments clawed their way out of the red mist and threw the Northern Cavalry into chaos, Dakari checked his ship's roster: only 61 soldiers aboard his vessel, with roughly 100 or more scattered across the other evacuation ships—a heavy loss, though the survivors safely broke orbit toward the Pantera Solar System to report to SumireV.

Chapter 19 - Transition

Inside the Pantera Sector, SumireV waited for Interstellar Chronicle's key officers. Within minutes, everyone had gathered: MiRi, Dr. Pedro, Nagasawa, Dr. Arc, Iflaren, Dakari, Sky, Rahim, and the rest of the senior command.

"It's time for us to relocate to Sector C," SumireV announced. "It sits further away from Orion and The Hunt, though it puts us closer to Imperial Phalanx, Cluster Creed, and—most importantly—Parliament Abstract and the Pivot Federation. Both of those factions have fought us in the past, so stay sharp. Fortunately, Cluster Creed and Imperial Phalanx remain neutral toward us for now. Over the next three days, prepare all of Pantera for Lightspeed Travel. As for our outposts and sub-bases in other territories, they will remain in place. Dakari, your case is different since we lost our foothold in Order of North—we don't know what new weapons they might develop next. As for me, I won't be joining any major wars or skirmishes for a while. It's time to focus on technological development, so I'll be staying right here in Pantera alongside our finest scientists."

Meanwhile, inside Pantera's training simulator room, a sprawling virtual military base was instantly wiped off the map by "Impulse—Supercharged!"—a crushing shockwave strong enough to evaporate air molecules.

"Nice destructive force, Crown," Klein grinned. "Trying to turn into Pain from Naruto?"

"I wish I could, though mine's still limited," Crown replied. "I can push, just not pull. How are things looking on your end?"

Klein demonstrated her ability, Synthetic-Mirage (Plastic Manipulation)—conjuring an entire fleet of warships firing barrages of missiles and shells strong enough to flatten coastal fortifications, before sweeping the virtual city under a molten tide of liquid plastic. Crown stepped deliberately into the dead center; just before the molten flood reached him, he unleashed "Concentrated Frontal Shock," blasting the entire surging wave straight back down the avenue in a razor-clean line.

"Trying to imitate SumireV's Creation power, huh?" Crown teased.

"At least I can build a fleet—even if it isn't a full armada like SumireV's," Klein smirked.

Suddenly, Astra walked into the room. "We need both of you. Your abilities fit an upcoming operation."

Locking in immediately, they followed Astra down the corridor toward Meeting Room 3 while she briefed them. "We have an operation to steal intelligence from the Pivot Federation—they supposedly developed new tech meant to counter IC. Worse, we learned the Government Authority has built at least two Artificial Celestials rivaling Low-Tier Cosmic Celestials. At minimum, I can kill a Sentinel or Low Chronos, but a Celestial... that makes even me think twice."

Entering Meeting Room 3, they received a full briefing from Dakari. Their objective was straightforward: rescue any stranded IC soldiers or civilians they encountered and extract classified intel from the Pivot Federation National Guard Research Facility.

"Keep in mind, the Pivot Federation despises IC," Dakari warned. "Most of our forces evacuated their space earlier because of their relentless aggression, overwhelming bombardments, and insane logistics—fresh units arrive almost every second and adapt quickly to our tactics. Still, we won't go in empty-handed: we're deploying our Mythril Beast Fleet."

Boarded onto a Lightspeed Travel rocket alongside 200,000 troops, Klein, Crown, and Astra dropped into IC-held territory inside Pivot Federation space—where nearby factions like The Empire and Cluster Creed also housed covert IC outposts capable of assisting with evacuations or covering retreats with biochemical strikes. Commander Jun Wei welcomed them, briefing them on enemy fortifications, possible flanking routes, and mission odds.

Shortly afterward, confirmation arrived that IC's stronghold in the Pantera Solar System had successfully initiated Lightspeed Travel to safer space.

"There they go," Crown remarked. "We're on our own now—well, mostly. Let's get Operation Barracuda done." They set up a temporary forward camp to rest and organize.

Elsewhere, inside the halls of the Celestial Council, panic stirred.

"SumireV... the 301st wielder of the Core of Absolute," a Celestial Guardian warned. "A direct clash with her carries a 50/50 risk of total erasure. Unlike us, she has a human foundation—her mind holds unlimited potential paired with cosmic abilities, and she improves after every single fight. I suggest sealing her rather than engaging head-on!"

"I'm not sure sealing will work easily," an Omnipotence Celestial countered. "She killed our colleague Arbiter-11 when we sent her to ambush SumireV—we later learned SumireV erased Arbiter-11 point-blank inside her Dimension using Singularity Rebuild."

"ENOUGH!" the Overseer snapped. "Stop admiring that mortal! We must seal both her and her entire faction! We'll pool our greatest energy—exhaust them first while they're weakened, then seal her away!"

The Celestials exchanged grim glances, knowing it was their only viable play. Just then, another battered Celestial stumbled into the chamber.

"I'm back," he panted. "YOU GUYS DIDN'T TELL ME SHE WAS THAT STRONG! I tried one-shotting her with a Universal Beam and she TANKED it inside her Infinity Barrier! Before I could follow up, she dropped a black hole to slow me down and tried killing me with Anti-Celestial Missiles! How did she even build those?! I thought they only existed in ANOTHER universe! I seriously regret going to A0D4."

Unease rippled through the council. "Maybe we should offer IC a peace treaty instead..." the Celestial Gateway Guard murmured. "Have you forgotten previous-era wielders? Zimmerman, the 41st user... Ethan, the 262nd, who wielded 10th-Level Core of Absolute... and worst of all, Valket, who literally split three universes with his 9th-Level strike! Sure, Valket became a Sentinel, though he started out human! They may be ancient history, yet SumireV likely has access to every past user's knowledge—that makes her terrifyingly dangerous."

"NO!" the Right-Wing Celestial Guard insisted. "We follow our orders—seal SumireV at all costs! Stay sharp and charge up; our best opening will come when IC is locked in another war. Besides, SumireV is still only at the 3rd Level, isn't she?"

"Silence," spoke one of the Ancient Celestials quietly. "I crossed paths with her 90 years ago when she was merely at the 2nd Level. She was weaker then, yes—yet already a genius. Never forget: the Core of Absolute belongs to the Chosen One. I nearly killed her, only for her to trap me inside her Dimension of Logic. As she hurled everything she had at me, I barely survived by triggering my second life—sacrificing over half my power just to escape. SumireV is brilliant, battle-hardened, and endlessly versatile. You must expect the unexpected."

Chapter 20 - Wall of Fortress

Reaching the frontline, Crown and Klein stared up in awe at a colossal fortress wall towering 20 kilometers into the sky, bristling with anti-invasion railguns and layered defenses—with dead IC soldiers scattered across the approach.

"They built this wall in less than a week," Astra muttered. "Their logistics are insane. They haven't even brought out their true elites yet, though their robots alone are tough enough to challenge IC super-soldiers."

"Our objective is just pushing past this wall to reach the Research Facility a few kilometers behind it, right?" Klein asked. "Why not just fly straight over it?"

"Bad idea," Astra shook her head. "Fly over, and their elites will jump us in mid-air and overwhelm us."

Just then, four gigantic canisters dropped into their camp alongside a message from SumireV: "I've authorized deployment of Dr. Edward's Chemical Acid Attack. It'll corrode their defenses—use it wisely."

"Alright, here's the plan," Dakari ordered. "First, soften them with a lighter, heavy-volume barrage, then switch to super-heavy bombardment, release the chemical cloud, and send in the Mythril Beast Fleet—those cosmic monsters will absorb incoming fire and hurl it right back. FIRE!"

Rockets, lasers, and shells slammed into the towering wall, followed closely by earth-shaking heavy artillery and spreading acid fumes. Almost immediately, the sky turned bright orange as long-range Pivot Federation batteries returned fire—only for the Mythril Beasts to step forward, soak up the explosions, and redirect the force straight into the wall while IC infantry and armor divisions surged behind them.

"RELEASE THE CANISTERS!" Dakari roared.

Acid clouds washed over the fortifications, eating away alloy plating and slaughtering defenders. Overhead, Crown and Klein intercepted PF anti-materiel shells to shield IC artillery crews. Fully charged, the Mythril Beasts unleashed a blinding torrent of absorbed energy onto a corroded section of the wall, melting straight through as IC warships battered enemy defenders with concussive barrages.

"CHARGE!" Dakari yelled.

Crown surged forward, unleashing a massive Impulse Force blast that cleared a 30-kilometer stretch in front of them. Behind him, Klein manifested legions of carbon-fiber tanks, artillery pieces, and APCs to punch through the breach while Crown recharged. Pivot Federation robots flooded out to meet IC's vanguard in fierce close combat.

"Crown, we need to split up!" Klein called out, deploying even more synthetic units equipped with massive speakers blaring psychological broadcasts telling PF soldiers the war was hopeless and to go home to their families. "I'll hold the frontline and keep them distracted—you get inside that wall and knock out their defenses!"

On another flank, Astra danced through high-grade defenders, tearing localized reality apart with glowing strings.

Suddenly, Dakari felt a spike of energy overhead—dozens of incoming Nuclear Bombs plunging toward their lines. Instantly raising towering deep-forest trees to catch the warheads mid-air, Dakari absorbed what he could, though the resulting 60-megaton TNT blast still wounded or killed scores of IC soldiers (save elemental users who threw up shields in time). The Mythril Beasts absorbed the shockwave and blasted it right back against the wall. Dakari immediately hailed orbit, bringing in 20,000 fresh warship reinforcements.

Inside the fortress, Crown fought corridor by corridor, firing a specialized revolver loaded with Pulsar rounds capable of blowing soldiers apart on impact. Searching frantically as Klein warned over comms that casualties were mounting, Crown realized the fortress was too massive to locate the Control Room in time—until he stumbled straight into the Thermal Energy Room, packed with Micro Suns and Nuclear Reactors.

"Klein," Crown radioed, drawing the room's colossal energy directly into his reserves, "I'm going to blow this whole wall sky-high—hopefully it takes the Control Room down with it!"

Overhearing the plan, Dakari ordered all batteries to concentrate fire on the center. Before Crown could detonate, an enemy elite burst into the chamber: "There you are, Mr. Crown!"

Before the elite could swing his stun-sword, Crown unleashed a 150-megaton TNT shockwave. A massive crater blew open across the center of the wall, though the structure refused to collapse entirely.

"CONCENTRATE ALL FIRE ON THE BREACH!" Dakari barked. IC artillery hammered the weakened gap with cluster bombs, determined to break through.

Just as victory felt within reach, a massive enemy fleet warped in—spearheaded by Captain Raul, the Lord of Blizzard.

"So this is the IC army," Raul smirked, instantly plunging the battlefield into sub-zero fury. Crown blasted the freezing air back with a hyper-shockwave while Klein scrambled to hold off a fresh wave of PF robots and special forces. Dakari bombarded PF ranks with Pollinated Bloom, sapping enemy energy and siphoning it back to IC troops, while Astra deflected colossal railgun beams and stalled incoming ships.

"Let's dance—this is my first real deployment," Crown grinned, raising his anti-life handcannon at Raul.

Raul raised a glacier wall; Crown blew straight through it—only to find Raul gone. In a flash, razor-sharp scalpel icicles sliced Crown's arm clean off. "The wall was a distraction," Raul murmured as ice spears closed in from every angle. Crown detonated an Impulse Force wave, shattering the spears and hurling Raul backward.

"Damn right—that shockwave confirms how you stunned a Sentinel," Raul muttered, impressed. "IC truly lives up to its reputation."

Recharging off his Mini Nuclear Reactors, Crown charged back in, unloading his handcannon while Raul deflected bullets and conjured a roaring Ice Dragon. As the dragon unleashed freezing breath, Crown rushed point-blank and fired a Hyper-Charge Shockwave, blowing the dragon and blizzard apart.

Raul instantly closed into melee range, blitzing Crown before he could finish recharging. Forced onto the ropes, Crown activated Serum Phaser VII Stage 5 paired with Vulcan-E2, unleashing a Wall of Flame Destroyer. Raul's ice shields began melting fast as Dakari's Pollinated Bloom sapped his reserves dry.

"Artillery strike on our own position! All units fall back and regroup!" Raul barked.

A Pivot Federation mega-ship overhead rained indiscriminate laser fire down on both IC and PF soldiers alike. While IC elites and Mythril Beasts shielded their troops during the enemy's reload, Astra tore open a portal summoning IC warships to bombard the retreating PF lines—only for massive PF reinforcements to warp in, doubling their numbers and wiping out Dakari's Pollinated Bloom.

Knowing Dakari would need time to redeploy the blooms, Crown poured every last ounce of his reserves into one final strike. "Impulse Force—Lethal Dose!"

A hyper-critical frontal blast tore outward across a 200-kilometer radius, disintegrating 70% of the Pivot Federation army in an instant.

"NOOOO!" Raul roared as the smoke cleared to reveal fully charged Mythril Beasts and IC warships priming their beams. Covered by Raul's shattered vanguard, the Pivot Federation sounded a full strategic retreat. Dakari ordered non-stop rear artillery shelling to prevent them from ever rebuilding defenses while Klein blasted relentless demoralization broadcasts across the ruins.

Chapter 21 - The Worst Yet To Come

Once past the breached wall, IC established a forward operating base and turned the fortress's surviving guns inland. Even from their camp, soldiers could hear the distant rumble of heavy machinery echoing over the hills.

While Crown slept deeply to recover from overexertion, Klein asked Dakari, "So, Commander—what's our next move?"

"Intelligence warns the Pivot Federation plans to use human shields next, sacrificing civilians so they can blame IC and provoke other factions into declaring war on us," Dakari explained grimly. "Their main reinforcements from their homeworld arrive in five days, with smaller fleets arriving in one or two days from neighboring systems. Right across those hills sit three million enemy troops, bolstered today by another 1.2 million fresh soldiers and 440,211 abnormals—elemental users.

"Fortunately, SumireV just sent us four Armor Divisions featuring Dreadnoughts and ICADT-XIIs—Interstellar Chronicle Anti-Defense Tank Generation 12, engineered personally by SumireV based on our PF intel. She also cleared us to use Global Abomination orbital cluster strikes as soon as we knock out their ground-to-space guns. We have 600,000 soldiers right now, and Prometheus—a natural-born Gravity Manipulator implanted with Prometheus Modulation by SumireV—is inbound with reinforcements."

"What are our options, Dakari?" Astra asked. "Brute-force our way to the facility, or send a strike team to knock out those anti-orbital guns so Global Abomination can clear the sky? Either way won't be cheap—only four of us Elites are here right now."

"Relax, Astra," Dakari smirked. "They have no idea what's about to hit them."

IC kept PF pinned under an endless four-phase bombardment cycle: ground artillery, long-range rear heavy artillery, anti-invasion railgun barrages, and orbital spaceship strikes—with Klein filling every reload gap alongside hourly releases of VX Nerve Agent along the ground. Even so, PF poured 15,000 replacement troops into the meat grinder every thirty minutes.

Thirteen hours later, the reinforcements arrived: Dreadnoughts—legendary warships forged across 82 wars, firing specialized compound warheads that detonated once, pulled surrounding mass inward, and detonated a second time four times larger with inextinguishable Voltrauma (lightning-frostbite) flames jumping from target to target—alongside ICADT-XII tanks equipped with sonic/EMP missile deflectors, Arcadian Energy main cannons capable of dissolving metal on contact, four rotating-barrel Super Heavy Machine Guns (firing incendiary and semi-armor-piercing rounds), Thermobaric Drones, and Velocirax-Nyxvirus-Delayed Action shells that caused peeling skin necrosis before turning victims three days later. Three mega-carriers followed, launching Space-Fighters to stun and disable PF robots.

"Now we're talking," Dakari grinned. "We're up to 820,991 soldiers waiting on Prometheus and extra Mini-Nuclear Reactor extenders for Crown. Odds of victory sit at 90%—unless their main reinforcements show up early, dropping us to 45%. We need to end this fast."

Under relentless shelling, most PF defenders suffered permanent hearing loss as casualties climbed to 200,000 soldiers every hour.

"Where the hell are our MAIN reinforcements?!" Raul raged beneath darkened skies swarming with IC Space-Fighter Jets, Kamikaze Drones, Battleships, and Heavy Bombers dropping Delayed-Action Nyxvirus payloads.

IC rolled forward in a strict five-tier formation:

1. Vanguard: Mythril Beast Fleet.

2. Second Line: Mechatronic War-Mechs (four stories tall—scaling up to 60 meters against titans) and ICADT-XII Armor.

3. Third Line: Elite Units (Serum Phaser VII Stage 4 and Vulcan-E2 masters, led by Klein and Dakari).

4. Fourth Line: Mobile Artillery, advancing 20 meters behind every push.

5. Rear Line: Heavy Artillery and Warships managed by rear command.

As PF battleships joined the fray and enemy troops deployed Silent Penetration rifles capable of piercing IC armor, Dakari summoned ancient wooden beasts and spread Pollinated Bloom across the field. Approaching a dangerous mountain pass primed for an artificial landslide while IC warships and heavy artillery were still recharging, Dakari enacted an emergency tactic: Deep Rainforest Emergence. Thick, sprawling jungle erupted across the entire combat zone—creating ambush cover while draining PF energy reactors dry so IC's main force could catch its breath.

Meanwhile, slipping deep behind enemy lines, Astra and Crown spotted one of PF's three anti-orbital guns (while Dakari's troops assaulted Site C, Astra and Crown crept toward Site A).

"Use your handcannon," Astra urged.

"You sure? It's only meant for one-shotting living targets."

"Don't worry—I'll amplify the shot. Just aim."

Channeling her Core of Retribution—an ancient Celestial-tier Core—Astra empowered the barrel.

"Wait... you have a Core just like SumireV?!" Crown gasped.

"For a long time—older than SumireV's, in fact. Hers is perfection; mine focuses strictly on fracturing reality. Now shut up and shoot!"

Crown pulled the trigger—obliterating a 75-kilometer corridor straight through the Orbit Gun. Unbeknownst to the 50 fresh Pivot Federation battalions marching up nearby, Crown and Astra were already operating deep inside their rear lines.

Chapter 22 - History Repeating

Inside a Pivot Federation staging camp, a general addressed veterans recalled from 45 years earlier.

"Why send us to fight IC again?" one veteran protested. "We fought them 45 years ago and lost! We can't beat them unless—"

Using his Pressure Ability, the general crushed the man's skull instantly. "I'm sick of hearing your mouths. Get out there and defend this land!"

Soldiers were strapped with 20-kiloton TNT packs designed to detonate automatically upon death. Out of transport trucks, troops marched across the mountain ridge—passing an armored train packed wall-to-wall with mutilated corpses and spilling intestines.

"We're the 400th division replacing the last wave," a PF captain muttered. "Command's thrown 700 divisions into this meat grinder today—22,000 divisions total, over 80 million dead."

Roughly 621,000 veterans—including Henry, William, Jackson, and Boris—marched forward under fatalistic dread.

"Just like 45 years ago, Henry..." William sighed. "Our government doesn't give a damn about us."

Suddenly, a blinding yellow flash and deafening roar erupted as 5,000 IC shells rained straight onto the column. Boris prayed for his daughter before impact; beside them, a veteran whose lower half was blown clean off shrieked until a medic froze his waist and slapped mechanical prosthetics onto him so he could keep walking pain-free. Already two-thirds of the unit lay dead, with 120,000 fresh replacements marching right behind them. Crossing the burning forest below—where two Orbital Guns already lay crippled—they climbed down into blood-soaked trenches and activated explosive combat gloves capable of punching through armor.

Before they could charge out, a lone IC soldier leaped straight into their trench and began tearing troops cleanly in half—fusing Vulcan-E1 Wind blasts with hardened Nanosuit arm-cannons and a flesh-eating blade—until Henry and his surviving squadmates surrounded him and pierced his heart.

Marching through dense woods carpeted in torn organs and trench ladders slick with human flesh, silence suddenly fell as PF launched Anti-Matter Scatter missiles and nuclear rockets directly onto IC's line. A blinding blue plasma wave vaporized the entire forest back into the earth. Lighting up the dark with flares, PF forces spotted IC units only briefly stunned—Klein dragging an unconscious Dakari to safety—before the Mythril Beasts surged forward again, absorbing artillery and vomiting back molten lava, beams, and freezing breath.

Chaos swallowed Henry's senses. ICADT-XII tanks dissolved PF armor; legless IC Mechatronics still dragged soldiers close to rip their spines out through their throats; a 14-year-old PF conscript was shredded by spinning blades; a single IC super-soldier dropped a vacuum bomb that imploded nine PF elemental squads into atoms; Space-Fighter jets wiped out 5,000 advancing troops with corrosive bombs; and a fresh battalion descending the mountain vanished into red mist and scattered boots.

"I WANT TO GO HOME! I NEED TO SEE MY FAMILY!" Henry screamed, bolting toward the edge of the battlefield. East lay blocked by mobile artillery fire; West choked under Velocirax-Nyxvirus-Delayed Action mist, leaving soldiers bleeding from their ears and eyes as necrotic fingernails dropped off. Overhead, Klein's towering speakers boomed across the valley: "YOU CANNOT WIN THIS WAR! THE PIVOT FEDERATION IS SURROUNDED BY RADIANT GUARD, THE GOVERNMENT AUTHORITY, AND NOW INTERSTELLAR CHRONICLE! ABANDON ALL HOPE AND GO BACK TO YOUR FAMILIES!" Watching PF aircraft get shredded by anti-air fire while insane, bomb-strapped suicide troops missed blindly in the dark, Henry realized everything was lost.

Slipping over a quiet hill, Henry played dead among corpses—only to hear footsteps and realize Jackson, Malingo, and five other comrades had followed him out of the trenches. Hiding overnight, they woke the next morning to find IC rear artillery units already surrounding their ridge.

Astra strolled over, eyeing them. "So, deserters?" Wrapping a hand around Henry's throat, she murmured, "I could snap your neck in three seconds... psych, just kidding!" Letting go, she stepped back as Klein crossed her arms.

"Relax, boys," Klein smiled. "We take prisoners—we don't execute unarmed troops who stop fighting. Welcome to a one-way trip to our prison planet!"

Arriving at Homes of Ignorant, the terrified deserters were greeted by human-like AIs—and stared in disbelief at Captain Stward, officially listed as KIA 45 years ago, casually sipping coffee outside a sleek cafe.

"Captain Stward?!" Henry gasped.

"Hah! Look who finally got caught by IC too!" Stward grinned. "Grab a seat—life's way better here than back home!"

Stward explained the rules quickly: zero brute force, zero IC guards onsite—just AIs running society. Prisoners lived freely, earning Deco Currency (by creating art or goods sold to IC planets) and raising Credit Scores to upgrade from Free Motels to luxury apartments or mansions like Stward's. The only catch: everyone wore an explosive neck collar, a planet-wide destruct failsafe sat waiting if anyone rebelled, and leaving the planet wasn't allowed—though after serving set years, prisoners could choose release or bring their families to settle permanently.

Kicked outside by Stward so he could spend time with his AI companion, Henry and his baffled friends shrugged and headed toward their Free Motel to start earning Deco.

Chapter 23 - The Split Second

At the Pivot Federation Research Facility, alarms blared everywhere as IC troops fought room-to-room inside the complex while Crown and Klein pounded outer defenses. Yet the strain was catching up fast: Mythril Beasts, armor divisions, and Mechatronics lay battered and drained; Dakari's Pollinated Bloom struggled to siphon enough energy; Klein had dropped from manifesting fleets to firing standard guns; and Crown was down to his handcannon.

Astra crashed down from orbit, her skin badly scorched. "Sh*t—PF deployed a Herald! Stronger reinforcements are already here, and their main armada isn't even on-site yet! Where the hell is Prometheus?!"

In the distance, PF flooded the field with Dyfra-O6—a thick gray chemical smoke blinding soldiers beyond two meters while melting internal organs like the liver. IC suit filters held, yet coated soldiers glowed brightly as targets for PF Anti-Invasion Cannons, while Raul's elites and venom-mutated tank-sized berserkers pushed IC back through sheer suicidal numbers.

Suddenly, a deafening groan tore the gray smoke apart as a city-sized Black Hole opened right above the battlefield.

"Yep—that's him! Prometheus is HERE!" Crown cheered.

Mistaking the gravity master for SumireV, the PF General threw everything at him. Prometheus crushed vast swaths of terrain while redirecting Global Abomination tungsten-rod cluster bombs straight onto PF elite reinforcements—straining hard to keep the black hole stable.

Racing inside the eerily empty Research Facility, Crown and Dakari retrieved the classified intel from the storage vault. Sensing something terribly wrong, Crown blew an exit hole through the wall with Impulse Force—just as the facility overloaded its Super Anti-Matter Cell in a desperate self-destruct trap.

Grabbing Dakari and dragging every IC soldier within reach clear, Crown watched the blast vaporize half the planet's oxygen, flatten the landscape, and wipe out every PF troop alongside many IC units and elites.

Waking amid ash and wreckage, Crown tracked his GPS 20 kilometers out to regroup with Astra, Klein, Dakari, and Prometheus.

"They tried taking us all down with them," Dakari reported grimly. "We lost 520,195 soldiers out of 820,991—mostly frontline troops. Please tell me you still have the intel, Crown."

Crown nodded and held up the data drive. Without another word, all surviving IC forces withdrew from PF space.

Aboard the approaching Pivot Federation Main Reinforcement Armada, a furious Major raged at a hologram of the Ministry of Defense. "I CAN'T BELIEVE YOU KILLED OUR OWN MEN JUST TO STOP IC! Look at Raul—gravely injured, four percent odds of making it!"

"I don't care about anyone's life!" the Ministry snapped back. "Those cockroaches aren't worth as much as our research! Hopefully that blast wiped out IC and destroyed the intel!"

Before the commanders finished arguing, an emergency directive flashed from the Pivot Federation government: Dispatch 40% of total manpower—50 Billion soldiers—immediately to Radiant Guard space to assist The Empire in breaching the Wall of Alcatraz (failed 10+ times). Failure to comply carries execution.

Back at New Pantera, Dakari delivered the report in the control room. SumireV silently honored the fallen—520,195 IC dead, 14,000 aircraft, 8,000 tanks, and 2 Dreadnoughts lost, against 14 billion PF casualties—before dismissing her battered elites to recover.

Chapter 24 - Rules and Justice

Two weeks after the Chroni-Federal War, tensions boiled over inside the Government Authority's Intergalactic Meeting Center.

Pivot Federation delegate Dr. Washington fiercely condemned IC's assault and WMD usage. The GAU presented footage of A0D4's 90 trillion casualties; Order of North, The Empire, and Radiant Guard furiously demanded explanations for undercover IC populations hiding as civilians inside their borders.

SumireV answered coolly. "We place operatives there to prevent surprise attacks using hidden weapons—just like what happened a century ago. It's a precaution to minimize casualties." Orion sat silently in its seat, submitting only brief logs of their clash with IC.

GAU Marshall Chimera slammed a gavel and projected an updated galactic war map:

Major Wars: Pivot Federation & The Empire vs. GAU; PF & The Empire vs. Radiant Guard; The Hunt vs. Bureau Coalition; GAU vs. The Hunt; GAU vs. IC; Order of North vs. IC.

Minor Wars: IC vs. The Empire; IC vs. The Hunt; Orion-TW3 vs. IC; Tectonic Pact vs. Order of North; Radiant Guard vs. IC.

Near Outbreak: Orion-TW3 vs. GAU; Imperial Phalanx vs. The Empire; PF vs. Parliament Abstract; IC/Bureau Coalition/Tectonic Pact vs. Orion-TW3 (unclear).

Official Alliances:

Zenith Triarch: Interstellar Chronicle, Tectonic Pact, Bureau Coalition.

The Attrition Titan: The Empire, Pivot Federation (Imperial Phalanx and Cluster Creed pending).

Supreme Administrative Bloc: Government Authority of the Universe, Parliament Abstract, The Commonwealth.

The Polar Starborne Alliance: Order of North, Parliament Abstract, Sovereign of Eclipse.

Major Shifts: Imperial Phalanx won its internal civil war, surging to superpower status by converting whole planets into mobile war fortresses. Cluster Creed cracked a rapid process converting elite soldiers into Sentinels and Archons far faster than the GAU.

Dead Zone: The Southeast sector—devastated by a clash between a Nexus and a Celestial—now teemed with attack-immune Cosmic Monsters.

When the GAU called a vote to outlaw IC and PF bioweapon production, nearly everyone agreed except PF and IC.

"I don't accept your ruling," SumireV declared flatly. "Interstellar Chronicle is an independent faction—we don't answer to the Government Authority. Step foot inside my territory, and we will bring the entirety of our might to bear against you."

Even after Pivot Federation broadcast gruesome footage of IC acid gas, bioweapons, blood-mist bombardments, Global Abomination strikes, and troops ripped apart by IC soldiers, SumireV didn't flinch. "What did you expect when fortifying against IC? Besides, we use methods that kill fast and painlessly. This is war, not a game—and we remain neutral unless provoked."

Suddenly, the Orion delegate stood up angrily. "We want to join Interstellar Chronicle's alliance! We've had enough of the Government Authority—they betrayed us straight into IC's counteroffensive, costing us most of our fleet!"

"You can't do that—we have treaties!" Chimera barked.

"Enough of your bullsh*t! Why else would you stage 50 billion soldiers on our border?! Preparing another massive invasion?!"

Furious, the GAU representative drew a sidearm and fired at Orion—only for SumireV to drop the bullet straight to the floor with a flick of gravity.

"Stop," SumireV warned coldly. "You aren't in control here, Government Authority. You may hold the number-one military, yet you don't rule everyone." Promising to review Orion's request, SumireV watched the summit dissolve under suffocating hostility.

Returning to New Pantera, SumireV reviewed her war machines, realizing IC would be endangered if she ever fell—meaning stepping back from combat to push IC's tech two or three steps ahead of everyone else was vital. Dr. Arc reassured her that her leadership ensured loyalty unlike the old Intellectual Chronicle.

Just then, an approaching Orion warship tripped alarms. Lowering planetary defenses while placing ground troops on standby, SumireV flew straight up and ripped the ship's hatch off barehanded. Inside stood Capt. Cromwell, an envoy carrying Orion-TW3's official request to join the Zenith Triarch—noting their close bond with Tectonic Pact and shared philosophy of building human armies capable of challenging Cosmic entities through Artificial Heralds. Taking the treaty papers and promising an answer soon, SumireV reconstructed the ship's door seamlessly with Creation power and sent them home via Lightspeed Portation.

Descending deep underground beneath one of Pantera's worlds, SumireV checked on Project Zealous—a colossal Behemoth Space Battleship forged from Bureau Coalition alloys:

Main Armaments: 400 Plasma-Charge Energy Cannons, 80 Nuclear Rocket Silos, 6 massive Railguns (20 MT TNT each), and a heavy artillery battery firing airburst cluster munitions or Velocirax-Nyxvirus canisters.

Defense & Point-Defense: Anti-materiel hull plating, an energy shield on a 50-second cooldown, 600 Heavy Machine Guns (30mm), and 300 hypersonic Anti-Air Flak cannons firing 15-meter Titanium nets that shredded anything airborne.

Special Weapons: A massive bow-mounted Laser Cannon built to cripple motherships, a ventral flamethrower fueled by a Fallen Archon Core unleashing surface-melting Judgment Flame, and a Prometheus Modulation Prototype Machine projecting stabilizing gravitational shockwaves.

Power Core: Driven by 4 Mega Nuclear Fission Reactor Capsules, 4 Fallen Herald Cores mounted at the corners, and a Concentrated Dark Energy Chamber that grew deadlier the longer the ship fought—engineered to detonate upon total failure with enough force to wipe out half a planet.

Simulator-tested and ready, Project Zealous stood waiting as a brutal deterrent against any superpower foolish enough to provoke IC.

Chapter 25 - Engine of Destruction

Working in her office, SumireV analyzed captured Orion-TW3 and Pivot Federation hardware. Most proved outdated compared to IC—except Orion's Transversal Tech, which briefly slowed localized time for five seconds before aircraft dropped ordnance, pushing strike accuracy to 95%. She immediately forwarded the schematics to MiRi for mass integration into IC space fighters.

Flying to Planet Voltana in the New Pantera System, she checked on Dr. Pedro, who was attempting to install Voltrauma inside Vulcan-E2—tricky work since Saifuru was the only living Voltrauma user, though prototype results looked promising. Next, she visited Dr. Zack to order integrating Orion's Transversal Tech with AI targeting across all Divine Bullet GS-1 and GS-2 missiles, sending Zack scrambling to mobilize his entire lab immediately.

Stopping by Mecha Planet, SumireV congratulated MiRi, whose Aerodynamic Department had upgraded IC fighters to hypersonic speeds wrapped in Hyper-Hardened alloys (resistant to AA, though vulnerable to cosmic blasts) alongside Automatic Replacement bomb bays—allowing pilots to toggle 3D-printed payloads mid-flight between Gas, Incendiary, Explosive, and Energy munitions.

At the Bio-Chemical Department, Dr. Edward unveiled his latest breakthrough: Velocirax-Nyxvirus C (Cosmic). While milder against conventional soldiers, it heavily crippled Cosmic Entities—dropping their power by 60 to 70% (though Sentinels and higher resisted longer; effective for five minutes in atmosphere and three in space before requiring reapplication, plenty to let IC elites slay Heralds). Edward was also adapting Iflaren's Vile Venom serum to mass-produce inside IC battlesuits, boosting muscle density and lacing punches with acid. SumireV approved both projects: "Anything for IC's survival, in case I don't make it."

Teleporting to the Energy Research Department, SumireV broke up a heated collar-grabbing brawl between Commander Dakari and a scientist over integrating Pollinated Bloom directly into IC battlesuits after seeing soldiers run low on energy at the PF facility. Once the scientist realized dual inner-and-outer Micro Cells could absorb, convert, and store energy just like Mini-Nuclear Reactors, SumireV greenlit the upgrade immediately.

Retreating into a secret vault beneath the IC stronghold, SumireV examined Serum Phaser-IX—upgraded to Stage 11 (+920% general enhancement, compared to Phaser-VII's Stage 7 at +500%). Two weeks of testing now allowed ordinary IC troops to safely reach Phaser-VII Stage 6 without corruption, while Ampasy's modified blood could handle Serum-ESD up to Stage 9. Only SumireV could touch Stage 11—which killed her instantly, though the Core of Absolute restored her on the spot.

Scrolling through the computer registry, she reviewed IC's hidden Core users—most still dormant, save Saifuru and Astra:

Saifuru – Core of Haste

Mao – Core of Serenity

Iflaren – Core of Devouring

Crown – Core of Domination

Astra – Core of Retribution

Devin – Core of Reality

SumireV – Core of Absolute (unlike natural Cores born in-universe, hers was forged eons ago by Type-8 Civilization tech—making her its 301st user)

She also reviewed IC's last-resort PROTOCOLS:

Protocol 7: Full war mobilization against a faction.

Protocol 3: Releases Velocirax-Nyxvirus over a 30-kilometer radius (100% infection ground zero) if IC is losing while SumireV is comatose.

Protocol 11: Deploys IC's Cosmic-Grade fighters (Mikhail, Saifuru, Ampasy) capable of killing Archons/Sentinels and stalling low-tier Nexus.

Protocol 1: All-hands deployment against extra-universal invaders.

PROTOCOL-APOCALYPSE: Total annihilation contingency if IC falls. Grants every citizen Corruption (+700% from Serum Phaser-VII and +300% from Corruption) amplified by Mao—making even the weakest IC citizen Archon-tier—while awakening and fusing all IC Core users temporarily with SumireV into omnipotence, sacrificing all of IC to leave Universe-26 uninhabitable and impassable to enemies.

Summoning all elites and scientists to Hangar-B, SumireV unveiled the Interstellar Chronicle Golden Memory Archive—a pocket dimension she invented before ever gaining the Core of Absolute, preserving over 500 years of her combat memories viewable from every angle so every department could reverse-engineer enemy tech.

"For Interstellar Chronicle and humanity!" SumireV cheered.

"FOR INTERSTELLAR CHRONICLE AND HUMANITY!" the entire hangar roared in unison.

Meanwhile, inside Parliament Abstract, Sergeant Howell and Lieutenant Oswald finalized plans for a three-pronged ambush against the Tectonic Pact—hitting alongside the Government Authority (West) and Order of North (North) before TP could summon Orion or IC. Over on the GAU front, Major Potter unleashed an Anti-Matter barrage while Admiral Blackbear snapped: "Release the Artificial Celestial units! I don't care if they're unstable!"

Chapter 26 - Uncertain Tide

As the dust settled from the Anti-Matter missile barrage, Order of North deployed 1 Million Cavalry straight into the breach—only to slam headfirst into heavy machine-gun fire and artillery from blue-glowing IC battalions already stationed inside Tectonic Pact territory.

On the western front, the GAU unleashed 20 low-grade Artificial Celestials. Painfully unstable, many couldn't even fly or grasp their own powers—only four functioned operationally, and just two remained stable—yet backed by 50 Armor Divisions and 30 Enhanced Infantry Divisions, GAU paratroopers quickly crippled TP's railgun defenses. Suddenly, blinding light swallowed the forts as towering 5-to-8-meter warriors stepped out wielding waraxes, broadswords, maces, and deflectors: Orion-TW3's First and Third Shock Battalions, swatting GAU lasers right back as deafening sonic booms.

Over in the east, Parliament Abstract deployed 500,000 Cryo troops and 2,500 aircraft firing freezing rounds onto TP's weakest flank—until a figure radiating cosmic steam stepped into their path: Ampasy, Elite Unit Team One, tapping Serum Phaser VII Stage 6 for the first time. Clapping his hands to flatten a four-kilometer stretch ahead of him, Ampasy tore straight through frozen ranks, hoisted a 1,100-meter mountain overhead, and slammed it onto PA's lines.

"How unfortunate for us to run straight into IC monsters," Sergeant Howell muttered grimly.

Elsewhere, inside Cluster Creed space, citizens froze in terror as the sun abruptly vanished beneath a massive, unannounced IC armada—its flagship pulsing sonar shockwaves every five seconds, bristling with anti-war-machine tech as covert IC outposts across multiple factions quietly mobilized. Down in Riverfall City, a dying TP soldier spat blood at celebrating PA troops, warning them that once IC arrived, none of them would survive to laugh.

Hearing reports that IC's Zeroth-grade Elites had entered the war, GAU, PA, and Order of North scrambled reinforcements—only for GAU outposts and intercept fleets along the route to be shredded by IC's flagship, which absorbed incoming fire and returned deadlier lightning blasts; even Sentinels only dented turrets briefly before automated repairs sealed the hulls.

Outside the TP solar system, GAU and Order of North massed 4 Million warships to block IC's armada—only for IC to slip straight through a portal, emerge behind their lines firing rear batteries, and press right onto the main planet.

Landing onto the surface, IC unleashed Saifuru, Iflaren, and Mao against Order of North's 1 Billion Cavalry and Parliament Abstract's legions. Iflaren's Vile Venom melted organic and metallic troops alike into liquid; Saifuru tore through Heralds like paper at 520,000 km/h with Voltrauma; and Ampasy rampaged joyfully across enemy ranks ("Uncle A"). Up in deep space, Sentinel-tier Telekinesis Master Mikhail and Prometheus double-teamed a GAU Tier-0 Celestial—Prometheus locking the Celestial inside an Earth-sized Quasi Black Hole ("Have you seen stars crumble?") while Mikhail pinned the Celestial's hands with telekinesis, chuckling that Prometheus was copying SumireV's signature catchphrase.

Suddenly, colossal speakers echoed across the entire solar system:

"This is SumireV, Sovereign of Interstellar Chronicle. Fighting IC is futile—the war is already lost. Withdraw your forces—GAU, PA, and Order of North—or face the entirety of IC's mighty torrent of fire and iron. If we cannot win here, we will strike your homeworlds and families instead. Look how desperate the GAU is, throwing untested prototypes into battle. All IC—BEGIN THE REAPING!"

Orion flooded 1 Trillion soldiers into the TP theater to block incoming enemy reinforcements while IC systematically swept planet after planet (refraining from Velocirax-Nyxvirus on allied TP soil). Enemy casualties soared past 6 Billion against 2 Billion allied losses (mostly TP), culminating in Orion officially signing into IC's alliance.

Watching quietly from her flagship ("Celestials are watching my every move"), SumireV summoned a Temporal Black Hole and hurled it straight into a Pluto-sized GAU fleet, instantly erasing the entire formation along with 5 Million troops. Down on the ground, horrified GAU recruits stared into empty space—wishing they had been drafted to the Pivot Federation or Empire warfronts instead.

Chapter 27 - Unreasonable Risk

Following SumireV's broadcast, a challenge arrived directly from a Government Authority General demanding a duel.

SumireV arrived on the largest planet inside Tectonic Pact space. Interstellar Chronicle, Orion, and the Tectonic Pact lined up on the left; the Government Authority, Parliament Abstract, and Order of North stood on the right. Stepping forward stoically to meet General Victor, SumireV spoke evenly.

"Let's do this."

General Victor lunged immediately with a heavy chainsaw, trying to slice SumireV apart. Evading cleanly, SumireV manifested a scythe using the Core of Absolute and swung back—managing to damage his battle suit.

"Damn experienced," Victor muttered.

Switching to Vulcan-E2's lightning speed, SumireV blurred so rapidly she looked as if she were teleporting. Victor countered with a Magnet ability that made SumireV heavy and unbalanced, only for SumireV to neutralize the pull with Gravity. Firing EMP cluster rockets from his battle suit, Victor pressed forward, though SumireV blocked the blasts behind a solid Glacier Wall.

Victor kept forcing hand-to-hand combat while SumireV parried and evaded every swing. Trying to freeze time with a GAU-replicated Core of Time, Victor watched his spell collide into SumireV's Quasi Black Hole and Clock Lock—the opposing temporal forces canceling out into neutral nothingness. Firing a laser beam next, Victor swore as SumireV used Core of Absolute Creation to raise a Mirror and bounce it straight back.

"You think you're cool? Bi*ch!" Victor snapped, unleashing Metal Manipulation to stab at SumireV from every angle, only for the blades to melt instantly inside an Inferno Fire bubble.

"Water—heavy tsunami-grade Geostorm!" Victor roared, summoning an oceanic wall.

"Vulcan-E2: Ice and Wind—blizzard storming at supersonic speed!" SumireV countered, flash-freezing the entire tsunami solid.

Charging straight toward her using electricity for speed and stun, Victor swung in—only for SumireV to coat herself in Vulcan-E2 Rubber, leaving her completely immune to electric shock. Frustrated, Victor plunged into fierce melee combat while SumireV waited patiently for a clean opening.

Swinging her scythe downward, SumireV let Victor catch and pin the blade to the earth before driving a spear into her side. Victor smiled, thinking he had won.

"Look at the scythe," SumireV said calmly.

Looking down, Victor realized it wasn't a scythe anymore—it was the Hammer of Void, activating a Black Hole right in front of him! Anticipating the trick, Victor smashed a GAU capsule that instantly disabled every attack and cleared all energy within a 5-meter radius—only for SumireV to conjure conventional artillery and fire point-blank, wounding Victor with physical shells.

Victor gritted his teeth. "You're strong and so brilliant—I truly feel challenged. I used to be a champion in my faction, yet fighting you is like fighting an entire system. Still, if I can kill you, that makes me the strongest."

SumireV smiled faintly. "I like how your ego takes over—keep it that way. Also, I won't even need galactic-scale attacks at this rate."

Drawing his pistol, Victor opened fire; SumireV dropped every bullet straight to the dirt with gravity. Soaring skyward, Victor conjured a miniature Rigel star and hurled it down at her. SumireV raised a Prometheus Modulation Black Hole (PMBH).

"Not even light itself can escape this," she murmured as the star vanished inside.

Drawing a broadsword, Victor clashed blades in close quarters while SumireV formed a whip and lashed him across the face mid-dodge.

"What the fu*k—this is so itchy!" Victor snarled as SumireV kept cycling effortlessly between itchy, burning, and razor-sharp whips. Switching next to a plasma blade on her right arm and a shield on her left, she drove Victor entirely onto the defensive, carving small cuts with every evade until close combat became unbearable.

Enraged, Victor raised a shock hammer and smashed the ground so violently the hammer itself shattered—yet SumireV stood completely unfazed behind an aerodynamic tungsten wedge that split the shockwave cleanly around her. Too late, she caught onto his distraction: using temporary 30-second GAU gravity tech, Victor had dragged a planetoid as large as a Moon straight down from orbit! Manifesting an Orbital Annihilation cannon designed to shatter worlds, SumireV blew the moon apart into a raining meteor shower—only to spot Victor concealing himself among the falling rocks.

"Old trick—only dumb people fall for that," SumireV muttered. "Guess I'll destroy all those stones too." Manifesting a Tsar Bomb via her Core, she launched it skyward into a blinding detonation.

Crashing down bleeding, Victor pulled out a vial of poison and jammed it into his neck—mutating himself into something less than human.

"Sacrificing your intellect just for this fight is a grave mistake," SumireV warned coldly.

"You aren't my mother trying to lecture me!" Victor roared.

Stomping her foot onto the earth, SumireV sent crystal spikes erupting straight through him before manifesting artillery batteries and charging through the shelling to pour Molten Steel over his body. Victor lunged frantically to crush her head, only for SumireV to crush his hand with gravity and call down Lightning Overload—a concentrated thunderstorm paralyzing the poison inside his veins.

Spotting his hidden weakness—black pus oozing behind his knee—SumireV bound his limbs with manifested chains and a giant earth golem before driving a neutralizer straight into his weak spot. Crippled and dying, Victor collapsed.

"That's why you should think before acting," SumireV said sternly, looking down at him. "Look at you—dying now. You were genuinely strong, though honestly there were five hundred faster ways I could have killed you; I only gave you a chance so I could study you. Either way, I'll finish you so you can't come back for revenge." Gripping him firmly, she boosted her strength with Serum Phaser VII Phase 7, drove her thumb straight into his eye, and split him cleanly in half.

Turning toward the remaining enemy ranks, her voice rang out harshly: "Parliament Abstract, the Government Authority, and Order of North will pay for every bit of damage done here—since attacking Tectonic Pact was your decision. Now go tell your leaders!"

Enemy troops scattered instantly in panicked teleports and frantic flights. Sitting down with a quiet sigh, SumireV shook her head. "That guy was tough, though brainwashed by their propaganda. He never even realized I can heal almost instantly."

Saifuru walked over beside her. "What's your plan now? The GAU won't attack us anytime soon since they're still locked in war with the Pivot Federation and The Empire."

"We turn our attention to the third layer: Radiant Guard," SumireV replied. "They're built differently—the kind of faction that forces enemies to fight on their terms, and they withstand our damage abnormally well. We need research on them, and I've sensed energy matching theirs before back in another realm." Together, IC and Orion set to work rescuing survivors and rebuilding the scarred Tectonic Pact worlds.

Back inside her office in New Pantera, SumireV immersed herself in abnormal and spiritual research, convinced of a deeper link to Radiant Guard. She remembered how IC had once attacked a planet called Living Death—whose inhabitants bypassed IC armor entirely to strike mentally and spiritually—prompting her to study every holy scripture across known religions just to be thorough.

"Radiant Guard doesn't fight in the traditional way," SumireV mused aloud. "They use guns and melee weapons, sure, yet their primary edge is casting magic orbs from their hands—using energy completely unlike the Natural Energy I wield. I call it Magical Dust. Under Core Vision, I can see them converting Natural Energy into their own energy—Mana—which we can't tap into. Think about it, Sumi... in all of IC, only two people can use magic. The Wall of Alcatraz is built differently too—Pivot Federation and The Empire failed ten assaults against it, and behind that wall sits an impossibly strong defense, possibly hiding even more unknown factions. Only one faction holds a clue: Sovereign of Eclipse, who are economically allied with Radiant Guard using mixed tech. Still, I should head to Earth 2 first to study spiritual powers—if I find a lead, even better. And I'll need backup."

Halting minor skirmishes against The Empire, Radiant Guard, and GAU, SumireV ordered covert IC outposts inside those factions relocated and hidden. Heading down to Hangar-D alongside Ampasy, Devin, and Crown—with Nagasawa and Dr. Zack standing by on IC Intergalactic Comms—she turned to Devin.

"Devin, you told us about a genius who thinks almost the same way I do, except leaning more toward Magic and Spirits. Can you take us there?"

Devin nodded. "Yes, boss. His name is Codling—he's my friend from Earth 2. I can show you the way."

Naming the squad Team-Expedition Realm (T-ER), SumireV had Devin teleport them straight into Codling's apartment on Earth 2—a room crammed wall-to-wall with research notes and Interstellar Chronicle emblems.

"Cody, I brought our boss, SumireV—the Leader of IC," Devin introduced. "She needs you."
