Adjusting his glasses hastily, Codling bowed. "Oh, hello, SumireV! It's a pleasure to meet you—I'm Codling."

Shaking his hand, SumireV studied him for a moment. "I'm SumireV. You seem brilliant—by my estimate, you have a 225 base IQ. Why haven't you joined IC?"

Codling blinked in confusion. "Join IC? I've been trying to figure out how! Earth 2 doesn't have space tech, and Devin never told me—he just said IC was full."

SumireV shook her head dryly. "No, you're in IC starting now—welcome aboard. Back to the main topic: tell us everything about Magic, Spirits, and how they tie into Radiant Guard."

Codling shifted into a serious tone. "Magic operates on a completely different layer from Tech, Conventional, or Cosmic power. IC and other factions use technology to mimic elemental strikes, whereas true Magic draws on Magic Dust—it doesn't use Natural Energy and doesn't support it. It runs on Mana, and from what I've uncovered, Radiant Guard possesses tech capable of converting Natural Energy into Mana. Purely logical, physical tech from factions like ours can't wield Mana without proper training. Worse, Magic attacks injure you physically while also tampering with your soul and mind, accelerating corruption.

"Aside from Magic Dust, there is Spirituelle Pythagoras—which only has three alignments: Holy, Darkness, and Neutral. Holy counters Darkness, Darkness counters Holy, and Neutral harms both at half potency. Spirituelle Pythagoras strikes Mortal and Immortal souls directly; once your soul is destroyed, you lose everything. As for the connection to Radiant Guard: my research proves Radiant Guard uses a hidden Gate to enter a Spirituelle Pythagoras realm called the Honduran Path, where Natural Energy barely exists—almost everything inside is Magic Dust and Neutral Spirituelle Pythagoras. That Gate lies inside the realm, and to reach it we have to go to the Antares Deep Jungle right here on Earth 2."

"What's your own power?" SumireV asked. "Can you survive in there?"

"I have an ability called Petrification," Codling replied. "As long as I look directly at an enemy's body, I turn them to stone—though I'm still bound by human limits."

Using her Creation ability, SumireV manifested a portable battlesuit fabricator, equipped Codling with an IC Battlesuit, and dissolved the fabricator back into energy.

"That settles it. Let's go, team—Cody's coming with us. Devin, open the portal to Antares Jungle."

Arriving amid the sprawling, ancient jungle canopy, Codling pointed ahead. "Follow me."

Chapter 28 - New Opening Realm

Uncovering the invisible gate, SumireV and her team stepped inside into a dread-soaked, unfamiliar dimension. Thick mist blanketed the frozen earth beneath an existing yet dimmed sun. Checking her sensors, SumireV saw Natural Energy sat at roughly 2%—everything else registered as unknown.

"Don't worry—that's Radiant Guard's Mana," Codling warned. "I assume they've built defenses in here."

Crown tried lifting off, only to realize suit recharge rates crawled at 0.2% per second without sufficient Natural Energy.

"We'll have to rely purely on Nuclear Reactor energy and backup batteries for attacks—and mostly stick to physical melee," SumireV instructed.

Hailing Nagasawa over comms—"Come in, this is SumireV, we've entered the Honduran Path realm inside Antares Jungle"—she received only static and buzzing back from Nagasawa due to the starved Natural Energy. Before they could advance further, Radiant Guard soldiers ambushed them out of the fog, pinning Crown down.

"ENGAGE THEM!" SumireV shouted. Leaping onto one soldier, she drove a waraxe straight through his skull. Codling blocked incoming RG Magic Bullets behind a nanotech shield while Devin opened a portal unleashing a Cyber-Tiger to tear into enemies. Ampasy tore troops apart barehanded—requiring slightly more effort to strip their vests first—while Crown carved clear with a lightsaber before blasting attackers back with a short Impulse Force burst, leaving Ampasy to stomp knocked enemies flat.

Checking everyone over, only minor scratches showed—until a thunderous warning echoed across the valley: "Breach! An unknown hostile has entered our territory! All nearby units proceed to Area 22 to neutralize!"

"Move out—we can't stay here," SumireV ordered.

Reaching a mountainside overlooking Area 22, they watched RG aircraft sweeping the sector below. Both SumireV and Codling immediately reached the same conclusion.

"To defeat an enemy this strong," Codling murmured, "you take out whatever keeps them running."

"Energy—their Reactor," SumireV finished. "Just like IC relies on Energy Beacons to convert Natural Energy."

Moving across grim hills piled with unburied corpses from past wars against Radiant Guard—mostly Pivot Federation and Empire troops alongside scattered IC and GAU dead—they suddenly dove beneath heavy RG artillery fire, sheltered under a thick sheet of Titanium manifested by SumireV. Before they could catch their breath, at least 50 Radiant Guard soldiers descended, conjuring glowing Magic Orbs in their palms to hurl at T-ER.

"Just like I predicted!" Codling called out. "The bigger the orb they cast, the stronger and higher their rank! SumireV, target their Captain!"

Manifesting a laser cannon, SumireV fired—an enemy stretched a Magic Orb wide into a thin shield, yet the laser punched straight through anyway, killing two soldiers. When the RG Captain hurled the shield toward SumireV, Crown batted it aside with Impulse Force.

"Tsk! Not bad... as expected from IC," the Captain sneered. "A hero, a destroyer—and a danger to nature itself."

Conjuring eight massive Magic Orbs, the Captain unleashed a barrage straight at T-ER while SumireV intercepted them with eight manifested missiles. Codling laid down heavy machine-gun cover while Devin rode the Cyber-Tiger firing quad suit cannons. Meanwhile, Crown and Ampasy held back 2,000 incoming RG reinforcements—Ampasy tearing through ranks on Serum Phaser Stage 5 and pure physical Percentile Strength while Crown rationed energy, splitting enemies with his Anti-Living-Things Handcannon and saving 10-to-50-meter Impulse Force bursts for tight spots.

Rising into mid-air to duel the Captain directly, SumireV's eyes blazed white at full Creation output. Encasing herself inside a protective energy dome—fighting off suit Natural Energy and backup batteries while refusing to risk touching her TON 618-level Core Reserves—she bombarded the Captain with manifested artillery, lasers, rockets, battleships, swords, daggers, axes, scythes, and hammers all at once.

"Fu*king cheating—what IS this?!" the Captain screamed. "How do you have so much energy?! Only a handful of IC units should pull this off! Pivot Federation sent five divisions here and lost!"

"This is IC, not PF. Idiot," SumireV smiled coldly.

Desperate, the Captain manifested a colossal Magic Bomb and detonated it directly on SumireV—only for her artillery to detonate the orb mid-air while a giant manifested hand burst out of the smoke and snatched the dazed Captain clean out of the sky.

"You lost," SumireV said evenly. "Tell me where your energy reactor is—fast, not slow. Either way, we'll find it sooner or later."

"Fuk you, SumireV! Bich who*e—my friends will kill yo—"

"Sorry, mate. Have to kill you," SumireV sighed, closing the giant hand until he burst into pulpy blood.

Dropping back down beside her panting team while the Cyber-Tiger finished off the fallen corpses, SumireV left behind 25 energy-constructed battleships and dozens of illusory tanks as decoys before leading T-ER onward to locate the Energy Converter.

Spotting a fortified military camp ahead, SumireV switched tactics. "Stealth this time—handle it in your own stealth styles." Everyone engaged cloaking fields (two minutes invisible, one-minute cooldown, though shadows remained visible).

Crown and Ampasy hit the perimeter and mortar pits: Crown dropped silently from above with Impulse Disintegration, wiping everything inside 25 meters, while Ampasy silently seized two guards by the skulls and smashed their heads together at Mortar Position A. Meanwhile, Devin and Codling slipped through four barracks blocks—Codling cutting down sentries cleanly with a laser knife while Devin dispatched sleeping troops with oversized shears.

Inside the command interior, SumireV faced elite guards capable of sensing her presence—crushing their heads silently with localized gravity and twisting a close sentry's neck like opening a bottle cap. Slipping into the armory, she encountered a Heavy immune to both gravity and telekinesis; locking his feet in place with Vulcan-E2 Crystal, she invoked "Core of Absolute, Dimensional ability: De-Construction," erasing him down to sub-atomic dust. When a second Heavy burst into the room aiming at her, SumireV leaped smoothly to pin him flat and manifested a man-sized saw to split him cleanly in half.

Continuing deeper under invisibility, she spotted an enemy platoon of 71 troops being briefed by a Sergeant. Slipping behind a cargo container, she silently flooded the floor beneath their boots with Vulcan-E2 water, coated her own legs in rubber, and discharged lightning—electrocuting the entire platoon silently in one stroke. Reaching the Control Room last, she dropped a Black Hole to erase the casting RG officers before hailing T-ER to rendezvous. Reviewing the base maps, they locked onto their target: the City of Spectre.

Chapter 29 - Guilty And Hero

Inside the Sovereign of Eclipse—also called the Sovereign of Sentinels, where 29% of the population were born natural Sentinels—crowds gathered on May 13 to honor their greatest heroes against Interstellar Chronicle.

"Citizens of the Sovereign of Eclipse!" the opening speaker declared across the grand plaza. "We gather today to remember our great war against Interstellar Chronicle! Two hundred fifty years ago, Sovereign of Eclipse, The Commonwealth, Cluster Creed, and a portion of Orion-TW3 formed a mighty coalition called the Sigil Compact, led by The Commonwealth! For thirteen years the war raged—deploying the Big Seven Sentinels, backed by 50 Archons and 130 Heralds from our closest allies. Sadly, outcomes turned grim: SumireV survived into a coma, and IC's contingency bioweapon shattered our coalition—leaving us with staggering losses while SumireV lived on."

As the speech ended, a towering curtain parted to unveil an endless memorial wall listing over 50 billion dead from Sovereign of Eclipse alone. Crowds cheered as Support Lines marched past smiling and waving, followed by proud Rear-Echelon forces—before falling into suffocating, haunted silence as Frontline Survivors shuffled past, men whose souls had died on the battlefield 250 years ago, most tying blindfolds tightly across their eyes so they wouldn't have to face reality, twitching uncontrollably every few seconds.

"Those back there... are the ones who faced SumireV's battalion," someone whispered. Even when reporters offered millions just to hear their stories, veterans refused to speak of what happened 250 years earlier.

Finally, an aged Sentinel hero named Avalon accepted the microphone as crowds showered money onto the stage. Looking out with eyes devoid of all shine, Avalon spoke slowly and steadily.

"I am Avalon. Four days before the Big Seven Sentinels deployed, I was sent to fight SumireV's battalion. We led the assault with 50 Divisions drawn from Commonwealth, Orion, and Sovereign of Eclipse—over 90,000 Cosmic beings, mostly Heralds and several Sentinels, rushing straight at their defensive line.

"Then I heard a shrill whistle overhead—and there stood SumireV, landing a few hundred meters ahead of us, drenched in blood and burns from earlier fronts. Staring straight into my soul, she said, 'We, Interstellar Chronicle, welcome you with open arms—dedicated by me, Leader of Interstellar Chronicle.'

"Suddenly, hundreds of thousands of orange flashes erupted behind her. Within the blink of an eye, she created an entire arsenal out of pure energy matching our numbers—mowing us down and obliterating most of our forces, treating Heralds like toys. She manifested motherships and full armadas from raw energy, backed by divisions of 60-meter Mechatronic Titans tough enough to tank Herald blasts alongside endless APCs—spending zero real resources, fighting completely alone!

"Thirteen hours into battle, Cluster Creed gambled on our strongest Sentinel-and-Archon strike unit, claiming SumireV had to be exhausted after days of fighting. We tried blitzing her—yet she fought strictly on defense without much pressure, needing only one clean opening to kill instantly, shattering Archons and tearing Sentinels apart while I kept asking myself: What kind of human fights Cosmic beings like this?!

"Keeping her pinned down, we unleashed our final attack—Lunar of Finality—sacrificing the casters holding her inside the beam just to destroy her. When the glare cleared, SumireV lay mangled and bloodied in the dirt. Then I blinked... and she stood fully healed, eyes blazing red!

"Rising into the sky, she said, 'You are strong—good coalition, smart tactics, I admit it. Yet despite being Sentinels and Cosmic beings, I have one question for all of you: have you ever seen stars shattered?'

"Knowing doom was here, I poured every last drop of energy into speed and bolted straight off the planet into deep space—just as I heard her invoke, 'Core of Absolute: First Level Neutronium Stars, and Second Level Sagittarius A*.' Time dilated violently around me. Looking back, I watched a Supermassive Black Hole swallow four entire Commonwealth planets whole—everyone I knew dead. My brother, Goran, died right before my eyes, screaming as he fought hopelessly against the Singularity's infinite density..."

Stopping mid-sentence, Avalon dropped the microphone and stumbled quietly offstage, sobbing. Everyone in the crowd knew the horrors that had followed.

Five days after Avalon's defeat, Cluster Creed urged striking IC immediately, though The Commonwealth insisted on five days of preparation to assemble 3 trillion Conventional Coalition Forces (CCF) bound for Umbara alongside 30,000 Elite Cosmic Division (CCD) fighters—led by the Big Seven Sentinels: Stunner, Immobilizer, Reflector, Blinder, Absorber, Striker, and Buffer—intending to kill SumireV first so she couldn't relieve Umbara.

Yet SumireV was always prepared. Reaching the ruins of a freshly wiped Cluster Creed defensive line, CCD watched a battered SumireV walk out—instantly heal—and declare, "This is either my final fight or a new beginning." Casting a Quasi Black Hole that swallowed half the planet and wiped out most of CCD, she rained thousands of laser cannons down on the survivors.

The Big Seven launched their synchronized rotation: Buffer supercharged all six teammates; Blinder blinded her; Stunner shocked her nerves; Immobilizer locked her down; Absorber and Reflector negated counterattacks; and Striker delivered crushing blows.

Coughing blood, SumireV blanketed the field in a Vulcan-E1 solar storm so thick they could rely only on energy sensing. Leaping at Buffer with her Lycan Axe Splitter, she baited Immobilizer into pinning a mere hologram decoy while slicing Buffer cleanly in half—though his energy surged immediately into the remaining six! Blinded and hurled straight into a Sun by Reflector, SumireV lost half her jaw, healed instantly ("Fuk, nice move—let's see how well you adapt!"*), and blazed red-eyed with Universal Pull—blocking Striker's earth-shattering punch with Infinity Barrier before scattering them inside flaming ash and a Jupiter Storm. When Reflector cleared the storm, they found Blinder's body crushed inside a Black Hole!

Straining rapidly, SumireV tapped Serum Phaser-VII Phase 5 and two Mini Nuclear Reactors, saturating the five survivors behind a four-armada barrage before surrounding them with nine Radical Collapses. Racing at lightspeed to destroy a decoy orb, Striker left the real Radical Collapse to maim Reflector—before SumireV shoved Reflector straight inside her Dimension of Logic, erasing his existence so his energy couldn't pass to the others!

Enraged, Absorber disintegrated himself to empower Immobilizer, Striker, and Stunner, tearing SumireV's arms away under relentless Beam Blitzes until she burned her final two Mini Nuclear Reactors, her Core Absolute Reserve, and hidden deep-space Energy Beacons.

Holding out her palm, SumireV spoke evenly: "This is my final gamble—and by my calculations, enough to kill all of you. Risky, yet as long as I live, IC won't fall. Core Absolute, Ability Creation: 9th Solemnity—Singularity Engine Replica! Paradise will kneel before me! Good thing blurred Core memories let me understand this, even at thirty percent output. The difference between you Cosmic beings and me is simple: you're driven by ego, while I run on logic and abstract thinking. How does it feel to be defeated by a human?"

"No—impossible!" Immobilizer gasped, recognizing the legendary 9th key from millennial multiversal wars.

"Axiom Mindstorm!" A massive dome erupted, siphoning energy directly into SumireV. As Stunner and Immobilizer clawed at her hands and Striker lunged for her head, SumireV unleashed "Barrage of Fracture"—gravity-charged lightning shredding all three—followed by "Endless Cognition Fall," raining starships, trains, tanks, and warships across the dome before executing Striker with Pseudo Black Hole Acceleration Destruction. As the last two tried overloading themselves in a suicidal charge, SumireV froze the dome under "Time Dilation Replication" and crushed them inside Artificial Singularity Rebuild—before collapsing into her 10-year coma while the 3-trillion coalition marched on Umbara, shattered twenty days later by IC's Velocirax-Nyxvirus outbreak.

Back in the present near the Radiant Guard border, a Sovereign of Eclipse civilian watched in awe as a massive 500-billion-soldier invasion force from the Pivot Federation and The Empire pounded away unsuccessfully against the Wall of Alcatraz for the eleventh time, distant flashes flickering across Cluster Creed and Order of North.

Chapter 30 - City of Spectre

SumireV checked on Team-Expedition Realm (T-ER) as they caught their breath. "What's everyone's status?"

"We're fine—just low on energy," Devin and Codling reported.

"Something feels off, though," Ampasy added uneasily, Crown nodding beside him. "We keep hearing demonic whispers in our ears, like something's trying to mess with our heads."

SumireV recognized the effect of remaining inside the Honduran Path, and Codling confirmed it. "Even though this place is saturated with Magic Dust, it isn't actually Radiant Guard territory—just a forgotten realm they captured to use as a gateway. Because it's a forgotten realm, it slowly eats at our spirits and minds."

SumireV hesitated briefly before deciding. "Let's rest here on the mountain first. I'll place eight energy beacons for us to draw from later—take time to recover before the next stage." Deploying sixteen more energy beacons around their camp, she knew the fight ahead would be brutal, though the objective stayed simple: reach the Mana Converter and destroy it.

Six hours later, after drawing energy from the beacons, the team gathered closely to hear SumireV's plan, her voice firm and face dead serious.

"By now, Radiant Guard already has reinforcements inside the city and knows we're coming," she explained. "The real question is how we break through. First, I'll cause massive distraction-scale destruction outside—enough damage to make them think an entire army is laying siege to the city. Codling and Devin make the best pair for infiltration: Codling navigates while Devin teleports inside. Ampasy and Crown specialize in destruction and brute force, so you two know your jobs—Crown hits strategic points with Impulse Force while Ampasy hunts their elites. Don't forget, Ampasy: your blood handles Serum Phaser VII Phase 6 to 7 without tearing up your organs, and you can push Phase 8 if needed, though it isn't a great idea. We hit fast—I'll pin down their main army outside, and anyone who slips past becomes Ampasy and Crown's problem. Move out."

Launching the assault outside the City of Spectre, SumireV conjured a massive illusory fleet and army blaring loudspeakers: "This is Interstellar Chronicle! We are here to defeat Radiant Guard! Our entire force has been deployed—you cannot win this fight!" She pounded the outer defenses with artillery barrages while conjuring SR-71s overhead to shatter defenders' focus with deafening sonic booms.

Inside the sprawling energy facility, Devin and Codling kept teleporting straight into wrong rooms.

"Man, I've never been in here—where the heck is the generator room?!" Devin groaned.

"Forget it—let's hit the Control Room instead," Codling urged. "Shutting it down from there saves your energy, and we can't waste any." Together they darted corridor by corridor searching for the control deck.

Out in the city streets, Ampasy tore through Radiant Guard squads, punching straight through chests and hurling bodies across avenues. "Crown, do it, man—detonate that nuclear-ahh Impulse Force!"

Leaping straight off the tallest skyscraper, Crown plunged down into an airburst: "IMPULSE FORCE—OMEGA BURST!" A colossal shockwave swept across a 50-kilometer radius—not lethal to everyone, yet strong enough to leave entire garrisons dizzy and stumbling.

Word spread fast across the Honduran Path; Radiant Guard forces outside the realm poured straight through the gates alongside nearby bases converging on the City of Spectre. Drawing all local city defenders toward herself outside the perimeter, SumireV glanced at her Natural Energy gauge—down to 22%, leaving only Batteries and Mini-Nuclear Reactors (MNRs).

"Devin, hurry up and kill their power so we can finish them easily—and grab any classified documents you find!" she shouted over comms. Defeating the frontline surge outside, she smoothly reabsorbed her offensive constructs back into a tight defensive line. Meanwhile, Crown demolished the underground metro to carve entry deeper inside while Ampasy slaughtered RG elites and Herald-class officers—reminding himself aloud: "To beat a Herald, kill him before he charges enough energy for a strike strong enough to hurt us."

Overhead, house- and truck-sized Magic Orbs screamed toward SumireV's line. She instantly vaporized them using Heatwave Cannons—right before massive cosmic juggernauts and mystical dragons breathing laser fire burst from the smoke, prompting her to rain heavy artillery that dropped dozens at once.

Suddenly, Crown's voice crackled urgently over comms: "Enemy paratroopers dropping from orbit—Elite Units!"

Crown fired off Impulse Force mid-air to scatter most of the drop, though roughly 50 heavily armed soldiers slammed onto the streets, firing exotic rounds that knocked targets back meters per shot. Stepping out of the clearing dust, Ampasy's veins pulsed violently as his hair flared with crackling blue-and-yellow bio-electricity from Percentile Strength. Grabbing one soldier's skull and crushing it flat, he bounded straight across the squad—shrugging off heavy impacts to tear straight through enchanted vests and flesh—growling fiercely as even more elites dropped around him.

Down inside the facility, Devin and Codling frantically beamed stolen machinery straight back through portals while Codling guarded the hallway behind his battlesuit's HMG and hardened shield.

"YO, DEVIN! HOW MUCH LONGER?!" Codling barked over gunfire. "SumireV said DISABLE the Mana Converter, NOT rob the whole building!"

"Shut up—I'm locked in! I already shut it down, AND Sumi needed this machinery! Hold them off a bit longer!"

Groaning in exasperation, Codling switched to Vulcan-E1 and flooded the entire corridor with molten lava and fire, sealing the enemy advance cold.

Hearing their comms, SumireV expended her final battery on a massive gravity strike. "Core of Absolute: Tectonic Pull—UNLEASH!" Hurling a dense, black-hole-like dark sphere skyward, she dragged chunks of earth and entire enemy regiments upward into a crushing suspended sphere, reabsorbed her defensive line into raw energy, and planted a manifested nuclear warhead right on top of the floating orb.

Inside the city, watching 30 enemy Archons descend through the smoke, Crown saw no other option. "Impulse Force—Anti-Attritions!" A devastating 25-megaton TNT blast knocked several Archons crashing downward and stunned the rest—right before Crown passed out cold from exhaustion, allowing Devin to snatch him safely through a portal straight back to Earth 02.

Still brawling swarms of elites alone, Ampasy roared as he engaged Serum Phaser VII Phase 6 paired with Serum Phaser ESD at 300% bloodstream sync: "Percentile Strength, bear with me!" Bounding from target to target, he tore off heads, ripped a soldier's spine straight out through his throat to swing as a jagged spiked mace against Magic Orbs, and snarled through bleeding wounds: "YOU ALL THINK YOU CAN WIN?! You stacked Magic Defense, well I pack PHYSICAL FORCE!" Nearly collapsing beneath overlapping blasts, he dropped backward—caught cleanly in mid-air by SumireV, who flung him straight inside her Dimension of Logic.

Turning to face a converging tempest of lasers, Magic Orbs, and missiles, SumireV raised a hand. "Let's see if I can mimic you, Crown. Core Absolute Gravity Ability—Outward PUSH!" Blasting a five-kilometer radius clear of buildings and incoming fire, she slipped neatly inside her Dimension to heal Ampasy's shredded cells with onboard restoration tech—right as the suspended Tectonic Pull dissolved overhead, slamming crushed earth back down moments before the nuclear bomb detonated above the ruins. Everything fell silent, ensuring fast, low-suffering annihilation for the greater good.

Returning safely to Hangar-D back on New Pantera—where Nagasawa and MiRi rushed to assist as Natural Energy surged back into her veins—SumireV found Crown, Devin, and Codling already waiting safely. Walking over to Codling, she offered an approving nod.

"You did well, Cody. Practice mastering your Petrify skill—it has serious potential. Feel free to explore New Pantera—welcome to IC, son." Signaling Devin to escort Codling to his quarters (while Devin noted the stolen Radiant Guard machinery sat secured in Emergency Storage at Hangar-E), SumireV muttered quietly to herself as they walked away: "Best for IC to stay clear of wars unless attacked. And best of all—Radiant Guard still has no idea I tapped Core energy or ran on batteries and Mini-Nuclear Reactors instead of Natural Energy."

Chapter 31 - Mystery Mighty Host of Humanity

Two days before SumireV and Team-Expedition Realm returned to New Pantera, Nagasawa, Klein, and Astra finished a research sweep inside the Interstellar Chronicle Golden Memory Archive.

Glancing down the endless glowing halls, Astra paused as an old memory surfaced—her first fierce argument with SumireV.

"Astra, don't be afraid—I'm not like the others," SumireV had promised back then. "I welcome you to IC. I want to save you. I know your universe was destroyed, and I'm offering shelter."

"YOU destroyed MY universe with your combined force!" Astra had raged. "You're a Core of Absolute user! For millennia, that power existed to slaughter Deities—they tormented my faction! You're just like them!"

"Look at my face, Astra! I don't view you as a threat! Those who hurt your empire were previous-era users—I'm SumireV, the new generation! Besides, they only acted because your empire expanded uncontrollably across neighboring multiverses! Look into my eyes—you know my true intentions."

Shaking off the memory, Astra strolled further down an unexplored archive wing, watching holographic records of SumireV shattering fleets, cosmic beasts, and invading armies—recognizing the true Protector within her. Stepping toward a sealed memory section, Astra froze as Nagasawa and Klein suddenly grabbed her shoulders from behind.

"What are you up to?"

"Just exploring old war logs," Astra replied smoothly.

"Same! SumireV-san is so strong—I love her!" Nagasawa beamed.

"I'm just following Nagasawa to make sure she doesn't touch anything forbidden," Klein added. "Besides, I'm curious what kinds of weapons SumireV creates with the Core of Absolute."

Exploring together, Klein pulled up the 13-year Sigil Compact Coalition War—watching in awe as SumireV held over six warfronts solo, healed near-instantaneously despite horrific injuries masked by Serum Phaser pain suppressors, manifested 500 warships alone out of pure energy, and battled the Big Seven Sentinels. Meanwhile, Nagasawa and Astra checked an early IC clash against the Government Authority of the Universe (GAU) back when GAU fielded barely five cosmic units—watching an inexperienced SumireV accidentally wipe out civilians using Sagittarius A* before she understood her Core.

Suddenly, Nagasawa shouted excitedly: "Hey! Both of you, come here! I found something huge about the old IC—when it was called Intellectual Chronicle!"

Running over, they read the title: The Betrayal and Destruction of IC—prefaced with a sobering line: "When logic and reason are brought into a world of the ignorant, not everyone accepts them. Humanity's greatest downfall is choosing ego and comfort over growth and improvement."

Playing the memory of IC's original 300-million-strong stronghold in the Vibrance Solar System, Klein grew impatient with the quiet opening and tapped fast-forward toward a bright red marker. Instantly, they heard lazy, spiteful Intellectual Chronicle citizens mocking SumireV behind her back:

"Protector of Humanity? She doesn't look like one."

"So what if GAU attacks? I'm just going on vacation!"

"SumireV? Lol, just a try-hard playing hero."

"Honestly, I lowkey want to watch SumireV fall—she's so strong she makes me feel small! Every battle she keeps upgrading, so I started telling her I was too busy to help just to slow her down and see if she'd break—yet she always comes back prepared with endless backup plans!"

"I only joined IC because they're strong enough to protect me. Deploy? Fuk that—I want peace, not war. If enemies invade, I'll just pretend I'm on their side."*

Fast-forwarding further, they saw SumireV sitting exhausted and stressed inside her room: "Those people have such low IQ... I gave them everything—freedom, luxury, life, food—and all I get back is neglect, guilt-tripping, and gaslighting. They disguise malice as jokes just to avoid feeling guilty for hurting me. Something terrible is coming soon."

Skipping ahead to the red mark, they watched the GAU invade a hollowed-out IC whose traitors had crippled morale and abandoned defensive posts—sealing SumireV inside a blinding maze called the Dimension of Illusion. Four hours later, SumireV shattered the seal using 3rd-Level Singularity Rebuild, emerging into utter betrayal. Her own best friend stood admitting the plot out of bitter jealousy over her endless growth, while the loyal 15% died fighting without kneeling—culminating in SumireV's beloved scientist Chris trying to confess his love just before an elite GAU Tanker Soldier crushed his head like a watermelon beneath a sledgehammer while smiling traitors looked on.

Floating high above the ruined city, crimson eyes distorting gravity as tears fell, SumireV whispered coldly: "I have nothing left to protect. Everything is gone. So humanity loves destruction, huh? All that remains is pain, death, and betrayal. Core of Absolute, Second Level: Dark Phoenix A—unleash your wrath!" An ultra-massive black hole swallowed every IC planet and the entire Vibrance system whole before SumireV passed out.

Silence hung heavy among the three observers until Astra spoke quietly. "So that's what happened. Wait—I have something else to show you both."

Searching the logs for The One Invasion, Astra pulled up a file she had witnessed only as a distant observer before joining IC. From Universe-121 came The One—an extra-universal juggernaut wielding Time Control, Cosmic Lightning, and stolen Nebula shapeshifting (transforming into blazing Suns and Cosmic Rays). With the Government Authority crushed and the Multiple Alliance Compact (MAC) abandoning Universe-26 as hopeless, The One ravaged a third of the universe until SumireV met his armada head-on.

Losing half her body in the fighting, SumireV pushed into overclocked Phase 7—burning organs and memories faster than expected—watched Zeroth Black Holes vanish under The One's Time stops, shattered his Dimension of Eternal Damnation using engineered Zeroth super-massive black holes and Level 3 Singularity Rebuild, blinded his time-immune senses behind High-Explosive battleship salvos and planet-ashing Solar beams, and exploited his lack of strategy by baiting his stamina dry until trapping him inside a Second-Level Primordial Black Hole pounded from every direction by ship-mounted Radical Collapses. Stripped of his tech suit at last, The One fell to SumireV's close-quarters gravity mastery, shredded by two spinning blades, and erased completely via 9th-Level Singularity Engine: Reality Reverse—while her archived personal vow echoed through the recording:

"I defended the meaning behind this Core of Absolute, and today SumireV of Intellectual Chronicle shall defend the universe from total annihilation! Tremble before the might of the Core of Absolute and Hero of Humanity! You're facing the ingenuity of the human race—this legion of humanity shall stand as long as I live! You declared war on my universe, and human civilization shall bring the entirety of its might to bear against you! Law and Logic, not the Chaos of Destruction and Viciousness—I shall prevail! Our flesh may be weak, yet we refuse to kneel! 9th-Level Singularity Engine: Reality Reverse!"

Klein stared at Astra in disbelief. "So SumireV really is the hero of the universe?"

"Not a clean one," Astra replied softly. "Think of how many innocent lives she killed by accident—deaths she desperately wished she could avoid—because things rarely go according to plan."

"Wait... SumireV is 1,200 years old?!" Nagasawa gasped. "She told us she was 500! She's carried unimaginable pain that no normal human could bear—truly someone everyone fears and envies."

Turning gently toward Astra, Klein asked, "Astra... where are you really from?"

Pain crossed Astra's face. "I'm from another universe—far outside this Multiverse Sphere, which holds 10,000 universes. My faction conquered our entire sphere until neighboring spheres and Deities and Demi-Gods waged a brutal 9,000-year war against us. Knowing it was unwinnable, our King self-destructed our empire to drag everyone down with us. Some survived like me, though the erasure wiped most of my memories—I only remember our King and how vast our empire was, not the details. That zone remains sealed by unstable energy even now. Keep this secret—only SumireV knows."

"Let's get out of here before SumireV-san comes back," Nagasawa urged—the trio slipping quietly out two days before SumireV's return.

Chapter 32 - Overseer of Filament

Deep across isolated space, the Arcadian Seal—locked shut by an ancient hero 5,300 years earlier—began cracking apart. Four guarding Sentinels broadcast an urgent warning back to Cluster Creed: "The Arcadian has awakened! Prepare for a great war—we'll hold them off!"

The sealing mountain shattered—not pulled inward, but annihilated outright by a destructive black hole—followed by a blinding orbital beam that flattened the Sentinels into a smoking crater.

Stepping out of the rubble stood Aetheris, Emperor of the Arcadian race, carrying five Crystals and one Core: the Core of Manifestation.

"Ahh... sealed away for 5,300 years, and at last we are FREE!" Aetheris boomed. "Surely that old hero is long gone. Now it's our turn to unleash terror across this universe—nothing can stop us this time! Let's go, lads—SHOW THEM WHAT WE'RE MADE OF!" Raising a hand, he unleashed four Overseer Beams straight into the Sentinels.

Meanwhile, inside an Orion-TW3 military camp, Matteo lay fast asleep until Arthur shook him awake.

"Oi, mate, wake up—today's the big day! We're officially in Zenith Triarch!"

"Bruv, you've already said that a hundred times," Matteo groaned.

"Just messing with you, mate—we're finally deploying to the GAU front! No sitting around doing nothing like during the Tectonic War—we've been chilling for five months!"

"And we finally get to test our Prototype abilities and experimental gear!" Sofia grinned, walking over—right before a Teleportation Port snapped open and Major Maelys stepped through.

"Briefing update: we're losing ground on the GAU front," Maelys announced briskly. "They're deploying Artificial Celestials—unstable, yet powerful enough to wipe out entire divisions. High Command wants Team Gamma on the frontline immediately: first hold the enemy advance, second reclaim lost territory. Good news—IC will assist; bad news—they'll arrive slightly late after getting hit by a Wild Cosmic Anomaly. Any questions?"

"No questions, sir!" Matteo snapped cleanly as Maelys opened the frontline rear portal.

Joining Alpha, Upsilon, Delta (Apis), and Omega Teams inside the forward briefing room, Commander Evan laid out the rules of engagement: protect Mecha and Armor Divisions from GAU cosmic hunters—and beware GAU's new OptCos Jammers, which blocked all cosmic energy sensing. Forced to rely entirely on Orion Air Force visual spotting against the #1 military in air, land, and sea, Matteo traded an anxious gulp with Sofia before deploying.

Arriving at Gamma Sector amid thunderous GAU mortar fire, Matteo plunged one hand straight into the soil—impaling enemy mortar batteries on erupting steel spikes. In the center, as GAU heavy armor pressed Orion's chokepoint, Matteo called over comms: "Enemy at three o'clock—Zero, do your thing!"

"Enjoy the show," Zero grinned, streaking forward wrapped in a glowing Radiation Aura and slamming straight into the armored column in a 100-kiloton TNT nuclear blast.

Overhead, Orion pilots swept the skies—spotting 20 Heralds closing fast. "I've got their flanker!" Arthur shouted, accelerating air particles hypersonically with MV (Momentum & Vector) to vaporize a 30-meter radius in dust while Zero overpowered two Heralds with Ionizing Rays.

Suddenly, a hand clamped around Matteo's throat from behind—an Archon.

"Oh great... an Archon. I hate you guys," Matteo muttered, revving a forearm chainsaw to carve free as metal springs launched him clear and his right arm morphed into a titanium scalpel blade. Slowing Matteo's strike by two seconds, the Archon blasted him backward with Burst Energy—watching in surprise as Matteo skidded to a stop intact.

"Oh, you survived? You're no ordinary human," the Archon noted.

"Correct—I'm a Sovereign," Matteo replied evenly. "Herald durability, Archon-plus strength."

Unleashing hundreds of melting titanium spikes to overwhelm the Archon's two-second time slows and cripple his regeneration, Matteo suddenly engaged Lightspeed Transversal—blinking behind the Archon before he could react.

"What did you do?" the Archon gasped.

"Sliced your core cleanly. You're dead," Matteo smiled as the Archon split in half. Racing forward to catch up with Team Gamma, Matteo skidded to a halt before an ominous wall of crimson ash contained behind energy barriers.

"Sofia, what is this red mist?" he asked.

"IC's biochemical weapon—Velocirax-Nyxvirus," Sofia whispered nervously, gesturing left toward Iflaren—the former enemy who had shattered their fleet months ago, now fighting beside them. Listening to muffled mutations and bone-grinding shrieks inside the mist, they waited for IC to clear the zone with Helium gas.

Meanwhile, back on New Pantera, Ampasy lay impatiently on a surgical bed. "Come on, SumireV—upgrade me to Serum Phaser-XI so I can handle Phase 7 or 8 safely, heal fast, and barely feel pain!"

Warned by SumireV, MiRi, and Dr. Edward that it meant deep biological overhaul on top of his existing cybernetics, Ampasy didn't blink—demanding to match Cosmic foes on physical grit alone. Six agonizing hours later, SumireV stepped back cleanly:

"Alright, Ampasy—you're upgraded. You can safely run Serum Phaser-XI Phase 7 and 8, though 9 and 10 will corrupt and tear you apart. Your bones are now Percentile-Strength nanorobots, your veins are Tungsten Tubes, and you run off Kinetic and Thermal Absorber Energy (TAE)—converting incoming heat directly into fuel—backed by Mini Nuclear Fission waste hardening your frame. Sadly, you're 90% machine now and only 10% human."

"Don't care—let's test it!" Ampasy bounded off the table straight to Simulator Room 3. Testing half Percentile Strength split the virtual earth for nine kilometers. Pushing SP-XI Phase 9, SP-ESD Phase 4, and 400% Percentile Strength (1,800% total output) shattered the simulated planet beneath repeated punches—and activating Carnage Mode (2,240% output for five minutes, ten-minute cooldown) blew the entire Earth apart in a single punch!

Chapter 33 - Aetheris of Arcadian

Inside Interstellar Chronicle, SumireV continued running experiments on Clock-Lock, which currently held a 30-kilometer radius and a five-minute cooldown—useful for finishing half-weakened enemies, yet far from ideal against stronger cosmic entities like Sentinels and above. Determined to expand the effect radius and shorten the cooldown, SumireV frowned at another failed calibration.

"That's the sixth attempt and still no change," she muttered. "If it fails ten times, we drop it and move on to another project."

Suddenly, Mao hurried in. "Hey, Boss! We're picking up a massive energy spike from the Cluster Creed sector! Our outpost there just spotted an ancient army called Arcadian waging war against Cluster Creed—you need to watch this footage."

Heading straight to the Energy Department Planet in New Pantera, SumireV studied the graphs closely before reviewing the combat recordings. Thirty minutes later, she reached a sobering conclusion.

"From what I can see... this Aetheris is wielding a Higher Authority ability."

Mao frowned in confusion. "Higher Authority? You mean the Celestials?"

SumireV shook her head. "No. It's an ability originating outside our universe. Those Higher Authorities are dangerously strong, and what he's using is Cataclysm—specifically the Havoc variant. Havoc means whenever he casts an attack, anything touched inside the blast zone is erased completely from existence. Watch right there when he fires Beam Destruction—the affected corridor is only twenty meters wide, yet everything inside vanishes utterly, slicing clean through that bunker. In short, do not get hit by it. I've encountered them before and usually had to escape—I've only ever killed one Higher Authority."

Out of nowhere, a voice spoke up behind them: "I know that power!"

Turning around, SumireV saw Astra stepping closer. "Tell me everything you remember," SumireV urged.

"Remember where I came from—that multiverse empire that fell?" Astra explained. "That power, Cataclysm, originally came from us. What Aetheris is using is barely ten percent of the original power because it's wildly unstable, and only my race—the Oblivion—can wield it safely."

SumireV nodded slowly. "Do you know how to counter it?"

"No... my memory was partially wiped after our King self-destructed our empire and the Deities covered everything up. I can't remember."

SumireV's expression turned calculating. "Then if the Arcadian army moves near our territory, that's when we act. First, we wait patiently for nearby factions to wear them down—then we strike and finish the Arcadians once they're weakened enough."

"What about Iflaren and Ampasy?" Mao asked. "They're still on the Orion front holding back the GAU invasion."

SumireV smirked faintly. "Hehehe... I have a gift waiting for the GAU. They call themselves the Top 1 Military, so let's test that."

Just as their conversation ended, a deep, vibrating hum shook the air above IC's Control Center. Mao and Astra stepped out to look up as the colossal silhouette of Project Zealous—the Behemoth-class war machine—glided overhead, bristling with 400 Plasma Charge Energy Cannons, 80 Nuclear Rocket Silos, a massive bow-mounted Laser Cannon built to cripple motherships, six 20-megaton Railguns, Velocirax-Nyxvirus cluster artillery, 600 Heavy Machine Guns (30mm), 300 hypersonic Flak cannons firing Titanium nets, anti-materiel plating behind a 50-second cooldown energy shield, a ventral Archon-Core Judgment Flame, and the Prometheus Modulation Prototype.

"Sumi, who is that meant for?" Astra asked.

"To fu*k up the GAU's economy and resources," SumireV replied evenly, "so they stop shipping armies against our alliance once it gets too costly. Meanwhile, the rest of us elites will dig into Arcadian intelligence—and integrate Arcadian Energy into our tanks."

Meanwhile, out on the Orion-TW3 frontline, Prototype Team Gamma and Iflaren fought fiercely against incoming cosmic units. Locked in a duel against a Generation-4 Sentinel, Iflaren sprayed acidic mist across the air.

"I'm using the Velocirax-Nyxvirus C variant," Iflaren thought grimly. "It cuts cosmic abilities by forty percent, though this bastard is resisting hard. I just need one clean opening to neutralize him."

Spawning a black hole, the Sentinel forced Iflaren to evade at burst speed and lash back with a Vile Venom Stream that scorched the Sentinel's skin—only for it to heal rapidly and sink Iflaren into the earth up to his neck. Igniting Serum Phaser-VII Phase 5, Iflaren ripped free, slammed the Sentinel straight into a mountain, and triggered Velocirax-Nyxvirus Annihilation—erupting in thick Crimson Acidic Steam and dripping venom as his form mutated.

Just as the Sentinel fired devastating Cosmic Rays point-blank, Ampasy slammed down directly between them and blocked the beam barehanded!

"Hello there, Iflaren—let's fight like pros!" Ampasy grinned.

"Just like the old days!" Iflaren laughed.

Both pushed into peak synergy—Iflaren glowing deep crimson under Velocirax-Nyxvirus Annihilation Stage 4's hyper-corrosive venom while Ampasy flared bright with Serum Phaser-ESD Phase 4 bio-electricity. Blitzing the Sentinel in relentless close quarters, Ampasy launched him skyward with a brutal uppercut before Iflaren morphed his forearm into an Acidic Anchor and slammed the Sentinel crashing back down at supersonic speed in a massive Red Mist detonation. Struggling upright to fire a desperate energy beam, the Sentinel found both elites darting left and right faster than its energy sensors could track—crushing its skull cleanly between a supersonic Duo Punch!

"Good game, Ampasy," Iflaren chuckled. "If we hadn't gotten upgraded, we couldn't have touched a Sentinel like that."

"Well, I got a way bigger upgrade than you," Ampasy boasted.

"You're nuts—we're all running Serum Phaser-XI now!" Laughing together, they fell back toward Orion lines while Matteo and Prototype Team Gamma stared after them in speechless awe—the Behemoth now less than two hours away from Orion space.

Chapter 34 - Behemoth Against Conventional

Inside Government Authority territory, communication channels jammed with frantic reports from sector after sector warning of an unstoppable IC killing machine tearing through space—the Behemoth.

Assembling 400 warships backed by Heralds and Archons, Admiral Adam addressed his crews firmly. "We are launching a decisive battle against that Behemoth! Whatever it takes, we have to defend our worlds—that Behemoth is sweeping through our territory bringing calamity in its wake. Allied fleets are moving to back us up! We can do this—long live the Queen!"

Halfway to the combat zone, an urgent distress alert flashed over comms: "Behemoth spotted over our Depository Planet! All nearby fleets engage and halt the Behemoth from causing further destruction!"

Intercepting the battered remnants of vanguard fleets along the way, Adam absorbed the survivors into his formation and summoned their commander aboard his flagship.

"What happened, Stefano?" Adam demanded. "Tell me what you faced out there—give me every detail you learned about this Behemoth!"

"It's... death kissing you," Commander Stefano whispered hollowly—abruptly turning aside to vomit as memories flashed back. Regaining his breath, he wiped his mouth. "That monster carries too many weapons. Our allied ships could barely tank its fire long enough to save us. It shoots biochemicals, endless explosives—and whatever you do, do NOT go underneath the Behemoth! It has a demonic flame below that burns everything until whole landmasses turn molten. I brought eighty ships and lost half before ordering a retreat to wait for you, Adam."

"Were there any soldiers or cosmic entities guarding it?" Adam asked sharply.

"Only conventional soldiers—zero sight of cosmic units. Even The Commonwealth confirmed it!"

Adam nodded slowly, pushing his flagship faster while muttering quietly to himself—having spent his entire career on The Empire skirmish front, this would be his first direct clash against Interstellar Chronicle.

Dropping out of warp above Depository Planet, Adam linked up with waiting armadas from the GAU, The Commonwealth, and Parliament Abstract, watching the Behemoth casually shell the world below. Convening quickly with allied commanders, he assessed what each brought to the table:

"Anything special in your arsenal, Commonwealth?" Adam asked.

"We carry a weapon capable of damaging the Behemoth's guns—Perseus Lasers—though we'll need escort protection to fire safely," Admiral Zucman replied.

Adam nodded and turned to Parliament Abstract. "What about you, PA? Anything useful? You only brought four small armadas."

"Most of our armada was destroyed in the Tectonic War, so we came with everything we had left," Commander Goh explained. "Our tech can only freeze IC's guns temporarily—they don't have an instant counter yet, forcing crews to thaw them manually. Keep in mind the Behemoth also raises an energy shield, so we have to time our strikes carefully."

"Alright, here's the plan," Adam laid out calmly—brushing aside a warning from his own GAU officer that the Behemoth mounted over 700 guns. "Don't worry, pal, we have allies and a strategy, just like on The Empire front! First, GAU barrages with 40% of our firepower; the instant its shield drops into cooldown, we pour in the remaining 60% to kill its secondary weapon operators. Then PA freezes the guns while Commonwealth blasts them apart. Begin Operation Goliath—move in from all sides!"

Plunging into the planet's atmosphere, the allied fleets hit a blinding sandstorm. The instant the dust parted, every barrel aboard the Behemoth swung toward them pulsing eerie blue light—GAU cruisers soaking up the opening salvo at a cost of 900,000 dead sailors.

"EXCHANGE FIRE!" Adam roared.

Twenty percent of the allied salvo struck the Behemoth's shield right before it flickered down into cooldown. Parliament Abstract immediately blasted freezing laser cannons across the hull, followed closely by Commonwealth Perseus Lasers crippling several railguns and artillery batteries.

"Closing range—prepare to deploy ground forces!" Adam ordered.

Before troops could drop, the Behemoth's shield cooldown expired—and its massive Railguns opened fire in all four directions, obliterating 40% of the combined allied armada in a heartbeat. Ears ringing wildly through the blast, Adam saw PA and Commonwealth pushing onward anyway and barked, "Lower ships! DEPLOY GROUND FORCES!"

Suddenly, Adam spotted an ominous red glow building inside the ship's belly—Prometheus Modulation! He ordered Commonwealth to fire at the spot immediately, only for the energy shield to snap back up seconds before the overloaded Prometheus Modulation ruptured violently from excess reactor and Herald-Core energy, sending the colossal Behemoth tilting earthward in flames.

Inside the shaking bridge, General Hazim shouted over comms to SumireV: "BOSS! This Behemoth is unstable! The energy from those Reactors and Herald Cores is overloading—we're falling and they're closing in!"

SumireV's voice came back cold and unyielding: "Inflict as much damage as you can before going down. Remember what you signed up for—show them the mighty force of IC technology!"

Assuming the crimson Prometheus Modulation controlled the ship's Judgment Flame, Adam ordered every cannon concentrated there—a fatal mistake. As the Behemoth plummeted, its ventral Judgment Flame ignited anyway, turning every ground unit underneath into boiling magma while West Force scrambled into retreat.

Crashing motionless onto the crust, the Behemoth lay surrounded by swarming GAU reinforcements roaring prematurely about victory—until Commander Hazim inside pressed the button activating Second Life.

Instantly, every surviving barrel roared back to life. Artillery fired Velocirax-Nyxvirus Cluster Canisters, HMGs swept the ground, nuclear warheads detonated across landing zones, and 300 Anti-Air Flak cannons fired hypersonic Titanium nets directly into infantry ranks—shredding soldiers into flying limbs!

"THEY FUKING USE ANTI-AIR ON US—ON FUKING HUMANS!" troops shrieked as crimson ash mutated dying comrades into screaming monsters tearing at their own allies until the Behemoth's barrels finally overheated.

"ALL UNITS FALL BACK NOW! RETREAT! RETREAT—THAT'S AN ORDER!" Adam screamed as 290 million soldiers died in mere minutes. Turning to flee amid exploding escorts, Adam froze as the Behemoth's surviving Railguns and bow-mounted Laser Cannon turned straight onto his flagship. Desperate, Adam killed one of his own bridge crewmen to consume a spare life, barely escaping via jetpack as the devastating laser obliterated his ship and pushed the death toll to 657 million—while fresh armadas converged under planet-shaking bombardment.

Back inside GAU Production Headquarters, Marshall Raymond slammed his fist down: "WHERE ARE OUR HERALDS?! ARCHONS?! EVEN CELESTIALS?! WE NEED THEM—THAT BEHEMOTH IS RUINING OUR MAINLAND!"

"Sir, we can't mass-produce them!" a scientist stammered. "Cluster Creed hasn't shipped the engine to build them—we don't have the tech to replicate it, and we're completely out of Marrow Energy!"

Raymond seized the scientist by his collar. "Contact Cluster Creed and get Marrow Energy NOW!"

"We just got word from Cluster Creed, sir!" another scientist cried out. "They're locked in war against an ancient army—all international trade routes are shut down!"

Clenching his fist furiously, Raymond snapped, "Pull our soldiers back from Orion, The Empire, and the Pivot Federation fronts immediately to defend our mainland!"

Chapter 35 - Reclaim Land

Thirty-six hours after the Behemoth tore into GAU territory, Prototype Team Gamma met with Iflaren and Ampasy on the Orion Front.

"So the plan is we flank the enemy from the left side?" Matteo asked.

"Yes," Iflaren confirmed. "We IC will take the center and absorb the heaviest hits since there are more cosmic defenders there—you guys are too valuable with your new tech. Still, I won't lie and say your flank will be easy; it's defended too, just manageable for Orion."

"Don't forget," Ampasy added, "GAU pulled huge numbers back home after our Behemoth wrecked their space. This is our chance to kick them clean out of Orion-TW3 territory!"

"Yeah, we're sick of fighting that giant GAU," Arthur grinned. "Thanks to IC, we finally got relief—plus GAU betrayed us during our war with IC and left us to die."

"Oh, sorry about wiping out your fleet back then," Iflaren chuckled awkwardly. "Anyway, everyone should know by now how irresponsible GAU is. Here's the mapping from Rear Command."

Minutes later, after locking in the assault plan, Prototype Team Gamma stood poised for Iflaren's signal. Across the center, a blinding plume of Crimson Ash erupted from Iflaren's attack.

"ATTACK! Kick those bast*rds out of OUR LAND! ORION SHALL RISE!" Matteo roared.

Orion Air Force jets swooped low dropping incendiaries while Zero streaked down to detonate GAU mortar nests with miniature nuclear blasts. Catching Matteo with Momentum & Vector, Arthur hurled him skyward so Matteo rained thousands of piercing, melting steel nails across the trenches below before diving to liquefy his arm into a massive spiked mace and smash through enemy pillboxes while Zero engaged Heralds and Archons.

Suddenly encountering an Ice Herald backed by an energy-nullifier turret that froze his metal power useless, Matteo barked over comms: "Need backup!" Seconds later, an Orion jet dropped an ear-bleeding high-pitch sonic device across the trench, allowing Matteo to hurl six molten steel blades, slice the Herald cleanly apart, and smash the artillery railgun before pushing deeper through the tunnels to clear pillboxes and nullifier turrets.

Meanwhile, pinned down and bleeding under waves of suicide robots, Zero retreated while calling urgently over comms: "Team, I need medical attention right now—assist me!"

While Sofia blanketed incoming GAU troops with bombardment and Matteo plunged deeper to spark panic, Sofia dropped an aerial supply pod into Zero's reach. Installing the emergency bionic arm and leg healing kit, Zero surged back into battle against two Heralds—peeling their skin away with air-vacuuming Ultra-Gamma Rays, staggering them with atomic-explosion radiation punches, and finishing both with piercing Alpha Rays before inhaling anti-radiation gas from his suit inventory to heal his own scorched skin.

"Whoa—our powers really still are prototypes," Matteo remarked catching up from behind.

"It's okay, boy," Zero smiled faintly. "I used to be a Nuclear Engineer, and I love being deployed to fight for Orion."

Receiving orders from Command to hold the ridge as a defensive line until IC arrived, Team Gamma watched IC reinforcements lock down the heights—deploying hypersonic Titanium-net Anti-Air Flak cannons that tore eight fresh GAU divisions into flying limbs, prompting swift retreat even among Archons. Across the valley, they watched Ampasy rip a Sentinel's head clean off before IC's Global Abomination dropped tungsten rods dispersing hundreds of VX Nerve Agent canisters followed by saturated artillery fire.

Nagasawa walked up to meet Team Gamma on the ridge. "Alright, boys, we did it—GAU is retreating, or mostly wiped out. Your land is free of GAU; Codling and Astra will mop up any pocket resistance. Focus on reconstruction now, and begin researching the Arcadian Army—it's mandatory across IC, Tectonic Pact, and Bureau Coalition."

Shaking his head with a tired grin, Matteo signaled his squad. "Here we go again—let's move, boys!" Prototype Team Gamma headed back toward their homeland.

Chapter 36 - Balancing Timing

Inside the Interstellar Chronicle Stronghold on New Pantera, SumireV stood before a classroom of new recruits explaining how Prometheus Modulation worked.

"As you can see, Prometheus Modulation—or PM for short—contains a specialized gravity adjuster," SumireV taught. "The only catch is it cannot be wielded barehanded unless you possess cosmic durability or deep mastery of the tech. It only casts black holes, pushes, and pulls vertically. For students, I only recommend using Amplified 2, which creates an artificial black hole with a 10-to-100-meter radius. Keep in mind this is a pseudo black hole whose maximum directional pull can be fixed—an actual black hole is far more dangerous, completely uncontrollable, and causes massive collateral damage."

Finishing her two-hour lecture, she returned to her office and gazed outside at the aftermath of IC's intervention in Orion-TW3, allowing her people time to unwind and relieve built-up tension.

Checking the latest reports on the ongoing war between the Bureau Coalition and The Hunt, she saw The Hunt remained significantly active despite getting hammered by IC's Divine Bullet. SumireV rubbed her temples and sighed quietly.

"I just want my IC to stay out of wars. We can't sustain prolonged strain like this forever—we need to focus on one problem at a time." Still, the Arcadian Army spikes flaring inside Cluster Creed alarmed her despite limited news coming out of that sector. Deciding to wait patiently until the Arcadians either neared IC borders or tangled with neighboring factions, she planned to strike from their exposed flank once they wore themselves down.

Six days later, SumireV sparred inside the simulator room against an AI replica of herself, probing for hidden openings and weaknesses. She confirmed three critical flaws: having the Core of Absolute physically ripped away; draining herself in exceptionally long battles despite layered reserves (though rare); and facing energy nullifiers or enemy energy converters that cut off Natural Energy when dueling stronger cosmic foes.

Stepping out of the simulator, she found Dr. Edward waiting outside.

"Sumi, I have urgent news," Dr. Edward reported. "The Arcadians already managed to leak into Imperial Phalanx territory. Cluster Creed couldn't hold back their momentum, and even though IP deployed a Planet Fortress that held well at first, Aetheris arrived personally and destroyed it. Pivot Federation and The Empire are already rushing reinforcements to assist IP—the problem is they could leak straight into IC territory if they spot a weak seam."

"We'll prepare," SumireV replied evenly. "If they breach our border, let five days pass with zero resistance so they drop their guard—then I'll confront them personally." Dr. Edward nodded and stepped out while SumireV and IC's engineering divisions stepped up daily drills and stockpiled massive reserve fleets.

Meanwhile, across GAU territory, government forces were still struggling desperately to take down the parked Behemoth. Fatalities had already climbed to roughly 491 billion, and their Artificial Celestials couldn't even approach the wreck without having their unstable attacks disrupted by its energy field.

Furious arguments erupted inside GAU High Command over whether to obliterate the Behemoth with a Planet Destroyer Laser or keep throwing elite units at it to salvage IC tech intact. Admiral Blackbear fought fiercely to capture the technology, yet every other commander and admiral overruled him due to catastrophic casualty rates—forcing the GAU Ministry of Defense to dispatch four Orbital Planetary Destroyers directly toward the Behemoth.

Chapter 37 - Revelation Past

Sitting together inside the IC lounge, Astra and several elite members talked quietly about what Astra, Nagasawa, and Klein had witnessed inside the Golden Memory Archive.

"Look, we already knew SumireV wasn't just strong—she's capable of killing nearly anyone she wants," Astra said evenly.

"Come on, tell us more about it!" Iflaren urged.

"Are you slow?" Klein asked dryly. "SumireV literally creates thousands of armadas out of pure energy—I don't even want to imagine how much energy she keeps stored inside that Core of Absolute."

"All I know," Astra continued, "is that several of us carry implanted Cores too: Saifuru holds the Core of Haste; Mao, Serenity; Iflaren, Devouring; I hold Retribution; Devin holds Reality; and of course SumireV holds the Core of Absolute."

Iflaren's eyes lit up instantly. "I have a CORE?! I didn't even know that! No regrets, though—it makes me strong. Maybe... just maybe I can manifest armadas like SumireV!"

"NO!" Astra cut in firmly. "Every Core is different, and we have no idea when yours might fully awaken—only Saifuru and I have awakened ours, and I've lived for thousands of years! Besides, ours are pseudo-links, and IC tech builds upon existing principles to forge new tools, unlike natural Cores born in-universe with unpredictable ceilings."

"And SumireV's Core of Absolute isn't even from this universe," Nagasawa added softly. "Her Core was forged by an ancient multiversal superpower named Ernst, who sacrificed Vigintillions of Lights and Lives to build it."

"Antonietti Ernst—first user and inventor," a calm voice echoed from the doorway.

Everyone stiffened—even Saifuru's heart hammered against his ribs—as SumireV stepped inside smoothly.

"I sacrificed everything the multiverse offered me to forge the Core of Absolute using every universe's colonized technology, rising to a Type-8 Civilization—erasing light itself to forge a weapon capable of rivaling cluster-multiverse destroyers," SumireV recited. "It was built to be passed down to chosen wielders whose memories and messages remain inside so newer successors won't repeat their fatal mistakes. Forged by humans, for humans—not born naturally. Every fallen user knew one grim truth: 'How many creations are sacrificed just to create another creation?' That's why Ernst called it the 'Core of Vigintillion Lights and Life.' Still... I overheard everything, so what were you all talking about? Feel free to ask me directly."

Silence lingered briefly before SumireV reassured them. "Relax—I'm not mad. Why would I be?"

"Sumi," Ampasy spoke up, "tell us about the previous users. How is the Core of Absolute so broken and so tragic at the same time?"

SumireV walked over and sat comfortably on the couch. "Gather close and listen. Let's start with what the Core of Absolute actually is. It was forged to counter overwhelming cosmic entities—even rivaling Deities and Demi-Gods—embodying the true ceiling of the human mind. It proved that even though our flesh is weak, our intellect can create weapons rivaling cosmic beings, born out of exhaustion from endless cosmic oppression. Ernst dismantled all knowledge across countless multiverses to create it, which immediately made humanity a massive target under Cosmic Rules because it broke the natural hierarchy.

"As for its abilities, they depend on each wielder's affinity, though anyone can study every pillar of Mastery of Physics: Space, Time, Gravity, Thermodynamics, Acoustics, Quantum, Matter, Creation, Dimension, and Electromagnetism. Previous users were far stronger than me—though a few were weaker. Trust me, some of them could split multiple universes apart; someone like Ethan used to kill Celestials casually just to warm up. They're still beyond what we fully grasp—not quite your ceiling yet."

The elites nodded slowly until Saifuru smiled. "Bring us inside your dimension—we want to see it."

SumireV's eyes blazed bright blue. In a single blink, the lounge vanished—replaced by a dizzying Tesseract-like void filled with drifting numbers, equations, and constantly morphing geometry.

"What would you guys like to see?" SumireV asked.

"A supermassive black hole," Klein volunteered.

Effortlessly, SumireV manifested TON 618 hovering harmlessly right before them.

"Wait—how aren't you straining or burning energy here?" Ampasy asked in amazement.

"I govern the Fundamental Laws in here at will—I'm only showing the visual without unleashing real damage, though staying here still consumes my Core energy steadily."

"Among all the previous users, who do you like the most?" Nagasawa teased.

SumireV's cheeks flushed faintly. "Well... it's Ethan, the 262nd user. He carries himself well, invented Prometheus Modulation, loves gravity and logic, lived in a similar era—and most importantly, he came from Universe-26."

Jaws dropped across the room. "Wait—older Core of Absolute users actually walked inside OUR universe?!" Iflaren gasped. "I thought they only existed in other universes!"

"SumireV," Astra spoke quietly, "show me the weapon built specifically to rival Deities... you know the one I mean."

SumireV nodded gently. "I know, Astra. The weapon previous wielders used to defeat your empire. Creation Ability: 9th Principal Ingenuity—Singularity Engine."

Inside her Dimension, she manifested the complete, devastating Singularity Engine capable of slaying Deities—explaining she could only manage a Replica version under real-world strain—followed by a dazzling showcase of black hole variants (auto-expanding strikes, random blinking singularities, and variable sizes) before bringing everyone back out to the lounge to conserve reserves.

Immediately, Iflaren tried forcing his own Core awake, only for SumireV to shake her head. "Iflaren, it only awakens in truly dire moments—until then, it passively boosts your energy capacity and recharge rate. Crown already knows he holds the Core of Domination, though his is dormant too. Remember, several rival factions hide Core users of their own—some dormant, some already awakened with reality-warping attacks. Stay sharp out there so none of you get killed and let enemies steal our tech—and keep in mind the Celestial Council likely has eyes locked onto IC as well."

Chapter 38 - The Empire

Aboard an outbound troop transport in Empire space, Corporal Zola hugged his wife and young daughter tightly goodbye before joining his platoon.

His close friend Xipil bumped his shoulder enthusiastically. "Zola, my guy! Aren't you pumped to deploy into Cluster Creed?! This is my first real deployment!"

Zola offered a weary, solemn glance. "Xipil, this is war—and you're human, not an Archon like me. I fought on the GAU front, and trust me, death comes almost instantly if you aren't careful."

Sitting down with their platoon to pass the flight over a deck of cards, Ajay asked, "Zola, tell us what the GAU front was really like—give us some real combat experience."

"War isn't some fun TV adventure," Zola answered quietly, tossing down a card. "It's survival by any means necessary. You barely ever see the enemy unless they ping on radar—everything comes down to who spots whom first. Most of the time, you're pinned flat behind cover praying someone else shoots the sniper aiming straight at your skull."

Young cadet Luka leaned forward curiously. "How did you awaken as an Archon?"

Zola froze briefly over his cards before exhaling slowly. "Long story—I'll keep it short. I forced my power awake under desperation. My captain already knew I carried dormant Archon genetics passed down through generations. Back when the GAU overran our Empire city of Seldom, their invisible Heralds tore through our lines so badly that Command panicked and ordered us to shoot anyone suspicious—including civilians. On the frontline, a Herald hit me with an Implosion blast that blew my lower half clean off. Crawling through the rubble as my life flashed before my eyes, I triggered Dyfra-NGS05—a toxic ethanol-droplet mist burning at 300 degrees Celsius. Looking down across the city afterward, I saw everyone dead... including a mother clutching her lifeless baby. Knowing I caused that horror snapped something inside me—awakening my Archon Equilibrium Flame at 40,000 degrees Celsius."

Silence settled over the card table—faces flickering with a heavy mix of fear, respect, and quiet disgust—before briefing packets landed in their laps.

Reading closely, Zola saw Cluster Creed was already half wiped out. Allied units carried strict neon identifiers across the theater: Pivot Federation wore Neon Orange armbands; Imperial Phalanx pulsed with Neon Red-and-Black bio-electric auras; Cluster Creed wore Neon Dark Brown; and The Empire wore Neon Red-and-White. Imperial Phalanx and Pivot Federation deployed mostly conventional armies, while The Empire and Cluster Creed fielded larger cosmic platoons—totaling 17 billion Empire soldiers, 32 billion from Pivot Federation (whose entire economy had shifted to total war), 9 billion from Imperial Phalanx, and 24 billion surviving Cluster Creed troops against over 1 trillion Arcadians (mostly conventional troops, as their cosmic units appeared diverted elsewhere). Video briefings warned that Arcadians grew significantly stronger in daylight—prompting Cluster Creed to blot out starlight behind Imperial Phalanx's Planetary Fortresses.

Suddenly, the cabin doors hissed open to heavy, arrogant footsteps. Zola clenched his fist immediately as four elite Commandos—Heroes of The Empire—sauntered past: a mutant pulsing with glowing veins, a soldier able to transform into any material he touched, a duplicator, and General Nikolai—the most arrogant of them all, capable of slaying Sentinels effortlessly with his Time power. After spending several minutes belittling ordinary infantry, they stalked back out—Xipil gazing after them in starry admiration while Zola forced a tight, bitter smile, remembering past arguments with those Commandos.

Dropping onto The Empire's forward base inside Cluster Creed, Zola stepped out across scorched craters littered with unburied dead where allies had barely clawed back ground—watching landing warships unfold smoothly into fortified bases beside Pivot Federation troops digging mass graves, Imperial Phalanx Orbital Guns looming above, and distant Cluster Creed turrets.

Called forward to a joint Cosmic Unit briefing uniting 2 IP, 9 PF, 11 Empire, and 19 CC fighters, the Cluster Creed officer barked orders briskly:

"Listen up! Protect our skies from Arcadian artillery—it's hyper-destructive. If you see a black galactic beam, do NOT try to block or tank it—it erases your body and soul outright! Their cosmic fighters aren't numerous, yet taking one down requires at least four of our Cosmic units coordinating tightly—without us, our conventional troops die! Arcadians assault every 24 to 32 hours, and our next wave hits in 24 hours. Before then, walk a few kilometers down to our Temporary Medical Camp and get vaccinated—Arcadians release lethal Spores upon death, so don't breathe them in!"

Strolling toward the Medical Camp afterward—noticing bitter tension between IP and Empire soldiers over last month's border clash, and rumors buzzing about IC's Behemoth ravaging GAU space—Zola paused near some Pivot Federation troops chatting about their recent war with Interstellar Chronicle. Curious, Zola asked what IC was really like.

"Tsk—no need for a long answer," a PF veteran spat. "IC are monsters. They roll in like a mountain and slaughter everyone they see unless you surrender!"

Walking thoughtfully onward to get his shot, Zola lay down at the medical camp to speed the vaccine's uptake—praying quietly the morning wouldn't turn into another slaughterhouse.

Chapter 39 - Titan Fall

Fifteen days later, the GAU finally concluded there was nothing left they could do about the Behemoth except destroy it outright. Behind prison bars, Admiral Blackbear screamed in fury over his arrest for defying High Command, while Admiral Adam felt a growing knot of dread in his stomach.

"I think we'd better pull our fleet away from the planet," Adam warned his commander quietly. "Otherwise, we have no idea what might happen next."

Ignoring his caution, the other admirals kept celebrating their imminent victory while the Ministry of Defense issued final orders to fire at reduced output—aiming to obliterate only part of the planet along with the Behemoth.

Inside the crippled Behemoth, Devin had already teleported aboard to rescue every surviving IC crew member. Splitting his team into three groups—upper level, mid level, and lower level—they swept room by room so nobody's waiting family would lose them. Racing past a massive hull breach blown open by an Artificial Celestial, Devin felt intense heat radiating from the burning emergency reactor while every remaining drop of power funneled straight into the Dark Matter Core—which had been charging steadily for over a week, pulsing strongly enough for Devin to feel through the deck plates. Checking his tablet as his exposed skin reddened and blistered beneath Serum Phaser-XI pain suppression, Devin saw evacuations moving cleanly across all four hangars.

Bursting into the Control Room, Devin found General Hazim slumped exhausted over the consoles.

"Hazim! We're getting all of you out!" Devin shouted, injecting him immediately with Nanobot Repair Tissue. "SumireV tasked you with initiating self-destruct—the blast is going to be huge, probably big as the entire solar system! We have to move!"

Hazim snapped upright. "Okay, EVERYONE LINE UP! Step straight through Devin's portal back to IC New Pantera!" Setting the detonation clock for five minutes, Hazim checked the tablet—all four hangars showed completely empty. With two minutes left on the clock, only he and Devin remained, stepping safely through the portal just as the hatch hissed shut.

In orbit, the GAU fired its Planetary-Destroyer Orbital Laser at lowered yield to spare two-thirds of the world. The beam struck cleanly, vaporizing the Behemoth and carving off a third of the planet's crust while GAU monitors cheered confirmation of zero life signs left behind.

Thousands of kilometers clear of the blast zone, Admiral Adam froze as an ominous purple-black surge erupted from the ruined crater—ripping outward across GAU territory and obliterating more than 6% of their 560 septillion population in an instant. Even IC New Pantera felt the energy spike rattle space, while light-years away, Aetheris halted briefly to stare at the distant pinpoint flash.

Inside IC Stronghold, SumireV nodded calmly at her monitors. "Mission complete. The GAU's economy and resources are heavily damaged." Waiting down at Hangars A and B—where 20,000 IC personnel had evacuated safely against 2,300 lost—SumireV rushed forward alongside every available Medical Unit to tend the survivors.

Meanwhile, along the Bureau Coalition border with Cluster Creed, an Arcadian vanguard smashed straight through BC orbital defenses under relentless tracker- and Destructor-laser fire. Returning meteorite barrages inflicted only light scratches as Sergeant Mustafa sounded the alarm to HQ, watching helplessly outside as Arcadian raiders carved into BC defenders in brutal close combat.

Blasting enemies back with a sudden Wind gust, Mustafa gasped as four Solar-Metal Rods slammed into his body—siphoning his energy dry within seconds. Dropping to his knees beneath a cult-masked Arcadian raising a heavy battleaxe over his neck, Mustafa braced for death—until thunder split the air, cleaving the executioner cleanly in half as Voltrauma lightning ripped through the entire Arcadian line, leaving every attacker twitching in spasms!

Stepping calmly through the smoke, Saifuru grinned. "Hello! I'm Saifuru of IC—we saw this ambush coming, and lucky for you I'm on patrol here. Go regroup; my unit and I will wipe out this Arcadian Battalion." Whirling his spear charged with planet-splitting explosive lightning, he shouted: "Zenith Triarch welcomes you with all its might! Plus we can go full power since SumireV isn't watching us!"

Watching Saifuru slaughter elites with practiced Archon-killing fury, Mustafa stared in breathless disbelief before rallying his men: "So THIS is IC? Thank God we're on their side! WE WILL FIGHT TOGETHER!"

Chapter 40 - Iron Curtain

Once she finished helping Devin settle the Behemoth survivors, SumireV returned to her office to review updated war statistics across Universe-26.

Swiping through the reports, she noted that the war between Pivot Federation, The Empire, and Radiant Guard remained paused due to the Arcadian crisis; Imperial Phalanx stood as the only faction withstanding Arcadians smoothly with minimal Cosmic units, while Cluster Creed had already lost nearly half its territory. Next, she read Orion's update: Orion had successfully expelled the final GAU resistance pockets thanks to IC support—especially Iflaren, Ampasy, Astra, and Codling—at a combined cost of 87 million casualties.
