Activating Levels 2 and 3 of the giant crimson Cross to strip Celestial immortality and energy alike, she delivered the execution:

"Now your death comes slow—you'll watch the universe die before you do! Creation Base: 9th Principal Ingenuity, Level 9—Truth Ringularity Redundancy, UNLEASH!"

A colossal, horizonless singularity expanded relentlessly outward at the speed of light across the multiverse, paralyzing THE's empire in helpless terror. Conjuring an emergency UMT paired with Clock-Lock to slow localized time, SumireV streaked away on 12% Core Reserved Energy, 0% Natural Energy, two Mini Nuclear Fission Reactors, and one final battery cell—barely outrunning the roaring edge of the Ringularity into Universe-4405, throwing up an invisible concealment dome just before passing out as the Ringularity finished swallowing THE's empire and stopped.

Back inside IC's Golden Archive, shocked elites watched in stunned silence—Klein and Nagasawa fainting cold—while inside the Realm of Absolute, Ethan cheered ("That's my SumireV!") beside beaming Julian and Ernst's proud echo. Far across deep space, Deities stiffened abruptly—recognizing the unmistakable 9th-Level Gravity signature of a Principal Ingenuity reawakened since the Great Multiverse War.

Chapter 62 - Grain of Sand And Beach

Five days later, SumireV woke up with critical energy warnings blinking across her vision and severe damage tearing through her body. Immediately slipping inside her Dimension of Logic, she climbed into the Restoration Casket to recover.

"I'm stranded out here in the multiverse," SumireV murmured, staring down at her palm as her wounds knitted back together. "There are plenty of enemies—some stronger than me, some weaker—yet the Core of Absolute was forged to slay Demi-Gods, making Celestials and anything below them little more than minor inconveniences. To kill efficiently: first, strip their footing by nullifying their energy; second, cripple their mobility and overload their sight, hearing, emotions, and focus; third, break them completely before executing them with Singularity Rebuild. Collateral damage? Invalid. Morality? Invalid. Efficiency? Absolute—because I'm outside my own universe, and nobody from IC or Universe-26 can see me. Even so, I know Deities and stronger Cosmic Entities sense my presence—and the moment I step into another universe, its defenders will feel my energy too. Anyone who stands in my way will be dealt with accordingly."

Fully restored inside the Restoration Casket, she stepped out of her Dimension of Logic and returned to reality—spotting an unnatural storm raging across distant space. Looking closer, she recognized it immediately: Corrupted Energy left behind millions of millennia ago by Oslo.

Boarding her fully recharged UMT after five days of recovery, SumireV pulled up her map and calculated she would have to cross roughly seventy universes scarred by Oslo's attack—some 80% corrupted, others only 14%. "Guess this is it. I'll have to navigate straight past them."

Compressing spacetime through Ethan and Eric's design, she tore through void after void faster than light. Whenever she passed through dead universes stripped of Natural Energy, she toggled glide mode and fed Mini Nuclear Fission Reactor power straight into the drive—until dropping out in Universe-3829, where she found the corruption had spread deep.

Stepping off her UMT while it recharged, SumireV stared out across the storm—watching ghostly echoes of ancient battles replaying endlessly inside the Corrupted Energy. "You made an even bigger mess than I thought, Oslo," she whispered. "You're strong and dangerous, yet the fallout you left behind is massive. Still, I suppose it was justified for humanity's sake—as long as you stopped those invaders, I can accept it, troublesome as this is."

Advancing forward, she came upon a massive Corruption Energy Barrier guarded by a towering ancient mecha and fleets of Scavengers looting the ruined universe. Spotting her instantly, the Scavengers mistook her for a Celestial scout and opened fire. SumireV raised a Mirror to bounce their opening salvo cleanly back, wiping out most of their vanguard.

"I'm sorry, though you're standing in my way," SumireV called out evenly. "Now witness my might! Creation Ability: Unleash Torrent of Fire and Iron from Humanity's Industrial Might!"

Dozens of World War I and World War II Dreadnought battleships and B-2 bomber tanks materialized across space, unleashing non-stop barrages—instantly replacing spent main batteries with freshly manifested cannons rather than pausing to reload. Unable to withstand the relentless deluge, the Scavengers' energy shields shattered, shredding their fleets to scrap.

Suddenly, the colossal ancient mecha roared to life. SumireV manifested a Radical Collapse capable of destroying an entire solar system and fired—only for the towering machine to absorb the blast outright, growing even stronger despite its slow, corruption-rotted frame! Smirking, SumireV cast a Zeroth-Level Quasi Black Hole—yet the mecha proved completely immune. Her eyes flashed bright blue as she switched to Dimensional De-Construction at the atomic level; it chipped away at the giant, yet its sheer size made erosion slow, and before long the mecha swung massive laser-firing blades with shocking speed!

"Wait—this mecha was built to fight Higher Authorities!" SumireV realized, recognizing the beam signature as identical to Aetheris's and Astra's Oblivion-era war tech. Invoking "Core of Manifestation: Barrage of Destructor!", she watched her blasts pass harmlessly through its plating before a colossal sword slash hammered her violently across space, shattering half her armor!

Thankfully, the blow was purely physical. Grinning through blood, SumireV muttered, "Time to end this—I won't waste any more time on you. Gravity won't work... so what happens if I overload whatever you try to absorb?"

Extending her palm, she burned two Mini Nuclear Fission Reactors and half of her Core of Absolute Reserved Energy:

"Core of Absolute, Creation Base: Recreate 6th Principal Ingenuity—The Inferno Unleash!"

A gigantic Battle Axe erupted into her grip, its blade three meters wide and five and a half meters long—radiating heat so ferocious that nearby planets scorched instantly and stars began behaving erratically while the mecha raised an arm to shield its face!

"This is intense... too overwhelming—it's burning my own skin! Nggahhh!!" SumireV gritted her teeth against the scorching recoil. "I have to do this—my people are WAITING FOR ME! 6th Principal Ingenuity, Inferno Unleash—Level 2: Abundant Majestic Flame Destroyer!!"

She swung the colossal axe. Reality fractured visibly along the arc—turning the dark void behind the mecha blinding stark white as reality itself appeared to melt away before detonating across 66 galaxies! Absorbing the apocalyptic inferno backfired violently inside the mecha, melting its core from the inside out while SumireV raised an Infinity Barrier that barely shielded her from the searing backlash.

Seconds later, silence returned—80% of local corruption wiped clean away. Checking her gauges, she found her Core sitting at 31% Reserved Energy and 76% integrity—knowing that hitting 0% would shatter the Core or leave it unusable for years. Slipping inside her Dimension of Logic to repair her Core and plan her next jump—

RIP!

A colossal mechanical hand tore straight through the wall of her Dimension of Logic! Thousands of Cosmic Entities—Heralds, Archons, Sentinels, Entropies, Nexuses, and Celestials wielding technology, cosmic power, and magic—flooded inside!

"We detected your energy, Core of Absolute!" a Celestial boomed. "We will end you before you become a threat!"

SumireV met their gaze with an unreadable expression. "I may not be the strongest, yet in the name of humanity and as bearer of the Core of Absolute, I will bring my absolute best to drag every last one of you down!"

Channeling her reserves, she locked the entire Dimension shut as her battle suit shrieked critical overload alarms, her eyes burning deep crimson-purple. Summoning the 7th Solemnity: Fallacy of Gods as a colossal crimson Cross at the center of the Dimension and raising the complete 9th Solemnity: Singularity Engine in the form of a protective umbrella, she slammed her power outward:

"Core of Absolute, 4th Level: Overhaul of Quantum! I'm going home no matter what it takes—let me show you all the Arts of Killing!"

Dragging external stragglers straight inside the sealed realm before snapping the exit shut, she dissolved incoming reality fractures outright, crushing the Celestials' and Nexuses' deep-dimensional shields under stifling energy suppression.

"I can't give up yet," SumireV whispered, coughing up blood. "As long as I live, my people won't fall—they're waiting for me. I am the Protector and Ingenuity of the human race. None of you understand what stands behind this power... dying is optional."

Abruptly, time seemed to slow to a crawl around her—and the crushing weight of every scar she had carried over 1,200 years flooded straight through her mind.

"In the end... I'm always alone," she whispered into the howling void. "That's what I am—SumireV. It's a shame everyone I loved and my family are dead, except my older sister who's lost inside her own mind. If only they could see me now—maybe they'd be proud of what their youngest child became: the Protector. Still, in the end I'm alone—even friends and companions fade away eventually. Only my shadow, my shallow heartbeat, and endless thoughts louder than any explosion remain. Sometimes I just wish someone understood me, or found me—or at least heard my story.

"I remember being five years old, knowing my parents didn't want me. I remember everything straight up to today. My childhood was ruined—if the world ran on eighty percent fortune and twenty percent misfortune, I lived inside that twenty percent. I remember the rivalry between men and women—men claiming women always gaslight and guilt-trip—while I tried so hard to be kind, open-minded, and prove not everyone was the same, only to end up humiliated by both sides. I remember trying to win a boy's affection while he wouldn't even acknowledge I existed, treating me like passing wind—I remember every disgusted face they made. Never once in a relationship—182 romantic rejections, and another 1,052 rejections from jobs and invitations. Everything failed. Even Chris and I weren't real lovers—I never had time to confess because every single chance got buried under war, research, or another crisis until he died right in front of me, his eyes full of unspoken love moments before his skull was crushed.

"I remember getting bullied in school because they hated my logical reasoning. I remember being humiliated in high school, turned into a joke and a meme. I remember my best friend stealing my crush—and stealing my boyfriend too; I actually believed he broke up with me to focus on studying, only to find out she was sleeping with him on my own bed! No wonder he stayed so calm every time we argued. Coming home hoping for peace meant listening to my parents scream and accuse each other daily until they both moved into a new house and left me behind to survive alone—while everyone in my family brushed me off, calling me mentally weak and telling me to tough it up. Ordinary friends only wanted my body or money, and I refused to sell my dignity. People blackmailed me for knowing their dirty secrets and targeted me whenever I stood against injustice. Always put second—artists gladly drawing my friends for free while refusing to draw me even when I offered to pay. Hurt after hurt, still hoping tomorrow might feel lighter. Getting blamed even when I avoided everyone; watching classmates wrap arms around each other and leave me behind; handing out birthday invitations at age nine while they stared in disgust and crumpled my cards right in front of my face saying maybe they'd come.

"I remember breaking down mentally in counseling just begging to be heard—no advice needed, just heard—only to hear straight to my face: 'You're nothing but a pretender faking it all; nobody gives a damn.' I remember other kids bringing whatever they wanted to school at age eight while the single toy I brought got reported and confiscated. Eight years of friendship repaid by stolen crushes, stolen money, gaslighting, and guilt-tripping. Tying my shoelaces while friends walked off without waiting—yet waiting gladly when anyone else stopped. People literally walking away the instant they saw me without me even speaking. Witnessing things vanish the moment I tried proving them, getting branded a liar and attention-seeker with no way to defend my ruined name—ninety-nine percent odds still landing me inside that one percent curse.

"And then my own uncle—trying to assault me after blowing his money gambling, then trying to sell me off to thugs to cover his debts. Escaping him forever before the A0D4 Civil War finally killed him as he deserved—leaving me lonely all over again. Depression, paranoia, emptiness taking over—locking myself in my room for weeks beside trash bins stuffed with blood-soaked tissues just cutting to check if I could still feel pain. That night I swallowed an overdose—finally feeling peace inside my chest before waking up alive the next morning, wondering why I survived just to wander centuries trying to rebuild who I was while nobody cared whether I lived or died.

"That's when I held onto 'Nothing lasts forever'—deciding to build my own path so the world might become better for everyone else. When war came and slaughtered my parents and siblings, not a single tear came out—all my tears had burned dry by age six. Only Estella survived on another planet as a VIP Architect—and I don't blame her for turning into a cold-blooded killer either. I lost everyone except myself. I'm only human after all—flawed, no prophet, no messiah—just an ordinary woman using logic as my compass and heart as my strength. Still... I have nothing left to lose. If pain, destruction, and suffering are the only language this universe understands... then let me show you true destruction!"

SumireV's tears vaporized in crushing gravitational heat.

"9th Solemnity, Level 8: Dark Energy Materialized!"

The Dimension of Logic shrieked, fractured, and bled integrity—flashing sweet and bitter memories one final time before imploding completely! Tearing herself free at the final split-second missing half her body, SumireV sank into a deep coma—dreaming quietly inside an endless white expanse surrounded by everyone she had lost, alive at peace.

Chapter 63 - The Empire Engagement

Somewhere inside Imperial Phalanx territory, Corporal Zola stared quietly at a worn photograph of his wife before tucking it away as his squad prepared to assault an Arcadian stronghold alongside Allied units in thirty minutes.

"Hey, Zol," his close friend Xipil murmured beside him, glancing out across a cratered valley carpeted with corpses. "Think we're actually going to win this war? We've barely pushed deep into enemy lines—Cluster Creed is 70% captured, and IC's alliance is the only one that actually threw the Arcadians off their land. Meanwhile we're holding on by our teeth getting fed Pivot Federation attrition manpower—just look out there."

Before Zola could answer, their captain walked up briskly. "Alright, boys, gather up. Your unit's tasked with clearing the enemy artillery bunkers. Since Zola is a Flame Archon, Imperial Phalanx will back you with artillery—just pop a flare or paint them with a laser. Pivot Federation is dumping millions of troops ahead of us; they don't care how many die as long as Arcadians burn. Clear those artillery bunkers and storage depots to open a corridor for our battle tanks. Clear?"

"Crystal clear, sir!" Zola and his squad barked in unison.

Gripping his flare gun, Zola looked across the valley toward a Pivot Federation sergeant waiting for the signal, then glanced back at his squadmates' grim, resigned faces. Firing the flare skyward, he led their war cry straight over the ridge.

Imperial Phalanx artillery thundered overhead onto visible enemy bunkers while millions of Pivot Federation infantry swarmed forward like ants. Arcadian towers unleashed blinding suppressive fire—Zola instantly raising his Equilibrium Flame to vaporize two incoming shells before spotting an Arcadian Cavalry charge erupting in mid-air under outdated Pivot Federation space jets ("Hey—better than nothing when everyone's desperate," Xipil shouted over comms as PF pilots coordinated strikes).

Weaving past machine guns and shell bursts until reaching the barrier wall, Zola fired a second flare. "GET DOWN! ARTILLERY INBOUND!" Melting a deep foxhole into the dirt for Xipil and himself, he emerged seconds later as bunkers collapsed—bellowing, "LET'S GO!! PUSH, PUSH!!"

Engulfing enemy trenches in roaring 40,000-degree Equilibrium Flame that left defenders deaf and dazed, Zola pushed forward beside Xipil's swift machine-handgun, flashbangs, and Ajay's turbo-charged lightning bursts—shielding Ajay from sniper artillery until turning to check the rear... and freezing cold.

Young Luka lay motionless in a muddy trench, his intestines spilled across the dirt.

Before Zola could even cry out, an unseen claw whistled past his face—a cosmic-resistant Arcadian Beast lurking behind an invisibility cloak! Igniting his whole body in blazing fire, Zola charged straight in alongside "Flaming Pour" to melt the surrounding fortifications, only for the furious Beast to vanish again and slash Zola cleanly across the back, spraying blood.

"Need backup—a Beast just wiped Team C!" Zola yelled over comms.

Darting in at sub-sonic speed, Xipil hurled a flashbang blinding the monster and hoisted Zola onto his shoulder—only for the relentless Beast to catch up and sever Xipil's arm in a single swipe! Stepping firmly between the snarling predator and his bleeding comrade, Zola seized the monster straight back toward Arcadian lines, melted one claw off, and jammed a lit flare deep inside its regenerating jaws—holding it pinned inside a blazing Equilibrium Flame Shield until Imperial Phalanx and Pivot Federation shells locked onto the flare and vaporized the Beast into ash.

Moments later, Ajay swooped down in an extraction hovercraft, hauling Zola, Xipil, and every surviving Empire trooper clear as heavy Empire Armor Battalions rolled forward into the breach behind them.

Chapter 64 - Old Enemy

Back in Universe-26, deep inside Government Authority of the Universe (GAU) space, a reclusive scientist worked quietly inside his black-hole and gravity laboratory—until heavy boots kicked the door open and twenty Military Police stormed inside.

"Ghoul Brown! You're recalled to Military Headquarters immediately!" the captain barked.

Brown shook his head firmly. "Look, I retired. I don't have to join any more operations—I already cut a deal with the Ministry of Defense!"

"This is urgent—you're needed!"

Rising slowly to his feet, Brown prepared to invoke Geometric Spatial Compression—capable of manifesting bottle-sized cylinders that sucked everything within five meters violently inward to crush it into pulp—when the captain snapped out one sentence:

"SumireV is gone, and IC is wide open to attack!"

Brown froze mid-motion. Lowering his hand, he spoke calmly: "Take me to HQ right away."

Riding swiftly toward Headquarters on another world, bitter memories surged back through Brown's mind. He had once belonged to Intellectual Chronicle (Old IC)—until petty, festering envy drove him to become one of the chief traitors poisoning morale, slipping spies inside, and turning weak-willed comrades against SumireV, quietly vanishing one month before the GAU launched its massive assault.

"SumireV... you were always so brilliant and strong," Brown muttered quietly to himself. "So strong and flawless I waited endlessly just to watch you fall. Even after I planted spies and turned your own people against you, you barely flinched. When I heard the GAU hit Intellectual Chronicle, I expected you dead—only to learn everyone inside Old IC and the GAU fleet got swallowed by a massive black hole instead. I never imagined you'd go that far... and worse, you still didn't fall. Fighting you head-on is suicide, so I prayed someone stronger would kill you—until today."

Arriving at a frantic GAU Headquarters reeling from IC's Behemoth fallout and Arcadian breakthroughs in Parliament Abstract, Brown hurried past his old lab—unmoved since his retirement—straight into the War Room.

"Tell me straight," Brown demanded of the Marshall. "Is it true SumireV is gone and IC is vulnerable?"

The Marshall nodded toward Admiral Adam. "Technically, yes," Adam confirmed. "We lost SumireV's signature entirely inside Zenith Triarch, and intelligence spotted IC's main fleets moving deep inside Cluster Creed right before SumireV's signature spiked and vanished—leaving IC units panicking. It's our best shot to invade IC, though our scouts watching Orion-TW3 disappeared, leaving other allied movements uncertain."

"We're sending you to breach IC's defenses and steal their blueprints," the Marshall ordered coldly.

"I'm retired—just a lab scientist now," Brown protested.

"Do you seriously believe you choose what you are?" the Marshall replied flatly. "This is the GAU—we control everything, and your firsthand experience inside Intellectual Chronicle is vital for landing on IC. Name whatever support you need."

Brown paused—remembering the burning jealousy that still gnawed at him over SumireV's brilliance. Knowing he couldn't defeat her directly, he could at least tear down everything she had built. "Seems I still have unfinished business with IC. Fine—I'll deploy under two conditions: massive Marrow Energy reserves and alloys to upgrade my battle suit, and promotion to Head of the Energy Research Department."

"Confirmed—we'll begin arrangements next week," the Marshall smiled.

"Wait—there's a problem!" Admiral Adam cut in sharply. "Cluster Creed is caught in an Arcadian war and shut down all intergalactic Marrow Energy exports! If we need Marrow Energy, we have to invade Cluster Creed and reopen that trade route ourselves—risking clashing with Arcadians or IC along the way!"

The Marshall's smile soured immediately—yet Brown simply nodded. "Then send us into Cluster Creed first. I need that Marrow Energy for my prototype tech." After hours of heated debate, GAU High Command locked in the plan: invade Cluster Creed first, seize the Marrow Energy, and march straight onto Interstellar Chronicle.

Chapter 65 - Extermination of The Hunt

At the heart of Zenith Triarch, Codling fidgeted nervously with his fingers while hosting an emergency summit—waiting until Tectonic Pact's envoy stepped inside minutes later.

"I'll skip formalities since we're in the middle of a war," Codling said briskly, standing up. "Bureau Coalition is still under relentless attack by The Hunt, so I want us to wipe The Hunt out permanently. I'll send specialized IC Elites to end them."

"Codling, we can't!" Tectonic Pact's representative protested, rising immediately. "We're still rebuilding from the Arcadian attacks, and our Tectonic Armada is pinned at the Order of North border clearing Arcadian chokepoints!"

"Same on our end," Orion-TW3's envoy added. "We can provide fast transport, though most of our army is guarding Zenith Triarch's western borders to stop Arcadians leaking out of Attrition Titan—especially after pouring trillions of troops into the Tectonic Pact siege."

Agitation flashed across Codling's face before he nodded. "Fair enough—not much else we can do. Interstellar Chronicle will handle this alongside the Bureau Coalition alone."

Thirty-two hours later, an IC vessel dropped cleanly into Bureau Coalition space carrying Iflaren and Nagasawa straight into a briefing with BC generals and Orion flight officers.

"Keep it brief—I want this wrapped up fast so I can get back to IC and sleep," Iflaren said flatly as BC generals began stammering through cluttered maps ("Sector A has 50 Splitter Cannons, Sector B has 200 craft and dozens of elementals..."). "Man, fix your comms—just ping me their biggest population hubs! Orion, prep your Transversal Speed Jet—Nagasawa, mark the main cities for me from orbit."

Boarding an Orion Transversal Speed Jet that slipped cleanly past The Hunt's orbital railguns in a blink, Iflaren peered down through exploding city flak. "I'm ready—stay in the jet and track targets from above while I clean the ground. This is going to be fun."

Popping open a metal case holding eight vials of Velocirax-Nyxvirus Devastator Version Zero (VNDVZ)—experimental strain deadly to ordinary hosts and exponentially deadlier inside Patient Zero himself—Iflaren jammed one syringe straight into his neck (leaving seven intact) and leaped out into freefall.

Slamming onto the capital streets like a falling meteor, he instantly triggered crimson-bruise fumes swirling with infectious droplets amidst charging Skeletal Robots and City Defenders:

"Charged up! Velocirax-Nyxvirus, Zeroth Level: Vile Venom—UNLEASH!"

Screams of agonizing mutation ripped down every street before a colossal Earthquake user dropped from above and smashed a heavy warhammer straight into the ground—blowing the Red Mist clear with a thunderous shockwave!

"Tsk... pests," Iflaren hissed. "Velocirax-Nyxvirus 70/30—RELEASE!" Unleashing a hyper-dense surge straight through the defender's shockwave, he condemned the Earthquake warrior to a slow, rotting death before soaring upward trailing crimson mist—deliberately letting pursuing Skeletal Robots clamp down onto his arms and legs.

Hovering gleefully above them as the Earthquake defender swung one final hammer blow—Iflaren flipped them the middle finger, his eyes blazing blood-red:

"Gotcha! Every single one of you dies! Velocirax-Nyxvirus: Critical Overload Biomass!"

Viral tendrils exploded outward across the metropolis, consuming entire districts in seconds as Iflaren rocketed cleanly back aboard Nagasawa's jet.

"Hey! I kept feeding you priority coordinates and you swallowed the ENTIRE city!" Nagasawa yelled over the roar of engines.

"Didn't expect it to hit that hard—guess the red mist jammed comms," Iflaren grinned. "Alright, hit every high-population world next! This prototype vial works like magic!"

"You're actually enjoying slaughtering them? God, how desensitized—thank goodness I'm still human-based, hehe," Nagasawa sighed dryly, angling toward The Hunt's major commercial planet.

"What if I jump out and airburst straight over the skyline?" Iflaren asked, jamming a fresh vial into his neck.

"Whatever—just finish Operation Extermination so I can stop looking at this nightmare."

Diving out to 150 meters above the bustling metropolis, Iflaren flared bright crimson: "Velocirax-Nyxvirus, 4th Level 75/15: Critical Overload Biomass—UNLEASH!"

Crimson rain poured from darkened skies, turning panicked citizens against one another in seconds while Orion's Transversal Speed outran every pursuing interceptor—leaving The Hunt's military headquarters drowning in dead comms from dozens of collapsing worlds just as hundreds of IC warships crossed their southern border.

Chapter 66 - The Incompatible Wielder

Back on New Pantera in Universe-26, Codling sat in his office watching IC's 16th Armada clear Hangar B—turning as Nagasawa walked in holding thick status reports after he wrapped up a call with Devin.

Sliding on his glasses to review Operation Extermination, Codling saw 25% of The Hunt already reduced to ground zero by Iflaren; Bureau Coalition converting half its economy to mobilize 42 billion troops; Tectonic Pact deploying 440,201 auxiliaries; Orion-TW3 engaged 40 million meters off The Hunt's border; and IC's frontline (Dakari, Iflaren, Ampasy, Nagasawa) paired with a blockade led by Saifuru, Crown, and Dr. Zack (field-testing new nanobot durability). Flipping further, he confirmed Attrition Titan remained locked with Arcadians (Cluster Creed slowly pushing back while Imperial Phalanx took heavy pressure), Polar Starborne held against Arcadian Lords with Parliament Abstract's new fleet, and GAU forces inside Cluster Creed were preparing to strike IC—falsely believing IC's main armada was tied down far away while unaware SumireV was missing.

Clenching his fist slowly, Codling looked up. "Nagasawa... take me to our finest laboratory."

Arriving at IC Stronghold's top lab beside Dr. Edward, Dr. Arc, and MiRi, Codling asked with an unreadable stare: "I need to see the Principal Ingenuity."

Every head snapped toward him. "You're joking, right?" MiRi blinked.

"IC doesn't hold any original Solemnities," Dr. Arc explained carefully, "only archived recordings inside the Golden Memory Archive and intelligence logs of how they operate."

"Take me there—I want to see them," Codling smirked.

Inside the Golden Archive library with Dr. Arc and Nagasawa, Codling studied every Principal Ingenuity intently before asking, "Which one is SumireV's favorite?"

"The 9th Principal Ingenuity," Dr. Arc replied warily. "What are you planning? Have you even studied gravity?"

"I know what I'm doing—don't worry," Codling smiled, stepping outside hours later to manifest an SR-71 Blackbird and rocket straight toward Simulator Planet Grade-3. Nodding to a handful of stunned control-room operators, he launched a gauntlet simulation.

Wave 1 (10 enemies): Vaporized instantly by a single manifested tank.

Wave 2 (1 battleship, 2,000 soldiers, vehicles): Blocked cleanly behind thick manifested Quoridium walls while eight USS Iowa battleships fired Velocirax-Nyxvirus Heavy Aerosol shells, wiping the field.

Wave 3 (Sand Archon): Blinding sand swept Codling skyward—only for him to manifest an entire city beneath the Archon, drop a Global Abomination Velocirax-Nyxvirus rod from orbit to weaken him, and finish him with a manifested Divine Bullet GS-1!

Wave 4 (Blood Sentinel): As the Sentinel violently yanked at every drop of blood inside his veins, Codling screamed in agony, cranked Serum Phaser-XI to Phase VI, blitzed at hypersonic speed, manifested four giant mecha arms to rip the Sentinel's limbs off, and sealed him inside a shrinking coffin lined with spinning saw blades!

Wave 5 (Threat-Level 4 Chronos — compared to Level 7 Celestial): Pouring a full World War II air, sea, and land armada against him for an entire hour while the Chronos barely scratched and healed, Codling manifested Prometheus Modulation Zeroth-Level Black Hole to trap him before firing four Orbital Annihilations point-blank!

Wave 6 (Nexus): Exhausted and pushing Serum Phaser to Phase 9 in blazing blue-purple sparks, Codling unloaded hundreds of warships, nuclear warheads, Divine Bullet GS-1s, and even manifested Messier 31—only for the Nexus to lunge straight through the gravity pull and drive a blade deep through Codling's chest!

Running purely on Serum Phaser pain suppression and burning two emergency blood packs with a shattered heart, Codling staggered upright, determined to drag the Nexus down with him. Extending one trembling hand, he roared:

"Core of Diversity Potential... Create the 9th Principal Ingenuity: SINGULARITY ENGINE!!"

Instantly, sirens shrieked across all of IC territory: "CRITICAL GRAVITY ABNORMALITY — COLLAPSE OF SECTOR IMMINENT!"

Universe-26 itself groaned and split open! Overhead, the ceiling of the simulation disintegrated—not into a mere black hole, but into raw, depleting Universal Integrity, cracking dark empty space wide open to blinding light from beyond!

Through an emergency portal stepped Devin—slashing a spatial doorway straight through Codling's outstretched arm! Severed cleanly, the catastrophic collapse snapped shut instantly before Codling collapsed unconscious while MiRi slammed emergency containment protocols and Astra cursed knowing the massive energy flare would draw Entropies and Celestials closer.

Waking days later in the medical bay, Codling got grabbed and shaken furiously by a tearful Klein: "What were you thinking?! Are you out of your mind?! You nearly got every single one of us killed!"

"If I hadn't arrived in time," Devin added grimly, "you would've collapsed the whole universe—and cracked our dimensional barrier wide open to Oslo's unfiltered void corruption!"

"Don't rush things, boy," Astra warned sharply. "Having Creation similar to SumireV's doesn't mean you can instantly manifest and survive a Principal Ingenuity!"

"Codling, listen to me," Dr. Arc said gently. "We know you're trying to protect IC, yet you don't have to bear this alone. Remember why you built the Pillar System—you have Elites and researchers standing beside you to share the weight."

"I was over in Order of North when the whole universe shook and every soldier froze," Devin muttered, while Prometheus noted he had felt the impossible gravity spike all the way from Pivot Federation before racing back to check.

Looking quietly down at his bandaged arm, Codling whispered, "I'm sorry, everyone... I truly am. I won't repeat it. I just need some rest." Lying back, he knew his weak Cosmic Suppressant had nearly doomed them all—guilt lingering heavily until sleep finally claimed him.

Chapter 67 - Government Authority Landing

Landing inside Cluster Creed alongside twenty squadmates to force open the Marrow Energy trade route, Ghoul Brown listened over dropship comms to Admiral Adam's final orders: "All GAU units—expect heavy Arcadian resistance! Secure the beachhead and establish a perimeter! Expect zero Cluster Creed support and zero IC presence. Operation Opening begins!"

Outside, orbital bombardments shook shattered cities under criss-crossing Arcadian anti-air lasers. Setting down in a sheltered sector, Brown met Lieutenant Burner inside a grim bunker littered with rotting Cluster Creed corpses.

"Where's your Captain?" Brown asked.

"Killed during initial landing," Burner answered briskly, spreading a tactical map across a crate. "Arcadians hit hard—GAU deployed four quadrillion troops, though not all will arrive since many are stranded without Marrow Energy or clashing with rival factions. That's why we need you, Brown: most GAU cosmic soldiers run on artificial Marrow tech, whereas your natural ability can punch straight through to their Barrier Generator! Our saboteurs fixed part of it before getting wiped out, and the Arcadians already repaired it—our ships can't scratch the dome, leaving us trapped without orbital support."

"So what's the plan?" Brown asked evenly.

"Regular infantry will draw main Arcadian forces as bait while your squad hits the generator under air and artillery cover. Remember, GAU's running four simultaneous fronts against Arcadians: here in Cluster Creed; Order of North via Parliament Abstract; deep west inside the Arcadian Hive where our strongest natural Cosmic units are pinning Lord classes; and supply lines skirting Zenith Triarch carefully so we don't provoke their Behemoth."

BOOM!

An Arcadian shell rocked the bunker, deafening Brown momentarily as stray Temporal Havoc beams vaporized dozens of troopers on impact—requiring four GAU fighters working together just to pin one Temporal and sever his head. Grabbing Brown's hand to pull him upright ("Wake up, sir—we have to move!"), Burner led the squad advancing silently from building to building off main streets.

Summoning Geometric Spatial Compression, Brown projected thin, invisible cylinders straight through solid concrete walls—silently crushing everything within five meters inward into clean, straight corridors that let them bypass reinforced walls without alerting patrols, pausing briefly between buildings to recharge suits alongside covering airstrikes.

Reaching the heavily fortified Dam housing the Barrier Generator—guarded by swarming Temporal elites—Brown watched 220,611 remaining GAU troops clash desperately against 455,025 Arcadians. Spotting an enemy suppression trap pinning six of his teammates right before a Temporal aimed a lethal Destruction Beam, Brown desperately raised a flat rectangular Geometric Spatial Compression barrier to shield them... only for the Havoc beam to slice cleanly straight through, erasing all six men instantly! Out on cooldown and nearly killed himself, Brown survived only as a passing GAU gunship electrocuted the Temporal shooter.

Breaching the Dam alongside Burner—armed with specialized armor-piercing shotguns—and Strong Clap (who blew locked blast doors off corridors with thunderous claps), Brown watched GAU counts fall to 190,000, swapping depleted Marrow Energy capsules from his exhausted squadmates while clearing corridor after corridor. Reaching the massive Upper Generator, Brown tossed C4 charges—only to watch the damaged core begin healing itself! Comms dead under Arcadian jammers while his rear guard fought frantically on the stairs, Brown wrapped a tall cylindrical Spatial Compression field squarely around the Upper Generator and crushed it to scrap.

Fighting back upward to the surface as counts plummeted to 85,321 GAU against 314,205 Arcadians, Brown heard comms crackle back online from the lower shaft:

"I tried blowing it... it's too tough!" Burner gasped amidst gunfire and a dying groan. "No choice—for the sake of the Government Authority, we won't die in vain!"

BOOM! A 500,000-kilogram TNT explosion shook the entire Dam, collapsing the lower structure into a roaring torrent of floodwater! Swept away clinging to failing jetpacks ("Thank goodness I didn't realize they couldn't fly," Brown muttered), Brown watched a GAU Generation-3 Sentinel flash-freeze the raging floodwater solid.

"Brown—fire the flare!" the Sentinel ordered.

Scanning upward to verify the sky barrier had vanished at last, Brown aimed skyward and fired the signal flare. Collapsing onto the ice with barely 42,901 GAU troops surviving amid 200,000 stunned Arcadians, Brown blacked out as the waiting GAU armada finally poured down from orbit—sighing bitterly to himself: "I'm nowhere near SumireV... way too tired."

Chapter 68 - The Rise of Chronic

After the GAU successfully landed in Cluster Creed to reopen the trade route, IC intelligence quickly uncovered their true objective: launching a full-scale invasion of Interstellar Chronicle and declaring war on Zenith Triarch. The moment that report reached Codling's ears, IC began preparing.

Inside the Interstellar Chronicle Meeting Room, Codling and the other IC members argued heatedly.

"Cody! Are you sure we should really go to war against the GAU?!" Ampasy protested. "We barely finished fighting the Arcadians, and The Hunt cleanup is still underway! Now we're gearing up for a GAU war? We need time, Codling!"

"Ampasy is right, sir," MiRi agreed. "We need breathing room. I know you're worried, though why not let the GAU starve their military down on the Arcadian front first? Right now the GAU is busy trying to play main character, showing off their might to their alliance and Attrition Titan."

"You're all misunderstanding me," Codling replied calmly. "I didn't say we declare war on the GAU right now—even though that was my first plan. Thanks to MiRi, I have a better solution. We run a 20-day military buildup: during the first week, we push production at maximum speed—building thousands of armored divisions and ramping up recruitment—then slow to a steady pace afterward. Trust me, Orion will supply tons of manpower. That way, if the GAU attacks early, the seven-day surge covers us automatically. And we execute everything systematically." The other IC leaders traded glances before nodding in agreement.

Nineteen days after the GAU declared war on the Arcadians, all of Zenith Triarch braced for a historic announcement. Inside Interstellar Chronicle's Ingenuity Square, 300 million citizens packed the plaza while over 142 quintillion tuned in across Zenith Triarch media. Walking slowly up to the podium, Codling waited for the roar to fade into silence, tapped the microphone, and began.

"Alright—this is Codling, the new leader of Interstellar Chronicle, speaking to everyone listening. This is going to sound harsh, yet it needs to be said. For the past 800 years since IC was founded—and the 20 years since Zenith Triarch formed—we have been condemned, blockaded, and repeatedly violated by the GAU without ever knowing peace. Every faction around us keeps attacking us despite our warnings that IC is neutral unless provoked! Look around with your own eyes: they don't care! They only want Zenith Triarch crushed because we threaten their control and expose their atrocities while they point fingers at ALL OF US!

"That is unacceptable! All of this comes from their greed, their immorality, and their depravity—wanting us suffering on our knees while preaching peace and prosperity! Well, we aren't stupid, and we aren't blind! We learned the bitter truth from what they did to Orion-TW3! NO MORE! No more bullsh*t, no more lies, no more nonsense! We've had enough of their games! Brothers and sisters, my beloved people and allies—I know, you know, and we all know what comes next! They keep accusing Zenith Triarch of wanting total war—do they want TOTAL WAR with us?! Do they want to see what we are truly capable of?!

"We've endured hardship since the day they existed—starting today, NO MORE! We end this for ourselves, our people, our friends, our families, and our soil! Our flesh may be weak, yet we refuse to kneel! No matter what they hurl at us, we will stand and bear against it—AS LONG AS WE STAY UNITED, WE SHALL NOT FALL! EVERYBODY, RAISE YOUR ARMS!"

The entire square erupted in a thunderous roar of total-war fury.

"Now or never," Codling shouted over the cheers. "If we lose, there is no tomorrow—so we fight like THERE IS NO TOMORROW! Let the GAU and anything in our way witness the true might of Zenith Triarch: minds strong as bamboo branches in a storm, hearts hard as steel, the unbreakable embodiment of the human race!" The crowd boiled over in raw, fanatical determination.

Three days after the speech, all of Zenith Triarch shifted into a total war economy. Artificial cosmic production jumped twentyfold, and conventional output surged fiftyfold. IC scaled from 50,000 spaceships straight up to 400 million warships; Orion enlisted over 50 trillion troops per day; Tectonic Pact flooded its allies with raw alloys; and Bureau Coalition mass-produced medical supplies inside IC factories—sending a mighty torrent of fire and iron rolling toward the GAU border while the GAU stayed pinned fighting Arcadians.

Six days after the speech, Zenith Triarch established a unified lab inside IC Stronghold: the Zenith Triarch Combined Researcher Project, uniting IC, Orion, Tectonic Pact, and Bureau Coalition to integrate technology, evolution, cosmic combat, and adaptation ahead of its time.

Reviewing early breakthroughs, Codling checked human-integrated Velocirax-Nyxvirus enhancements developed from Iflaren's variant—boosting strength, durability, and speed—alongside Zenith Triarch aircraft upgraded with Orion transversal tech and Tectonic Pact Quoridium armor, plus a joint tri-faction alloy combining IC nanotech, Bureau Coalition restoration chemistry, and Tectonic Pact's toughest metals in Universe-26. Watching every department race eagerly to prove itself the finest, Codling officially announced to allied leaders that Zenith Triarch was now renamed the Zenith Quadrant Citadel—reflecting all four founding powers—and set a one-month countdown for Operation Unyielding Will.

Chapter 69 - Titan Attrition vs Arcadian

Inside The Empire's capital complex, Dictator Hohenstaufen stood staring down at a launch button beside eight senior ministers—some pressing agree, others disagree.

"Give me your reasons, the three of you," Hohenstaufen said evenly to the dissenters.

"Hohenstaufen, the moment we deploy our Core users, rival factions will detect them," the first general warned.

"Are we even certain our Core users are ready?" the second general added. "We've never tested them in a real war since they surfaced in our Empire!"

"Besides, we only possess three original pure Core users," the third minister pointed out. "The remaining nine are artificial—lacking the high potential of the natural three."

"And what about those of you who voted agree?" Hohenstaufen turned smoothly.

"Because Imperial Phalanx already deployed two of their Core users," one supporter answered immediately, "and recent intel confirms Pivot Federation is deploying one Elite Core user—their veteran from thirty years on the Radiant Guard front—alongside 30 million newly trained Cosmic Division troops: mostly Archons and Heralds, some Sentinels, and roughly 20 Entropies, all artificial, hoping The Empire backs them up!"

"Not to mention," another minister spoke up, "the GAU is already inside Cluster Creed trying to pry open the Marrow Energy trade route! If the GAU hoards most of the Marrow Energy and this Arcadian war ends, they could turn straight on Zenith Triarch—and we'd be next! We have to deploy main forces to help Imperial Phalanx hold their footing first, then push into Cluster Creed."

Hohenstaufen smiled coldly. "So very reasonable. In that case, deploy two Cosmic Divisions and one Cosmic Spearhead Unit to punch through Arcadian defenses into Cluster Creed—and I'll march out to assist personally." Unclenching his palm, Hohenstaufen revealed the glowing Core of Dying Epoch.

Across the Attrition Titan alliance, every front strained near breaking:

In Imperial Phalanx, 15% of territory already lay lost as defenders awaited eastern counter-offensive reinforcements. Their two Core users—Core of Divine Light and Core of Despair—had fought nonstop for two weeks on minimal rest at maximum strain, facing another 200 trillion inbound Arcadians from the Hive while desperately needing Pivot Federation motherships to fix stalled supply logistics.

In Pivot Federation, after months of external campaigns, Arcadians finally pierced deep space inside PF's own borders—forcing special forces and elites to stall invaders while Cosmic Divisions scrambled toward The Empire, led personally on the ground by Pivot Federation's pure-human President wielding top-tier technology.

In Cluster Creed, despite 70% devastation and occupation, surviving defenders launched fierce counterattacks once the GAU landed—spearheaded by Serenity Cliff veterans, Paladin pockets, and Cluster Creed's newly awakened Core user, who cloned fallen heroes from past wars fighting at their absolute peak while flooding allied channels with distress calls before Arcadian Hives consumed their worlds whole.

Chapter 70 - Crown & Federation

Inside the Pivot Federation's Red Zone Arcadian front, 16 million Federation soldiers stood completely encircled by an onslaught of 25 million Arcadians.

Bleeding and exhausted, Commander Maxime held the perimeter while reinforcements remained trapped behind an ambush and Raul's division fought a desperate retreat elsewhere. Spotting four Arcadian Temporals closing in, Maxime thrust both hands forward—whipping up his natural Sand ability to choke and blind them before sweeping enemy ranks aside beneath a roaring Sand Tsunami. Lifting dozens of wounded men atop swirling dunes, he skimmed over ruined city blocks at 130 km/h toward the Federation's second defensive trench, where heavy artillery roared under frantic orders to release Dyfra-08 poison gas across barely 600,000 surviving defenders.

Minutes later, millions of Arcadians surged forward behind Lord and Temporal classes firing sweeping Destructor Beams. Pulling his flare gun and firing skyward for urgent backup, Maxime glanced around—only to see identical emergency flares rising across Raul's and the cosmic fronts alike. Every sector was crumbling. Running out of Dyfra-08, the Chemical Unit snatched up rifles beside standard infantry as Maxime braced them:

"We fight to the last man to buy our rear army time to assemble a full counterattack! Don't be afraid—your commander fights right beside you! It's been an honor fighting alongside every one of you!"

Suddenly, a titanic shockwave tore straight through the rear of the Arcadian horde!

Falling silent in astonishment, defenders stared through clearing dust at a lone figure standing amid the crater: Crown of Interstellar Chronicle.

"Impulsive Force: Immense Shock!" Crown struck again, blasting waves cleaner and faster than months before to vaporize and stun advancing columns while bewildered Federation troops whispered in disbelief at seeing IC rescue them. Bouncing seamlessly across shattered streets, Crown met two charging Lord Class Arcadians swinging colossal battleaxes—"Impulse Force: Lethal Pump!"—compressing force inside a tight bubble before detonating outward from a 40-meter crater and hurling both Lords hundreds of kilometers away.

Spotting 2.2 million fresh troops and tens of millions more surging over the horizon, Crown signaled PF troops to duck and cover before igniting Serum Phaser-XI Phase 8. Launching skyward like a guided missile past raining Destructor Beams, he plunged straight into the center of the enemy host—absorbing incoming blows inside a tight sphere around his crouched frame before bellowing:

"Witness my power! Impulse Force: Tachyon Obliterator—UNLEASH!! RRAGHHHH!"

Federation troops hundreds of kilometers back watched a tiny spark swell into a blinding dome before erupting outward—shattering one-third of the planet beneath a shockwave felt across neighboring worlds and wiping out 9 million Arcadians, including their logistics and elite vanguard!

Panting exhausted amid the ruins, Crown spun sharply as a surviving Dispute Class Arcadian—stronger even than an Overseer—conjured a Destructor Orb right beside him! Blinking behind the monster, Crown blasted it dozens of kilometers away and surged into Serum Phaser-XI Phase 9 paired with Nuclear Fission Reactor 2!

Opening four portals to blast both Crown and the PF trenches off the map, the Dispute snarled, "Playtime's over—"

Only for both its arms to vanish cleanly in a single blink!

Down on the ridge stood a woman with glowing light-blue eyes. "Arriving straight from the Radiant Guard front—and who might this young man be?"

"Crown of IC, here to assist," he called back.

"Very well—I'm Isabella of the Pivot Federation," she smiled, sending a roar of stunned cheers bursting across the trenches as Commander Maxime recognized their legendary veteran Core user wielding the Core of Contradictory!

"Let's crush this dog-sh*t together!" Crown grinned.

Regenerating its arms inside a defensive Destructor Bubble while firing wild beams, the Dispute suddenly froze as Isabella raised one hand: "Core of Contradictory: Unhinged Illusion!" Flooding the terrain beneath the Dispute with an overwhelming ocean of chaotic cosmic energy, she overloaded its senses until its protective bubble dropped—allowing Crown to blast the staggered creature straight into Isabella's confining anti-matter ring, stripping its armor to shreds before Crown punched it hurtling into deep space!

"Core of Contradictory: Unravel Opposition!" Isabella snapped cleanly—inverting every atom inside the Dispute until its skin and organs flipped inside-out, killing it instantly!

Floating back beside Crown, Isabella smiled warmly. "You fought brilliantly, boy—expected nothing less from IC. Never imagined teaming up with IC, though—I figured our first meeting would be a fight, haha!"

"There are bigger threats than IC right now," Crown replied.

"Don't be so serious—I've got your back. The Arcadians will panic seeing us both, though they'll probably send their deadliest hunters next. We can handle them." Turning toward the Federation trenches, she barked cleanly: "All troops fall back and escort the Cosmic Division convoy heading to Imperial Phalanx! Crown and I will wipe the rest of these Arcadians off the board!" As PF forces pulled back swiftly, Crown traded a confident nod with Isabella and rocketed deep into enemy lines.

Chapter 71 - The New Beginning

One month after Operation Unyielding Will concluded, Codling sat inside the Citadel Meeting Room inside the Bureau Coalition Occupation Zone—formerly The Hunt. Outside the glass, traces of Iflaren's residual virus lingered harmlessly in the air for vaccinated ZQC forces while Dakari waved cheerfully below, sprouting lush rainforests across barren plains. Lighting a cigarette, Codling puffed slowly until all representatives arrived eight minutes later.

"Alright, let's get straight to business," Codling began. "Operation Unyielding Will is complete. Just as expected, the GAU didn't attack while they're bogged down fighting Arcadians in Cluster Creed. Our stockpiles are full, so war production can ease back to normal—and Dakari's cleanup teams will finish restoring The Hunt. More importantly, I propose we assist Attrition Titan next."

Orion-TW3's delegate shot straight to his feet. "Codling! Why help them?! Haven't you guys been at war with them for nearly 500 years?!"

"Relax and sit down," Codling smirked. "This goes way beyond old grudges—it gives us four clear advantages. First, we field-test our Zenith Triarch Combined Researcher Project weapons. Second, giving Attrition Titan hope keeps them fighting harder to bleed the Arcadians dry. Third, our presence will expose the GAU's real motive inside Cluster Creed—looting reserved Marrow Energy desperately needed by Attrition Titan and everyone else."

"And fourth," Tectonic Pact's envoy caught on immediately, "we get to monitor GAU operations up close, gather sensitive intel—and likely provoke Attrition Titan into attacking the GAU for trespassing inside their space without permission!"

"Bullseye," Codling pointed approvingly. "Send out your expeditionary armies to reinforce Attrition Titan. Dismissed!"

Returning to IC Stronghold with Nagasawa, MiRi, and Dr. Pedro, Codling finalized strike rosters—each backed by Devin's portals and one Behemoth:

Pivot Federation: Crown, Klein, Mao, and Zero.

The Empire: Mikhail, MiRi (eager to test her Colossal Mecha against Arcadian Dragons), Astra, and Arthur.

Imperial Phalanx: Iflaren, Ampasy, Saifuru, and Dr. Zack.

Cluster Creed: Codling and reserve elites.

Reviewing over 50 prototype technologies (20+ already proven in The Hunt, 30+ queued for testing—including Standard ZQC Battlesuits, Orion-Aerial Supremacy, Healing Energy Beacons, adaptive Quoridium ship hulls, Global Abomination H-ZQC healing spores, corruption resistance serums, temporary corruption cosmic buffs, and Prometheus's reactive black-hole tendrils), Codling closed the report noting over 1 quadrillion GAU troops had mobilized without firing on ZQC yet—only tense standoffs wherever patrols crossed paths.

Heading down to IC's secret laboratory, Codling checked on Dr. Pedro's wildest experiment yet. Lying bloodied on the operating table while Bureau healing tech mended his flesh continuously, Prometheus gritted his teeth: "More! Give me more energy—merge it straight into ME!"

Opening the lab roof to draw Natural Energy while Codling threw up six Prometheus Modulations to fight off tearing gravitational anomalies, Dr. Pedro channeled a second Prometheus Modulation in pure purple-crimson energy straight through Prometheus's eyes and mouth into his bloodstream—forging twin Prometheus Modulations inside both his heart and veins. Critical gauges flashed solid red, glitching wildly before falling dead silent—and Prometheus rose alive, IC's first official Low-Grade Celestial!

Soaring straight up to IC's colossal orbital Kinetic Black Hole Ring—the massive energy heart SumireV used to maintain for IC—Prometheus raised one palm: "TON 618... come into existence!" Time dilated as all of IC trembled, igniting the ring cleanly back to full power. Heading into Simulator Room 3 with Codling, Prometheus shattered an entire planet in one strike ("Planetary Shattered!"), dragged a world into a star to detonate it, balanced Serum Phaser-XI at Phase 8.5 (feeling strain only at Phase 10), and paired "White Hole: Unleash!" with black holes to crush 100,000 simulated troops at once.

"Hey, Codling—I'm ready for deployment!" Prometheus grinned.

"Roger that, Promy—we deploy next week to relieve Attrition Titan."

Chapter 72 - Relief for The Empire

Inside The Empire's warzone, defenders waited anxiously behind heavily fortified border walls a thousand kilometers back from the Alpha Line. Minutes later, comms crackled urgently: "The Arcadians breached Alpha defense and are engaging the Beta Line!" Listening to roaring enemy fleets approach, Caesar grabbed a heavy mega-scope as fire orders rang out—unleashing wide laser beams straight from his eyes, sweeping left and right across charging hordes until thirty seconds burned his energy dry. Triggering M-GenMu Grade 2, he barked at his squadmate: "Oi, plug the cable in!" Feeding directly off a portable generator, Caesar poured giant continuous laser beams into the swarm for two solid minutes until the generator died completely.

When Empire Cosmic defenders buckled, Rear Command ordered a fallback to nuclear-blast the wall. Jetpack malfunctioning amid retreating chaos, Caesar watched all four of his squadmates get ripped to pieces behind him by snarling Arcadian Beasts—saved at the last split-second when his senior, a Magnet Archon, slammed the beasts downward and shouted, "GO! RUN, CAESAR!" Crushing the monsters inside their own metal armor, the brave Archon fell moments later to an Arcadian Temporal's Destructor Beam.

Diving through an Empire-only teleporter onto the Third Line just as Anti-Matter bombs, nuclear missiles, heavy artillery, and orbital strikes vaporized the Second Line into atoms, Caesar braced alongside surviving defenders—until suddenly, the charging Arcadian hordes floated silently mid-stride and dissolved into drifting dust!

"Relax—we're here to assist!" Arthur of Orion winked warmly as thousands of ZQC Mechatronic warships dropped overhead—12 trillion ZQC reinforcements bolstering 250 quadrillion Empire defenders (with 500 quadrillion more and Temporary Chronos units inbound under General Nabil) against 20 quintillion Arcadians.

Surging forward angrily, swarms of Temporal and Lord Class Arcadians prepared to fire—only for Astra to swallow an entire third of their forces inside a massive Reality Fracture before Mikhail plunged from orbit like a meteor, linking energy straight from Mao through Devin's portal to pop surviving elites cleanly!

Facing the final Disputed, Overseer, and Divinity Class elites, Saturn (Core of Time) slowed incoming Destructor Beams to a crawl just as MiRi descended inside a colossal mecha the size of four Earths combined! Powered by over six Fallen Cosmic Entity Cores and dodging effortlessly via Orion's Lightspeed Transversal, MiRi sliced through elites while back-mounted energy tendrils snatched invisible flankers and dismantled them cell by cell for fuel! Pinning the final Divinity Class General in place with Mikhail's telekinesis, MiRi clamped her left hand (Controlled Prometheus Modulation) and drove her right hand—a screaming chainsaw edged in Judgmental Flame—straight through him, sucking fleeing stragglers cleanly into her black-hole reactor!

Settling into the perimeter under ZQC-RDE command, Arthur noticed Caesar keeping his eyes squeezed shut relying strictly on hearing.

"If I open my eyes, they fire unstoppable lasers—it's how my parents died," Caesar admitted quietly.

"Sorry for your loss," Arthur said gently, tearing nanotech off his glove and shaping protective visors with side generator ports. "Try these—now you can see safely while storing excess beam energy inside your suit."

While Mikhail crushed Arcadian corpses to stop spore outbreaks, Astra patrolled the perimeter, and MiRi planned defensive forts and field hospitals with General Nabil (backing his 120,000 local survivors), Arthur stepped outside for a quiet smoke beside Caesar—chuckle rising at a playful text from Zero boasting about meeting Core user Isabella on the Pivot Federation front, flashing a quiet thumbs-up before resuming their watch.

Chapter 73 - Legend of the Past

Inside a cluttered Empire apartment lined with anime posters and piles of broken gear, Gunther raged violently at his computer monitor—occupying himself aggressively with his power until losing a match, standing up, and smashing his entire gaming rig to splinters beside twenty replacement setups waiting delivery. Messaging his friends he'd be offline until tomorrow, he froze at sharp knocks rattling his door.

"No one's home—go away!" Gunther yelled.

"We're Empire Enforcers! You're recalled to the battlefield!"

"Not interested! My wars ended centuries ago—it's my time to relax!"

"Your old friends are here too—and SumireV is gone!"

Throwing the door wide open wearing nothing but a waist towel, Gunther spotted his two surviving comrades standing behind the officers. "Alright, you enforcers—fu*k off and give me and my friends room to talk NOW!"

Stepping inside, Brunhild sighed at the mess. "Wow, you really let yourself go, Gunther."

"Fu*k off—it's my life, I don't care," Gunther snapped. "What's this about? Arcadians? I figured."

"Technically yes," Ted explained seriously, "plus Zenith Quadrant Citadel just showed up on our frontline to help. Our leader wants all surviving Spearhead Bringers there for two reasons: crush the Arcadians, and prove The Empire still hides real monsters behind our conventional armies. Out of eight Spearhead Bringers, five are dead—three killed by SumireV—leaving just us three. And you hold the Core of Zeroth Nexus, our faction's pride!"

"That was 600 years ago!" Gunther groaned. "I'm rusty—I haven't used that kind of power in centuries!"

Forced aboard an outbound warship moments later as Enforcers stressed reaching the frontline ahead of main reinforcements, Gunther stared grimly out the viewport as dark memories surfaced: battling SumireV alongside his friend Bennett, zeroing Natural Energy to erase her Quasi Black Hole only for Bennett's Supercharged Gamma Ray to shear off SumireV's limbs—before SumireV dragged them both inside Dimension of Logic and fused TON 618, Phoenix A, and Holmberg 15A into 3rd-Level Singularity Rebuild! Bennett had torn open a gem portal at the last second to shove weakened Gunther back to safety—dying crushed under infinite density right before Gunther's eyes.

Shaking off the memory as the Enforcer officer confirmed ZQC had also deployed to Imperial Phalanx and Pivot Federation (with Cluster Creed still unclear), Gunther looked down over ancient craters and stone statues honoring his past victories over the GAU and Pivot Federation—clinging quietly to Brunhild's encouragement and praying The Empire's armies and his Core would keep him alive.

Chapter 74 - Veni, Vidi, Vici

At Imperial Phalanx's Horizon Fortress, countless Arcadian Beasts and hordes lay slaughtered across the ruined approach. This marked the 17th Planet Fortress Line; sixteen lines had already been shattered and overrun out of 31 total built—far short of the 100 originally planned before the Arcadians struck Universe-26. Each Planet Fortress bristled with millions of laser cannons, thousands of anti-invasion railguns, and six planet-destroyer beams. Exhausted and bleeding after fighting nearly a solid month nonstop, Imperial Phalanx's two Core users—Core of Divine Light and Core of Despair—staggered as their Core reserves scraped rock bottom.

Suddenly, a streak of lightning blurred past—snatching both Core users cleanly into a hospital bay deep behind defensive lines.

"Hey, hey! It's me, Saifuru from IC!" he grinned cheerfully as they blinked in shock. "Nice to meet you, Core users of Imperial Phalanx! We came to assist—go get some rest. We'll take the heavy hits from here, everything will be safe." With a crackle of lightning, he vanished.

Out on the frontline, advancing Arcadian hordes halted uneasily as creeping Crimson Mist drifted across the plain, stinging and sapping strength wherever it touched skin. Falling back cautiously, they heard heavy machinery humming, sharp sonar pings echoing inside the red fog, and arcs of flickering lightning. As the mist parted, millions of ZQC warships hung poised across the sky—and a colossal speaker boomed down from the flagship as Dr. Zack stepped to the mic:

"So you guys come from a Deity? Have you ever seen... Nanotech?"

Without warning, Behemoths and hundreds of Dreadnoughts dropped straight out of Lightspeed Travel, unleashing thunderous barrages that wiped out billions in seconds. Hearing distant roars echoing across the horizon, bewildered Arcadian infantry braced—only to be buried beneath systematic, multi-layered bombardment: mortars, howitzers, field guns, mobile artillery, Global Abomination strikes, and Dreadnought main batteries cycling without pause. The instant the surviving wave closed within 60 kilometers, Dr. Zack smirked and loosed swarms of Nanobits—mosquito-sized nanobots flooding the battlefield like ravenous locusts, devouring Arcadian ranks alive!

Shocked at facing technology completely beyond their era, the Arcadian General poured 800 trillion more troops around the flanks to bypass ZQC straight toward Imperial Phalanx's Planet Fortress. ZQC units barely glanced at the flankers—until the charging Arcadians collided head-on with IC's Mythril Beast Fleet! Carving off limbs and killing a handful of monsters, the Arcadians watched in horror as Bureau Coalition healing tech knitted the Mythril Beasts right back together while corpses piled into hills and blood soaked the earth.

Enraged, the Arcadian General unleashed his finest assets: Elite Class Arcadians—Disputed, Temporal, and Overseer units—targeting the Behemoths with Destructor Beams that punched dozens of smoking holes across their hulls. Yet the Behemoths were simply too colossal to drop; closing range relentlessly, they opened up with close-range Anti-Air Flak firing hypersonic Titanium nets—agonizing to Elite classes while shredding regular troops like jelly hit by a badminton racket—alongside nuclear missile salvos. Watching ZQC replace losses faster than expected, Imperial Phalanx defenders rushed back to their battle stations, raining heavy beams into blind spots while portals tore open pouring wave after wave of fresh Arcadians.

Suddenly, thick Red Mist rained down across the battlefield from overhead as Iflaren unleashed his power: "Patient Zero: Vile Devouring—UNLEASH!" Mutating instantly, infected Arcadians tore wildly at their own comrades while Iflaren swept overhead drenching formations in virus.

Across the sky, purple-red lightning flickered violently as Saifuru blurred past at 940,199 km/h—surging two to three times faster in bursts—dismembering Elite Arcadians and bounding across over a hundred worlds whose crusts cracked beneath his impact shockwaves! "Witness my power—Core of Haste and Voltrauma! Kill every Arcadian—leave no survivors!"

Cornered amid the chaos, a Disputed Class Arcadian tried to deconstruct an entire ZQC flank, struggling to stabilize energy amidst ZQC maneuvers, Behemoth gravity waves, and erratic Natural Energy—until a heavy hand clamped onto its arm.

"You're strong, though... wonder what happens if I do this?" Ampasy grinned—ripping the Disputed's arm clean out of its socket and ramming it straight through its own face, killing it instantly! "Welcome to the end of the world, Arcadian! You're facing the ingenuity of the human race, and we will stand bearing everything against you, FALSE DEITY!"

Slaughtering relentlessly to beat both Iflaren and Saifuru's kill counts, Ampasy soaked up Destructor Beams across his torso and limbs—instantly regenerating thanks to nanotech and his armored frame while guarding only his skull—growing fiercer by the second. Grabbing another Disputed Class, he tore open its ribcage, ripped out its small intestine and spine, and swung the backbone like a bloody club across enemy faces with bone-grinding force!

Panic gripped the Arcadian General as casualties reached 500 quadrillion out of 4.5 quintillion. Calling desperately for Hive reinforcements, he learned the Hive itself sat blockaded by GAU Artificial Celestials and Core users locked in brutal war against Lord and Empyrean classes. Looking back out onto the frontline, he froze as three overwhelming, unstable auras erupted across the sky: Iflaren blazing deep crimson with Velocirax-Nyxvirus Destructor Variant, Ampasy flaring bright with Serum Phaser-ESD bio-electricity, and Saifuru crackling in bruised purple-red Voltrauma!

"So we run Serum Phaser-XI Phase 9?" Ampasy grinned.

"No—we run Phase 10," Saifuru shot back. "Wait—how?"

"Relax, boys," Iflaren smirked, tossing them vials. "Temporary corruption serum—lasts one hour cleanly, just inject anti-corruption serum right after."

Slamming the vials into their necks, all three surged simultaneously—Ampasy into Serum Phaser-ESD Stage 5, Saifuru into Core of Haste 4th Level, and Iflaren into Velocirax-Nyxvirus Destructor Critical Biomass 3rd—charging forward together to wipe the Arcadian forces off Imperial Phalanx soil once and for all.

Chapter 75 - Operation Liberation

Inside Cluster Creed's underground Command Center, Vihaan notified Franco that Zenith Quadrant Citadel would be landing shortly, with The Empire arriving 16 days later behind massive troop deployments.

"Franco, are you ready to launch Operation Liberation?" Vihaan asked over comms. "Command Center is locked in—the Royal Army is coming alongside pocket resistances and Paladin Forces. We hit all at once, with you and the Royal Army spearheading since you both hold trillions. Expect the Arcadians to throw everything they have here. ZQC, The Empire, and the GAU will all be present too—though the GAU arrived without notifying us and refuses all communication. It'll be a bloodbath, yet we have to purge these Arcadian Spores off our worlds!"

"My first wave—200 billion Serenity Forces—is already moving," Franco confirmed steadily. "Second wave will be me and the rest of the old-era heroes. Operation Liberation begins now!"

Turning to his command crew, Vihaan barked: "CONTACT ALL FORCES! OPERATION LIBERATION STARTS NOW!" Hands slammed down onto launch consoles across the hall.

Out on the southwestern ruins, 500 million marching Arcadians halted abruptly upon spotting a lone survivor standing in an open field, moving forward eagerly to devour him—until the figure spoke calmly:

"Come all you want... I am Revenant, wielder of the Core of Necromancer! Reanimated Fallacy of Hope!"

Instantly, an eerie ghost-green tempest erupted across the field as hundreds of ancient legends clawed upward from shattered soil—Sigil Compact heroes, the Big Seven Sentinels, and Azure Unit, all radiating unsettling emerald fire before unleashing strikes that split the planet apart!

Across Cluster Creed, Serenity Cliff forces hit landing zones beneath storms of Destructor Orbs; Vihaan and Paladin crews activated orbital satellite cannons signaling every underground rebel to rise up; Royal Guard divisions battled through ferocious encirclements far from relief, unleashing every Elite unit available; GAU forces pushed down the Marrow Energy trade route against Spores, Overseers, and city-leveling Arcadian Dragons clashing with Ghoul Brown's squad; ZQC landed in the South 12,000 kilometers from the GAU behind Prometheus and two Behemoths (despite fierce resistance from Disputed and Abomination Class Arcadians); and The Empire struggled with fuel shortages inside Imperial Phalanx, negotiating urgently with Orion-TW3 for fast Marrow Energy transport.

Two hours after launch, Vihaan sat inside his jammed bunker staring at orbital surveillance alongside his friend Martha. The map painted a ringed warfront: Serenity Cliff pressing North, GAU pushing East, ZQC driving South, Royal Guard holding West, and Vihaan's Paladins anchoring the center. Suddenly, an ominous ghost-green energy spike flared across the Southwest screen.

"Hey, Martha... do we have any Core users left?" Vihaan asked slowly.

"No—our legends and heroes topped out at Sentinel and Chronos, aside from our King," Martha replied—before spotting the screen and freezing pale. "Wait... by these readings, that IS a Core user! That entire sector is saturated in his domain—he must have been hiding there until the Arcadians walked right into him. I just hope his blast stays contained away from our mainland!"

Suddenly, comms crackled sharply as Codling punched through Arcadian jamming: "Comms are jammed—I'm redirecting your frequency temporarily using Core energy! It'll expose us briefly, yet we wanted you to know we linked up with local Cluster Creed rebels and are helping clear out Arcadians—"

"THE ABOMINATION CLASS ARCADIAN IS HEADING STRAIGHT FOR YOU GUYS!" Vihaan shouted into the mic. "GET OUT OF THERE!"

"What?! Abomination? Is that a new—"

Static killed the channel instantly, leaving Vihaan staring grimly at the dead screen before bowing his head in silent prayer for IC, triggering an orbital Solar Ray Beam to back up nearby Paladins and relay satellite photos to every allied front.

Meanwhile, fighting solo in the Southwest, Revenant raised 1 million Purge-Era superhumans—victims slain ages ago when SumireV hurled a literal star upon them—watching them hurl Arcadians aside like ragdolls until Temporal elites and Arcadian Beasts slaughtered them. Raising fallen Sigil Compact Sentinels next—including Goran, who unleashed invisible moon-eclipse radiation burns before sacrificing himself in a moon-sized explosion—Revenant staggered back as overwhelming numbers of Disputed Class elites crushed his Sentinels anyway!

Panic rising as his undead legions dwindled, Revenant cast his Core Vision deeper into ancient echoes... and locked onto an immense, abnormal signature lingering from ten million years earlier.

"What's this... Ethan, a Core of Absolute user? Damn, he might even be stronger than SumireV! Let me reanimate him!"

The instant Revenant tried pulling Ethan's echo back, his Core reserve plummeted straight to near-zero—his physical body locking rigid as horrific visions of Ethan obliterating universes and slaughtering Deities slammed through his skull! Paralyzed on his knees as Disputed elites and snapping Arcadian Beasts lunged for the kill, Revenant squeezed his eyes shut ("Maybe that was a mistake—don't die yet, WAKE UP!")—

FLASH!

Blinding radiance vaporized the lunging Disputed Class outright! Rainstorms of neon spears and arrows tore through the charging Beasts before a towering, blazing white Cross—a 20%-power clone of the 7th Principal Ingenuity—erupted overhead, nullifying incoming Destructor barrages into harmless sparks!

Snatched cleanly clear on glowing chains of light, Revenant looked up at his rescuer. "You're safe now, my son," the man said gently. "I'm just a priest from IC—my name is Joshua, an Elite Unit wielding Nameless Light."

Tossing back a tiny pebble-sized Macro Light before soaring skyward with Revenant secured, Joshua watched a 500-megaton-TNT sphere of blinding radiance swallow the pursuing Arcadians whole. "You'll be fine—I'm taking you to a nearby Tectonic Pact camp while Master Codling holds the main front."

Chapter 76 - One And Only Priest

Touching down at the Tectonic Pact camp amid scattered gunfire between defenders and straggling Arcadians, Joshua hurled four Divine Spears straight into Arcadian armor—blinding troops in intense radiance before melting their plating off without harming the soil—then blinded two Temporal Class B elites and struck them down with dual light-forged Excaliburs that burned their souls straight into ash.

Carrying Revenant into the emergency medical tent, Joshua handed him over to medics briskly: "Restore this gentleman—and stay alert, he's a Core user. I'm from IC, friendly; we just cleared millions of Arcadians to buy Tectonic Pact breathing room. Gotta go!"

Soaring skyward on wings of light, Joshua dropped into a besieged city ringing with desperate gunfire between Cluster Creed Rebels and swarming Arcadian Beasts.

"Hey! Pull back behind me—I've got your backs!" Joshua called out, stringing ten invisible Holy Strings across the avenue. Charging blindly forward, waves of monstrous Beasts sliced cleanly into pieces along the glowing wires. When dozens of Destructor Beams streaked down from rooftops, Joshua boosted his reflexes with Serum Phaser and raised the 7th Principal Ingenuity Replica strapped behind his shoulders—making every lethal beam vanish ten meters from his skin!

"Nameless Light belongs to Spirituelle Pythagoras," Joshua murmured quietly to himself. "As long as an enemy carries one of the Seven Deadly Sins, Holiness burns them. Only one way to test..."

Raising a hand, he conjured a glowing Cross—watching in fierce satisfaction as nearby Arcadian Beasts hissed and caught fire from their own Wrath! Multiplying floating crosses down the street to scorch the advancing horde, he pointed rebels down the avenue: "Head South! You'll hit the Tectonic Pact line—identify yourselves as allies, they'll let you through! Go now, I'll rescue the other Creedians!"

Before he could turn, a hurled car slammed straight into his ribs, sending him crashing across pavement!

"Curse—that actually hurt! Clever, switching to physical attacks," Joshua muttered, leaping back up as an Arcadian Halo hurled boulders and wreckage through the smoke. Conjuring a light shield to deflect incoming debris, Joshua surrounded himself with twelve autonomous flying daggers and a glowing spear before charging straight through the battalion—driving his spear earthward in a holy shockwave beneath twin colossal crosses weakening enemy holy defenses overhead! Sweeping down avenue after avenue while rebels laid down sniper support from rooftops and Joshua broadcast evacuation orders south across city speakers, he finally reached the northern outskirts.

Stepping beyond the North Gate, Joshua halted dead in his tracks.

Five million fresh Arcadians surged across the open plain like a living tidal wave—determined to swallow the city, rebels, and even their own stragglers whole.

Reaching up, Joshua gripped the simple cross hanging around his neck—the Gem of Eternal Peace—as heavy holy pressure slowed the charging wall to a crawl.

"This power was bestowed by heaven to protect the weak and innocent," Joshua spoke softly into the blazing stillness. "Eternal Peace: Gate of Eden Lights."

Endless towering Gates stretched across the northern horizon and swung wide—flooding the entire valley in pure, blinding radiance while 7th Principal Ingenuity nullified every desperate Destructor Beam aimed back by Overlords and Temporals. Within seconds, all five million Arcadians dissolved into hardened gray dust across charred earth.

"God's job is to forgive," Joshua murmured, lowering his cross quietly. "My job is to send them to God. May God forgive you all." Turning slowly around, he walked back south toward the Tectonic Pact lines.

Chapter 77 - Abomination Determination

Across the southern sector of Cluster Creed, Codling barked urgently over comms: "All nearby units regroup and assist Prometheus against the Abomination Class Arcadian ASAP!" Over 70% of ZQC forces converged on the sector, where a continent-sized monstrosity crawled forward shielded by a Disputed Class and thousands of healing Overseer elites. Returning from his rescue sweep, Joshua immediately soared straight toward the roar of battle.
